On a class of functional difference equations: explicit solutions, asymptotic behavior and applications
Nataliya Vasylyeva

Abstract
. For ν ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] \nu\in[0,1] and a complex parameter σ , \sigma, R ​ e ​ σ > 0 , Re\,\sigma>0, we discuss a linear inhomogeneous functional difference equation with variable coefficients on a complex plane z ∈ ℂ z\in{\mathbb{C}} : ( a 1 ​ σ + a 2 ​ σ ν ) ​ 𝒴 ​ ( z + β , σ ) − Ω ⁡ ( z ) ​ 𝒴 ​ ( z , σ ) = 𝔽 ⁡ ( z , σ ) , β ∈ ℝ , β ≠ 0 , (a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\mathcal{Y}(z+\beta,\sigma)-\Omega(z)\mathcal{Y}(z,\sigma)=\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma),\quad\beta\in\mathbb{R},\,\beta\neq 0, where Ω ⁡ ( z ) \Omega(z) and 𝔽 ⁡ ( z ) \mathbb{F}(z) are given complex functions, while a 1 a_{1} and a 2 a_{2} are given real non-negative numbers. Under suitable conditions on the given functions and parameters, we construct explicit solutions of the equation and describe their asymptotic behavior as | z | → + ∞ |z|\to+\infty . Some applications to the theory of functional difference equations and to the theory of boundary value problems governed by subdiffusion in nonsmooth domains are then discussed.



2000 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 39A06, 39B32; Secondary
35C15,35R11,35R35

1.
Introduction

Functional difference equations (
FDEs
) have
shown an incredible power to solve many problems arising in
mathematics, physics, biology and economics. In particular, they
give rise to many examples of applications including population
models, molecular evolution models, price and quantity levels and
indices, utility theory, production functions, Fisher equation of
exchange, theory of multi-sectoral growth, the famous problem of
the ’gambler’s ruin’, combinatorial problems encompassing the
recurrences generating many famous combinatorial numbers (e.g.
Fine, central Delannoy, Schröder, counting directed animals)
(see
[
2
,
8
,
CC
,
12
,
20
,
29
,
28
,
26
]
and also references
therein). Still another way in which difference equations appear
is as recurrence relations in connection with special functions
or as numerical approximations to differential equations and to
initial-boundary value problems (especially if these problems are
stated in the domains with singular boundaries)

[
3
,
5
,
4
,
11
,
23
,
22
,
19
,
17
,
27
,
30
]
. Finding explicit
solutions and closure forms of solutions for linear
FDEs

using various approaches, including Mallin, Laplace
transformations, series and infinite products, special functions
(Barnes double Gamma-functions, Maliuzhnets’ function,
Alexeiewsky’s
G
function), is one of the main research topics
related with asymptotic theory of analytic FDEs and symbolic
computations. Indeed, construction of solutions to difference
equations with different types of coefficients (e.g. constant,
not transcendental, trigonometric, rational) has been extensively
studied by many authors (see

[
3
,
4
,
6
,
9
,
10
,
13
,
18
,
19
,
22
,
21
,
23
,
27
,
30
,
31
]
and the
references therein).

In this paper, for fixed real numbers
\nu\in(0,1)
,
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
,
\beta
,
we focus on a linear inhomogeneous functional difference equation with a variable coefficient in the unknown complex-valued function
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{Y}(z,\sigma),

(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\mathcal{Y}(z+\beta,\sigma)-\Omega(z)\mathcal{Y}(z,\sigma)=\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma),\,z\in{\mathbb{C}},

(1.1)

where
\sigma
is a complex parameter,
Re\,\sigma>0
, and
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
is some given function whose properties will be specified later.
Coming to the coefficient involved, the function
\Omega(z)
reads as a finite or an infinite product

\Omega(z)=\mathfrak{d}_{0}\exp\{Az^{2}+Bz\}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{1}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{1}-z)\prod_{i=1}^{M_{2}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2}+z)}{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{3}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}-z)\prod_{i=1}^{M_{4}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4}+z)}\Omega_{1}(z),

(1.2)

where

\Omega_{1}(z)=1\quad\text{if}\quad\Omega(z)\,\,\text{ is a finite product,}

otherwise this function reads as

\Omega_{1}(z)=\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{5}}(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)\prod_{i=1}^{M_{6}}(\gamma_{i,n}+z)}{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{7}}(\zeta_{i,n}-z)\prod_{i=1}^{M_{8}}(\eta_{i,n}+z)}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta_{i,n}\prod_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta_{i,n}}{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}\prod_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma_{i,n}}.

Here
M_{j},

j\in\{1,2,...,8\},
are non-negative integer numbers, and
\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}
,
\gamma_{i,n},

\eta_{i,n},

\zeta_{i,n},

\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{k},

k\in\{1,2,3,

4\},
are real sequences,
\mathfrak{d}_{0}\neq 0,

A
and
B
are complex numbers.

Actually, as we will see later, equation (
1.1
) turns out to be more power tools in solving linear
FDEs
with variable coefficients containing meromorphic functions and in finding solutions of linear initial-boundary value problems governed by subdiffusion in domains with singular boundaries.

The first order homogenous equation (
1.1
) (i.e.
\beta=1
) with coefficients being independent of the parameter
\sigma
was discussed in
[
3
]
in the case of
\Omega(z)=\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}(\mathfrak{h}_{1,n}-z)(\eta_{1,n}+z)^{-1}
. Assuming convergence of the series

\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}[|\mathfrak{h}_{1,n}|^{-m_{1}}+|\eta_{1,n}|^{-m_{2}}]
with some real positive
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
, the author constructed the explicit solution in the terms of the Barns double Gamma-functions. We also refer to work
[
30
]
, in which exploiting the technique from
[
3
]
, the author obtained an explicit solution to the similar homogenous equation with
\Omega(z)=\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\frac{(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)}{(\zeta_{i,n}-z)}
, where
\mathfrak{h}_{2,n}-\mathfrak{h}_{1,n}=1
and
\zeta_{2,n}-\zeta_{1,n}=1
.

Concerning the first order inhomogeneous equation like (
1.1
), we point out the papers

[
4
,
6
,
27
]
, where several particular solutions were built in the case of a bounded analytic function
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
in
{\mathbb{C}}
for each
\sigma,

Re\sigma>0
,
a_{1}=1,a_{2}=0
and

\Omega(z)=\mathfrak{d}_{0}\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(\mathfrak{h}_{1,n}-z)(\gamma_{1,n}+z)\zeta_{1,n}\eta_{1,n}}{(\zeta_{1,n}-z)(\eta_{1,n}+z)\mathfrak{h}_{1,n}\gamma_{1,n}}

with real sequences satisfying relations:

\mathfrak{h}_{1,n},\gamma_{1,n},\eta_{1,n},\zeta_{1,n}=O(n)\quad\text{as}\quad n\to+\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{h}_{1,n}-\gamma_{1,n}\equiv C,\,\eta_{1,n}-\zeta_{1,n}\equiv C_{0},

(1.3)

with some non-negative constants
C
and
C_{0}
. We also recall that the first order equation (
1.1
) with
a_{2}=0
was analyzed in
[
5
,
7
]
, where the explicit general solutions were constructed in the case of the coefficient
\Omega(z)=\mathfrak{d}_{0}\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(\mathfrak{h}_{1,n}-z)(\gamma_{1,n}+z)\prod_{i=1}^{M_{7}}(\zeta_{i,n}\eta_{i,n})}{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{7}}(\zeta_{i,n}-z)(\eta_{1,n}+z)\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}\gamma_{1,n}}
, where
M_{7}>1
and the sequences
\mathfrak{h}_{1,n},\gamma_{1,n},\eta_{i,n},\zeta_{i,n}
satisfied (
1.3
) with positive constants
C,C_{0}
.
It is worth noting that all studies presented in
[
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
27
,
30
]
concern equation (
1.1
) with the coefficient
\Omega(z)
containing only real sequences.

In this paper, under weaker assumptions (compared to previous works) on the right-hand side of (
1.1
) and on the coefficient
\Omega_{1}(z)
(see (
1.2
)), we construct explicit solutions of equation (
1.1
) with
\beta\neq 0
as linear combination

\mathcal{Y}(z,\sigma)=\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})+\mathcal{Y}_{ih}(z,\sigma),

where
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma,{\mathbb{P}})
is an general solution of homogenous equation (
1.1
) depending on an arbitrary periodic function
{\mathbb{P}}(z/\beta)
while
\mathcal{Y}_{ih}(z,\sigma)
being a particular solution of inhomogeneous (
1.1
) is selected
in the integral form

\mathcal{Y}_{ih}(z,\sigma)=\frac{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma)}{2i}\int_{\ell_{d_{0}}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\beta\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+\beta\xi+\beta,\sigma)}d\xi

(1.4)

with the appropriate chosen kernel
\mathcal{K}(\xi)
and the contour
\ell_{d_{0}}
in the complex plane.

Concerning the function
\Omega_{1}(z)
, we just require that the sequences included in
\Omega_{1}(z)
are strictly monotonic increasing and satisfy the estimate:

\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}^{-2}_{i,n}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma^{-2}_{i,n}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta^{-2}_{i,n}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta^{-2}_{i,n}\Big]+\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}^{-1}_{i,n}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma^{-1}_{i,n}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta^{-1}_{i,n}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta^{-1}_{i,n}\Big|<+\infty.

Besides, in our work we analyze equation (
1.1
) in the case of complex sequences included in the coefficient
\Omega(z)
and accordingly in
\Omega_{1}(z)
.

As for the properties of the function
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
, we relax requirements on the growth of this function and on the region of analyticity of
\mathbb{F}
. Namely,
we consider both bounded and unbounded functions
\mathbb{F}
whose growth for large
|z|
is controlled by a specially selected analytic periodic rapidly decreasing function
\mathcal{K}
. As for the domain of analyticity for
\mathbb{F}
, we narrow this region from
{\mathbb{C}}
to a specified strip

z_{0}-1<Re(z/\beta)<z_{0}+d_{0}
with some fixed
d_{0}\in[0,1]
and
z_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.

In order to find
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
and
\mathcal{Y}_{ih}(z,\sigma)
we employ the following strategy.
First, performing the special change of variables and introducing new unknown function, we convert equation (
1.1
) with
\beta\neq 1
into the first order difference equation. Then exploiting the well-known properties of the Gamma-function, e.g.
\Gamma(z+1)=z\Gamma(z)
, and solutions of the simplest first order difference equations, we build general solutions
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma)
of homogenous equation (
1.1
) in the form of the finite product (if
\Omega_{1}=1
) or the infinite product (otherwise). After that, using the well-known asymptotic of Gamma- and Digamma-functions and technique of summation of the series from
[
3
, Section 2]
, we prove convergence of the corresponding products and obtain asymptotic behavior of
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
for
|Imz|\to+\infty
. Besides, we describe the region of analyticity of
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
. The properties of
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
and
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
allow us to construct a particular solution of inhomogeneous equation (
1.1
) in the form (
1.4
) with a suitable function
\mathcal{K}
.

Moreover, we discuss application of this technique to find explicit solutions of equation (
1.1
) in the multidimensional case. Namely, we analyze the linear
FDE
in the unknown function
\boldsymbol{Y}:=\boldsymbol{Y}(\mathbf{z},\sigma)
:

(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\boldsymbol{Y}(\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{b},\sigma)-\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{z})\boldsymbol{Y}(\mathbf{z},\sigma)=\mathbb{F}(\mathbf{z},\sigma),\quad\mathbf{z}=\{z_{1},z_{2},...,z_{k}\}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{k},\,k>1,

with

\mathbf{b}=\{\beta_{1},\beta_{2},...,\beta_{n}\},\quad\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{z})=\mathfrak{d}_{0}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\exp\{A_{j}z_{j}^{2}+B_{j}z_{j}\}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{1,j}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{1}-z_{j})\prod_{i=1}^{M_{2,j}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{2}+z_{j})}{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{3,j}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{3}-z_{j})\prod_{i=1}^{M_{4,j}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{4}+z_{j})}\Omega_{1}^{j}(z_{j}).

It is worth noting that, the one of the particular case of this equation in two-dimensional case arises in studying of a boundary value problem for the superdiffusion equation in the right angle
[
11
]
.

In connection with application of (
1.1
), taking into account the proposed approach to search explicit solutions of (
1.1
), we are able to obtain solutions of
FDEs
like (
1.1
) with the coefficient
\Omega(z):=\mathcal{S}(z)
being either a finite product of entire functions or their quotient. Appealing to properties of entire functions, we describe sufficient conditions on the function
\mathcal{S}(z)
, which allow us to factorize
\mathcal{S}(z)
as an infinite product similar to (
1.2
), and then apply aforementioned technique. Besides, we demonstrate how to use our technique in the case of
\mathcal{S}(z)
being trigonometrical functions and either their linear combinations or their quotients.

Another important applications of (
1.1
), as anticipated above, concerns with construction of solutions to transmission problem for Laplace operators with fractional dynamic boundary conditions governed by subdiffusion. It is worth noting that fractional differential equations play a key role in the description of the so-called anomalous phenomena in nature and in the theory of complex systems. In particular, these equations ensure a more faithful representation of the long-memory and nonlocal dependence of many anomalous processes. The feature of these anomalies in diffusion/transport processes is that the mean square displacement of the diffusing species
\langle(\Delta\mathbf{x})^{2}\rangle
scales as a nonlinear power law in time, i.e.
\langle(\Delta\mathbf{x})^{2}\rangle\sim t^{\nu},

\nu>0

[
25
]
. For a subdiffusive process, the value of
\nu
is such that
\nu\in(0,1)
, while for normal diffusion
\nu=1
, and for superdiffusive process, we have
\nu>1
.

Let
(r,\varphi)
be a polar coordinate system in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and
G_{1}
,
G_{2}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be plane corners. For a given
\omega_{0}\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, we set

G_{1}=\{(r,\varphi):\,r>0,\,\varphi\in(-\frac{\pi}{2},\omega_{0})\},\,G_{2}=\{(r,\varphi):\,r>0,\,\varphi\in(\omega_{0},\frac{\pi}{2})\},\,g=\{(r,\varphi):\,r\geq 0,\,\varphi=\omega_{0}\}.

For an arbitrarily given time
T>0
, we denote

G_{1,T}=G_{1}\times(0,T),\quad G_{2,T}=G_{2}\times(0,T),\quad g_{T}=g\times[0,T].

For fixed
\nu\in(0,1)
, we analyze the Laplace equations in the unknown functions
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
,
u_{1}:=u_{1}(r,\varphi,t):G_{1,T}\to\mathbb{R},

u_{2}:=u_{2}(r,\varphi,t):G_{2,T}\to\mathbb{R},

\Delta u_{1}=f_{0,1}\quad\text{in}\quad G_{1,T},\quad\Delta u_{2}=f_{0,2}\quad\text{in}\quad G_{2,T},

(1.5)

supplemented with the initial conditions

u_{1}(r,\varphi,0)=0\quad\text{in}\quad\bar{G}_{1},\quad u_{2}(r,\varphi,0)=0\quad\text{in}\quad\bar{G}_{2},

(1.6)

and subject to the transmission conditions on
g_{T}
:

r^{\mathfrak{s}_{0}}\Big[a_{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(u_{1}-u_{2})+a_{2}\mathbb{D}_{t}^{\nu}(u_{1}-u_{2})\Big]+\Big[\frac{\partial u_{1}}{\partial\mathbf{n}}-\frac{\partial u_{2}}{\partial\mathbf{n}}\Big]+a_{3}\frac{\partial}{\partial r}(u_{1}-u_{2})=f(r,t)\quad\text{on}\quad g_{T},

(1.7)

\frac{\partial u_{1}}{\partial\mathbf{n}}-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\partial u_{2}}{\partial\mathbf{n}}-\mathfrak{K}a_{4}\frac{\partial}{\partial r}[u_{1}-u_{2}]=f_{1}\quad\text{on}\quad g_{T},

(1.8)

and to the Dirichlet boundary condition (
DBC
) on
\partial G_{1}\backslash g
and
\partial G_{2}\backslash g
:

u_{1}(r,-\pi/2,t)=f_{2},\quad u_{2}(r,\pi/2,t)=f_{3},\quad r\geq 0,\quad t\in[0,T],

(1.9)

where the functions
f_{0,1}
,
f_{0,2},

f
,
f_{k},

k=1,2,3,
are prescribed,
\mathbf{n}
is the unit normal to
g
directed to the domain
G_{1}
.

Here the symbol
\mathbb{D}_{t}^{\nu}
stands for the Caputo fractional derivative of the order
\nu\in(0,1)
with respect to time
t
, defined as

\mathbb{D}_{t}^{\nu}u(\cdot,t)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(1-\nu)}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\int\limits_{0}^{t}\frac{u(\cdot,\tau)-u(\cdot,0)}{(t-\tau)^{\nu}}d\tau.

It is worth noting that in the limit cases
\nu=0
and
\nu=1
, the Caputo fractional derivatives of
u(\cdot,t)
boil down to
[u(\cdot,t)-u(\cdot,0)],
and
\frac{\partial u}{\partial t}(\cdot,t)
, respectively.

In virtue of presence of time derivatives in transmission boundary condition (
1.7
), this condition is called either dynamic boundary condition if
a_{2}=0
or fractional dynamic boundary condition if
a_{2}\neq 0
. If
\mathfrak{s}_{0}>0
the dynamic/ fractional dynamic boundary condition is called degenerate, while the case of negative
\mathfrak{s}_{0}
corresponds to the singular condition. It is worth noting that, transmission problem (
1.5
)-(
1.9
) is a key point in the study of the contact Muskat problem in the case of either normal diffusion (
a_{2}=0
) or subdiffusion (
a_{1}=0
). We recall that the Muskat problem (or two-phase Hele-Shaw problem) describes the evolution of the interface between the two immiscible incompressible fluids subjected to either the classical Darcy law or its fractional version (see for details
[
5
,
33
]
).

In this work, performing special change of variables and then Laplace and Fourier transforms, we reduce problem (
1.5
)-(
1.9
) to the functional difference equation like (
1.1
) with
\beta=\mathfrak{s}_{0}+1
. Then, incorporated the proposed technique leading to finding solution of (
1.1
), we construct the integral representation of the solution to (
1.5
)-(
1.9
) which will be play a key role in the further study of solvability and waiting time phenomena of the corresponding contact Muskat problem.

The paper is organized as follows. In the next section, we introduce notation, main assumptions on the data in (
1.1
) and state our main results related with explicit solutions of (
1.1
) in one-dimensional and multidimensional cases (Theorems
2.1
-
2.2
, Remark
2.5
). In Section
3
, we prove Theorem
2.1
concerning to explicit solutions of homogenous equation (
1.1
) (
\mathbb{F}\equiv 0
) and their asymptotic behaviors. The proof of Theorem
2.2
is given in Section
4
. Besides, in this section, we discuss finding solutions of (
1.1
) in the case of complex sequences included in
\Omega_{1}(z)
(Theorem
4.1
). Section
5
is related with searching solution of (
1.1
) with the coefficients being entire functions. The main results of this section is stated in Theorems
5.1
and
5.2
. Moreover, we demonstrate our technique with some explicit examples. In Section
6
, we discuss application (
1.1
) to solve transmission boundary problem (
1.5
)-(
1.9
). Finally, in Appendix, we prove auxilliary results (Proposition
3.1
) playing a key role in the course of the investigation of
of asymptotic behavior for homogenous solutions in Subsection
3.2
.

2.
Main Results

The first achievement of the paper is related to a solvability of homogenous equation (
1.1
) (i.e.
\mathbb{F}\equiv 0
). Throughout the work, the symbol
C
will denote a generic positive constant, depending only on the structural quantities of the model.
Besides, our important convention is if
n_{1}>n_{2}
, then by the definition

\sum_{i=n_{1}}^{n_{2}}\mathfrak{a}(i)=0\quad\text{and}\quad\prod_{i=n_{1}}^{n_{2}}\mathfrak{a}(i)=1.

Accordingly, we have that

\sum_{i=1}^{0}\mathfrak{a}(i)=0
and
\prod_{i=1}^{0}\mathfrak{a}(i)=1.

First, we state our general hypothesis on the structural terms of the equation.

H1 (Condition on the parameters):
Let
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
,
\beta
,
\nu
be real numbers, and
A
and
B
be complex, such that

a_{1}\geq 0,\quad a_{2}\geq 0,\quad a_{1}+a_{2}>0,\quad\nu\in(0,1),\quad\beta\neq 0.

Besides, we assume that
\sigma
and
\mathfrak{d}_{0}
are complex numbers satisfying inequalities:
Re\,\sigma\geq 0
and
\mathfrak{d}_{0}\neq 0
.

H2 (Condition on the infinite product
\Omega_{1}(z)
):
For each fixed
i
, infinite sequences
\mathfrak{h}_{i,n},

\gamma_{i,n},

\zeta_{i,n}
and
\eta_{i,n}
satisfy inequalities:

0<\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}<\mathfrak{h}_{i,n+1},\quad 0<\gamma_{i,n}<\gamma_{i,n+1},\quad 0<\zeta_{i,n}<\zeta_{i,n+1},\quad 0<\eta_{i,n}<\eta_{i,n+1};

\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}^{-2}_{i,n}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma^{-2}_{i,n}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta^{-2}_{i,n}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta^{-2}_{i,n}\Big]<C,\,\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}^{-1}_{i,n}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma^{-1}_{i,n}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta^{-1}_{i,n}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta^{-1}_{i,n}\Big|<C.

(2.1)

H3 (Condition on the coefficient
\Omega(z)
):
For non-negative integer numbers
M_{1}
,
M_{2},

M_{3}
and
M_{4}
, we assume that sequences
\{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{1}\}_{i=1}^{M_{1}}
,
\{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2}\}_{i=1}^{M_{2}}
,
\{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}\}_{i=1}^{M_{3}}
,
\{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4}\}_{i=1}^{M_{4}}
are real and besides,
\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{1}\neq 0
for each
i\in\{1,2,...,M_{1}\},
and
\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}\neq 0
for each
i\in\{1,2,...,M_{3}\}
.

Example 2.1
.

The following are examples of sequences satisfying condition
H2
: for
M_{8},M_{6}\geq 1,

M_{5}=M_{7}=M\geq 1,
and for
0<A_{1}<A_{2}<...<A_{M}<A_{0}
there holds

\gamma_{i,n}=n^{2}-\frac{i}{M_{6}},\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{6}\};\quad\eta_{i,n}=n^{3/2}+i,\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{8}\};

\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}=2A_{0}n-A_{i},\quad\zeta_{i,n}=2A_{0}n+A_{i},\,i\in\{1,2,...,M\}.

Example 2.2
.

It is apparent that the function
\tan z
can be presented as an infinite product
\Omega(z)
with
\mathfrak{d}_{0}=1,

M_{1}=M_{3}=M_{4}=0,

M_{2}=1,

M_{5}=M_{6}=M_{7}=M_{8}=1,

A=B=0
,
\mathfrak{d}_{1}^{2}=0
,
\mathfrak{h}_{1,n}=\gamma_{1,n}=\pi n
,
\zeta_{1,n}=\eta_{1,n}=\frac{(2n-1)\pi}{2},
i.e.

\tan z=z\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(n\pi-z)(n\pi+z)}{(\frac{(2n-1)\pi}{2}-z)(\frac{(2n-1)\pi}{2}+z)}\frac{\Big(\frac{(2n-1)\pi}{2}\Big)^{2}}{(n\pi)^{2}}

More examples of functions which can be presented in form (
1.2
) are discussed in Section
5.1
.

Denoting

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(n)

\displaystyle=\text{sgn}\beta\Big\{-\frac{\ln 2\pi}{2}[M_{6}-M_{5}+M_{7}-M_{8}]-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{\gamma_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\big[\ln\frac{\gamma_{i,n}}{|\beta|}-1\big]-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{\zeta_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\big[\ln\frac{\zeta_{i,n}}{|\beta|}-1\big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\big[\ln\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}{|\beta|}-1\big]+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{\eta_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\big[\ln\frac{\eta_{i,n}}{|\beta|}-1\big]\Big\};

\mathbb{L}_{1}(z)=\begin{cases}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\gamma_{i,n}+z}{\beta}\Big)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i,n}-z}{\beta}+1\Big)}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\eta_{i,n}+z}{\beta}\Big)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z}{\beta}+1\Big)}\Bigg(\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{\zeta_{i,n}}{\beta}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{\eta_{i,n}}{\beta}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}{\beta}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{\gamma_{i,n}}{\beta}}\Bigg)^{\frac{z}{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\mathcal{R}(n)},\quad\text{if}\quad\beta>0,\\
\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z}{|\beta|}\Big)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\eta_{i,n}+z}{|\beta|}+1\Big)}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\zeta_{i,n}-z}{|\beta|}\Big)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\gamma_{i,n}+z}{|\beta|}+1\Big)}\Bigg(\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{\zeta_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{\eta_{i,n}}{|\beta|}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{\gamma_{i,n}}{\beta}}\Bigg)^{\frac{z}{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\mathcal{R}(n)},\quad\text{if}\quad\beta<0,\\
\end{cases}

\mathbb{L}(z)=\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{2}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2}+z}{\beta}\Big)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{3}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}-z}{\beta}+1\Big)}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{4}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4}+z}{\beta}\Big)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{1}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{1}-z}{\beta}+1\Big)}\begin{cases}1,\quad\quad\quad\text{if}\quad\Omega_{1}=1,\\
\mathbb{L}_{1}(z),\quad\text{if}\quad\Omega_{1}\,\text{is an infinite product},\end{cases}

we state our first result.

Theorem 2.1
.

Let
\mathbb{F}\equiv 0
and let for
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
n\in\mathbb{N},
the inequalities hold

Re\,\Big(\frac{z+\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2}}{\beta}\Big)\neq-m,\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{2}\},\,\text{and}\,Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{d}_{j}^{3}}{\beta}\Big)\neq 1+m,\,j\in\{1,2,...,M_{3}\}.

(2.2)

In addition, in the case of
\Omega_{1}(z)
being the infinite product, we also require

\begin{cases}Re\,\Big(\frac{z+\gamma_{i,n}}{\beta}\Big)\neq-m,\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{6}\},\quad Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\zeta_{i,n}}{\beta}\Big)\neq 1+m,\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{7}\},\,\text{ if }\beta>0,\\
Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\Big)\neq m,\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{5}\},\quad Re\,\Big(\frac{z+\eta_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\Big)\neq-1-m,\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{8}\},\,\text{ if }\beta<0.\end{cases}

(2.3)

Then, under assumptions
H1-H3
, a general solution of homogenous equation (
1.1
) is given by

\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})=\exp\Big\{\frac{Az^{3}}{3\beta}+\frac{B-A\beta}{2\beta}z^{2}+\frac{A\beta-3B}{6}z\Big\}\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{0}\beta^{M_{1}+M_{2}-M_{3}-M_{4}}}{(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})}\Big)^{\frac{z}{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{z}{\beta}\Big)\mathbb{L}(z),

(2.4)

where
{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{z}{\beta}\Big)
is an arbitrary analytic periodic function,

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{z+\beta}{\beta}\Big)={\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{z}{\beta}\Big).

Moreover, the following statements hold:

(s.1)
for each fixed
z
satisfying (
2.3
), the infinite product
\mathbb{L}_{1}(z)
converges;

(s.2)
for each fixed
Re\,z
satisfying (
2.2
) and (
2.3
), and for
|Im\,z|\to+\infty
, there is the relation

\mathbb{L}(z)=[\Omega(z)\mathfrak{d}_{0}^{-1}\exp\{-Az^{2}-Bz\}]^{\frac{z}{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\{\Phi(z)\}\quad\text{where}\quad\frac{|\Phi(z)|}{|z^{2}|\ln|z|}\leq C.

(2.5)

Besides,
\Phi(z)=\exp\{C_{1}\ln z+C_{2}z+O(1)\}
if one of the following conditions hold for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
:

(i)

M_{5}=M_{7},\,M_{6}=M_{8},\,\text{and }\,\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-\zeta_{i,n}=C_{3},\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{5}\},\quad\eta_{i,n}-\gamma_{i,n}=C_{4},\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{6}\}.

(ii)
M_{7}=M_{8},\,M_{5}=M_{6},\,\text{and }\,\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-\gamma_{i,n}=C_{5},\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{5}\},\quad\eta_{i,n}-\zeta_{i,n}=C_{6},\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{7}\},

where
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are some constants, and the real quantities
C_{3}
,
C_{4}
,
C_{5}
and
C_{6}
depend only on
i
and are independent of
n
.

The following assessment is a simple consequence of assumptions (
2.2
), (
2.3
) and the explicit form of solution (
2.4
).

Corollary 2.1
.

Let for
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
the inequalities be fulfilled

Re\,\Big(\frac{z+\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4}}{\beta}\Big)\neq-m,\quad i\in\{1,2,...,M_{4}\},\quad Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{d}_{j}^{1}}{\beta}\Big)\neq 1+m,\quad j\in\{1,2,...,M_{1}\}.

If
\Omega_{1}
is an infinite product, we additionally assume that

\begin{cases}Re\,\Big(\frac{z+\eta_{i,n}}{\beta}\Big)\neq-m,\quad i\in\{1,2,...,M_{8}\},\quad Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}{\beta}\Big)\neq 1+m,\quad i\in\{1,2,...,M_{5}\},\quad\text{if }\beta>0,\\
Re\,\Big(\frac{z+\gamma_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\Big)\neq-m-1,\quad i\in\{1,2,...,M_{6}\},\quad Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\zeta_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\Big)\neq m,\quad i\in\{1,2,...,M_{7}\},\quad\text{if }\beta<0,\end{cases}

Then, under assumptions (
2.2
) and (
2.3
), the solution
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
given by (
2.4
) has no poles, and the function
\frac{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})}{{\mathbb{P}}(z/\beta)}
does not vanish.

If, in addition, for any fixed
d_{0}\in[0,1]
the relations hold:

Re\,\Big(\frac{z+\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4}}{\beta}\Big)\neq-m-1+d_{0},\quad i\in\{1,2,...,M_{4}\},\quad Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{d}_{j}^{1}}{\beta}\Big)\neq d_{0}+m,\quad j\in\{1,2,...,M_{1}\},

(2.6)

and, in the case of
\Omega_{1}
being an infinite product,

\begin{cases}Re\,\Big(\frac{z+\eta_{i,n}}{\beta}\Big)\neq-m-1+d_{0},\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{8}\},\,Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}{\beta}\Big)\neq d_{0}+m,\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{5}\},\,\text{if }\beta>0,\\
Re\,\Big(\frac{z+\gamma_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\Big)\neq-m-d_{0},\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{6}\},\,Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\zeta_{i,n}}{|\beta|}\Big)\neq 1-d_{0}+m,\,i\in\{1,2,...,M_{7}\},\,\text{if }\beta<0,\end{cases}

(2.7)

then the function
\frac{{\mathbb{P}}(1-d_{0}+z/\beta)}{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+\beta-\beta d_{0},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})}

does not have any poles.

Remark 2.1
.

Coming a behavior of the function
{\mathbb{P}}(z/\beta)
as
|Im\,\frac{z}{\beta}|\to+\infty
, the results in
[
24
, p.331-334]
tell us that
{\mathbb{P}}(z/\beta)
(if
{\mathbb{P}}\neq const.
) grows no slower than
\exp\{2\pi|Im\frac{z}{\beta}|\}
if either
Im\frac{z}{\beta}\to+\infty
or
Im\frac{z}{\beta}\to-\infty
. This fact will be a key point under construction of a general and particular solutions to inhomogeneous equation (
1.1
).

Remark 2.2
.

It is apparent that inequalities (
2.2
) and (
2.3
) describe the region of analyticity of the general solution
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
. Nevertheless, this domain can be extended (i.e. conditions (
2.2
) and (
2.3
) can be relaxed) if the function
{\mathbb{P}}(z/\beta)
fulfills a stronger requirements.
Indeed, assumptions (
2.2
) can be removed in Theorem
2.1
if we require that the function
{\mathbb{P}}
has zeros in the points

Re\,\Big(\frac{z+\mathfrak{d}^{2}_{i}}{\beta}\Big)=-m,\quad i\in\{1,...,M_{2}\},\quad Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{d}^{3}_{j}}{\beta}\Big)=1+m,\quad j\in\{1,...,M_{3}\}.

(2.8)

Clearly, an example of the function
{\mathbb{P}}(z/\beta)
satisfying assumptions of Theorem
2.1
and (
2.8
) is

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{z}{\beta}\Big)=\prod_{i=1}^{M_{2}}\exp\Big\{\frac{i\pi(z+\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2})}{\beta}\Big\}\sin\,\pi\Big(\frac{z+\mathfrak{d}^{2}_{i}}{\beta}\Big)\prod_{i=1}^{M_{3}}\exp\Big\{i\pi\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}}{\beta}+1\Big)\Big\}\sin\,\pi\Big(\frac{z+\mathfrak{d}^{3}_{i}}{\beta}+1\Big).

We are now in the position to construct a solution of inhomogeneous equation (
1.1
) (i.e.
\mathbb{F}\neq 0
). As a consequence of Theorem
2.1
, we are left with the problem of finding a particular solution to the given inhomogeneous equation.

Till the end of the paper, we assume that
d_{0}\in[0,1]
is an arbitrary fixed quantity, and we define the contour
\ell_{d_{0}}
in the complex plane
\xi\in{\mathbb{C}}
(see Figure
1
for geometric setting) having the following properties:

\bullet

\ell_{d_{0}}=\{Re\,\xi=-d_{0},\,Im\,\xi\in\mathbb{R}\}
if
d_{0}\in(0,1)
;

\bullet
if
d_{0}=1
, then
\ell_{d_{0}}=\ell_{1}
consists of three parts: the half-circle
\{|\xi+1|=d_{1},\,Re\,\xi>-1\}
with a small positive number
d_{1}
,
0<d_{1}<d_{0}/8,
and the intervals:
\{Re\,\xi=-1,\,Im\,\xi\in(-\infty,-d_{1})\}
and
\{Re\,\xi=-1,\,Im\,\xi\in(d_{1},+\infty)\}
;

\bullet
the contour
\ell_{0}
(i.e.
d_{0}=0
) is obtained from
\ell_{1}
after its shifting to the right-hand side on
Re\,\xi=1
.

Figure 1.
Typical configurations of the contour
\ell_{d_{0}}
,
d_{0}\in[0,1]
.

Next we introduce additional assumptions.

H4 (Assumption on the kernel):
Let

\mathcal{K}(\xi)=\frac{1}{2i}[\cot\pi\xi+i]\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi),\,\xi\in{\mathbb{C}},

where
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)
obeys conditions:

\bullet

\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)
is periodic with the period of
1
, i.e.
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi+1)=\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)
;

\bullet

\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)
does not have any poles in the strip
\{Re\,\xi\in[-1,0],\,Im\,\xi\in\mathbb{R}\}
;

\bullet

\mathcal{K}_{1}(0)\neq 0
, and
for each fixed
Re\,\xi\in[-1,0]
,
\underset{|Im\xi|\to+\infty}{\lim}\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)=0
.

H5 (Assumption on the right-hand side in (
1.1
)):
We assume that
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
is an analytic function for
z\in{\mathbb{C}}
and each
\sigma
satisfying
H1
,
such that for all
z
satisfying (
2.2
), (
2.3
), (
2.6
) and (
2.7
), and each
Re\,\xi\in[-1,0],

the relation holds

\underset{|Im\,\xi|\to+\infty}{\lim}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{K}(\xi)\mathbb{F}(z+\beta\xi,\sigma)}{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+\beta\xi+\beta,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}\Big|=0

with appropriately selected
functions
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{1}:={\mathbb{P}}(z/\beta)
satisfying
H4
and requirements of Theorem
2.1
, respectively.

Example 2.3
.

The following are examples of the kernel
\mathcal{K}_{1}
and the function
\mathbb{F}
satisfying assumptions
H4-H5
in the case of (
1.1
) with
\beta=1
and
\Omega(z)
given in Example
2.1
:

\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)=\sigma\sin\pi z,\quad{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(z)=e^{i\pi z}\sin\pi z,\quad\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)=\frac{\sin^{6}\pi d}{\sin^{6}\pi(\xi+d)}\quad\text{with}\quad 0<d-d_{0}<1.

In Section
5.2
we will discuss another examples of the function
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)
which correspond to various
{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(z/\beta)
and
\Omega(z)
.

Now, we are ready to state the main results related with solutions of inhomogeneous equation (
1.1
).

Theorem 2.2
.

Let assumptions of Theorem
2.1
and hypotheses (
2.6
), (
2.7
) and
H4-H5
hold. Then a general solution of (
1.1
) is a function

\mathcal{Y}(z,\sigma)=\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})+\mathcal{Y}_{ih}(z,\sigma),

(2.9)

where
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
is given by (
2.4
) and
\mathcal{Y}_{ih}
is a particular solution defined as

\mathcal{Y}_{ih}(z,\sigma)=\frac{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}{\mathcal{K}_{1}(0)}\int_{\ell_{d_{0}}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\beta\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+\beta\xi+\beta,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}d\xi.

(2.10)

It is worth noting that the assumption on analyticity of
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
in
{\mathbb{C}}
can be relaxed provided a stronger requirements on
Re\,z
.

Corollary 2.2
.

Let assumptions
H1-H5
, (
2.2
), (
2.3
), (
2.6
) and (
2.7
) hold. We assume that for each fixed
d_{0}\in[0,1]
, the function
\frac{\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)}{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+\beta,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}
is analytic in the strip

z_{0}-1<Re\,(z/\beta)<z_{0}+d_{0}

with some
z_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying (
2.6
), (
2.2
) and, additionally, (
2.3
) and (
2.7
), if
\Omega_{1}
is an infinite product. Then for each
z
from this strip, general and particular solutions of (
1.1
) are defined with (
2.9
) and (
2.10
), correspondingly. Besides,
\mathcal{Y}_{ih}(z,\sigma)
is analytic in the strip

z_{0}<Re\,(z/\beta)<z_{0}+1+d_{0}.

Remark 2.3
.

A choice of the function
{\mathbb{P}}
in
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
(see (
2.9
)) depends on the property of the desired solution and corresponding boundary problems. Indeed, if we look for an analytic solution of (
1.1
) which is bounded for
Im\,z\to\pm\infty
, then (see Theorem
2.1
and Remark
2.1
)
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
will grow as
Im\,z\to\pm\infty
unless
{\mathbb{P}}\equiv 0
. This means the desired solution of (
1.1
) is given by

\mathcal{Y}(z,\sigma)=\frac{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}{\mathcal{K}_{1}(0)}\int_{\ell_{d_{0}}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\beta\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+\beta\xi+\beta,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}d\xi.

Remark 2.4
.

It is apparent that assumptions on the function
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)
can be relaxed if the function
\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\beta\xi,\sigma)}{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+\beta\xi+\beta,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}
fulfills stronger requirements.
Indeed, if
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
is a rapidly decreasing function for
|Im\,z|\to+\infty
and satisfies the equality

\underset{|Im\,\xi|\to+\infty}{\lim}\Big|\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\beta\xi,\sigma)}{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+\beta\xi+\beta,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}\Big|=0,

then assumptions
H4-H5
fulfill automatically with
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)\equiv 1
.

Remark 2.5
.

Actually, with nonessential modifications in the proofs, the very same results hold for the equation like (
1.1
) in the multidimensional case:

(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\boldsymbol{Y}(\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{b},\sigma)-\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{z})\boldsymbol{Y}(\mathbf{z},\sigma)=\mathbb{F}(\mathbf{z},\sigma),\quad\mathbf{z}=\{z_{1},z_{2},...,z_{k}\}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{k},\,k>1.

(2.11)

Here we put

\mathbf{b}=\{\beta_{1},\beta_{2},...,\beta_{n}\},\quad\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{z})=\mathfrak{d}_{0}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\exp\{A_{j}z_{j}^{2}+B_{j}z_{j}\}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{1,j}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{1}-z_{j})\prod_{i=1}^{M_{2,j}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{2}+z_{j})}{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{3,j}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{3}-z_{j})\prod_{i=1}^{M_{4,j}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{4}+z_{j})}\Omega_{1}^{j}(z_{j}),

with

\Omega_{1}^{j}(z_{j})=1
in the case of
\Omega^{j}(z_{j})
being a finite product,
and

\Omega_{1}^{j}(z_{j})=\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{5,j}}(\mathfrak{h}^{j}_{i,n}-z_{j})\prod_{i=1}^{M_{6,j}}(\gamma^{j}_{i,n}+z_{j})}{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{7,j}}(\zeta^{j}_{i,n}-z_{j})\prod_{i=1}^{M_{8,j}}(\eta^{j}_{i,n}+z_{j})}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{7,j}}\zeta^{j}_{i,n}\prod_{i=1}^{M_{8,j}}\eta^{j}_{i,n}}{\prod_{i=1}^{M_{5,j}}\mathfrak{h}^{j}_{i,n}\prod_{i=1}^{M_{6,j}}\gamma^{j}_{i,n}}\quad\text{otherwise.
}

Here
M_{i,j},

i\in\{1,2,...,8\},

j\in\{1,2,...,k\}
are non-negative integer numbers, and sequences
\mathfrak{h}^{j}_{i,n}
,
\gamma^{j}_{i,n},

\eta^{j}_{i,n},

\zeta^{j}_{i,n},

\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{l},

l\in\{1,2,3,4\},
satisfy assumptions
H1-H3
;
A_{j}
and
B_{j}
are complex numbers. Under assumptions of Theorems
2.1
and
2.2
, equation (
2.11
) in the case of
\beta_{j}>0
,
j\in\{1,2,...,k\}
, has a solution defined by

\boldsymbol{Y}(\mathbf{z},\sigma)=\boldsymbol{Y}_{h}(\mathbf{z},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})+\frac{\boldsymbol{Y}_{h}(\mathbf{z},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}{\mathcal{K}_{1}(0)}\int_{\ell_{d_{0}}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{b}\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\boldsymbol{Y}_{h}(\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{b}\xi+\mathbf{b},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}d\xi,

with

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Y}_{h}(\mathbf{z},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{0}\beta_{1}^{M_{1,1}+M_{2,1}-M_{3,1}-M_{4,1}}}{a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu}}\Big)^{\frac{z_{1}}{\beta_{1}}-\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\mathcal{Y}_{h}^{j}(z_{j},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{h}^{j}(z_{j},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})

\displaystyle=\exp\Big\{\frac{A_{j}z_{j}^{3}}{3\beta_{j}}+\frac{B_{j}-A_{j}\beta_{j}}{2\beta_{j}}z_{j}^{2}+\frac{A_{j}\beta_{j}-3B_{j}}{6}z_{j}\Big\}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\frac{z_{j}}{\beta_{j}}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{2,j}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{2}+z_{j}}{\beta_{j}}\Big)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{3,j}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{3}-z_{j}}{\beta_{j}}+1\Big)}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{4,j}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{4}+z_{j}}{\beta_{j}}\Big)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{1,j}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i,j}^{1}-z_{j}}{\beta_{j}}+1\Big)}\begin{cases}1,\quad\quad\quad\text{if}\quad\Omega_{1}^{j}(z_{j})=1,\\
\mathbb{L}_{1}^{j}(z_{j}),\quad\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1}^{j}(z_{j})

\displaystyle=\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{6,j}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\gamma^{j}_{i,n}+z_{j}}{\beta_{j}}\Big)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{7,j}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\zeta^{j}_{i,n}-z_{j}}{\beta_{j}}+1\Big)}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{8,j}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\eta^{j}_{i,n}+z_{j}}{\beta_{j}}\Big)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{5,j}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{j}_{i,n}-z_{j}}{\beta_{j}}+1\Big)}\Bigg(\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{7,j}}\frac{\zeta^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{8,j}}\frac{\eta^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{5,j}}\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{6,j}}\frac{\gamma^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}}\Bigg)^{\frac{z_{j}}{\beta_{j}}-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\mathcal{R}_{j}(n)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{j}(n)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\ln 2\pi}{2}[M_{6,j}-M_{5,j}+M_{7,j}-M_{8,j}]-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6,j}}\frac{\gamma^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}\big[\ln\frac{\gamma^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}-1\big]-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7,j}}\frac{\zeta^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}\big[\ln\frac{\zeta^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}-1\big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5,j}}\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}\big[\ln\frac{\mathfrak{h}^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}-1\big]+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8,j}}\frac{\eta^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}\big[\ln\frac{\eta^{j}_{i,n}}{\beta_{j}}-1\big].

The similar representation to the solution of (
2.11
) holds in the case of either all or some
\beta_{j}<0,

j\in\{1,2,...,k\}
.

3.
Proof of Theorem
2.1

We first dwell on the special case where
\beta=1
, namely homogenous equation (
1.1
) is replaced by the simpler equation

(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\mathcal{Y}(z+1,\sigma)-\Omega(z)\mathcal{Y}(z,\sigma)=0.

(3.1)

In a further step, we will show how to reduce the general cas to this special one.

Here we will follow the strategy consisting in two main steps. In this first one, we construct the general solution
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
of homogenous equation (
3.1
) and prove the convergence of the infinite product
\mathbb{L}_{1}(z)
. Then, based on the asymptotic representation of the Euler-Gamma function, we obtain asymptotic (
2.5
) including the special cases (i) and (ii) in Theorem
2.1
.

3.1.
The General Solution of (
3.1
)

In order to verify that
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
given by (
2.4
) solves equation (
3.1
), we appeal to well-known properties of the Euler-Gamma function
\Gamma(z)
:

(z+\mathfrak{c})\Gamma(z+\mathfrak{c})=\Gamma(z+\mathfrak{c}+1)

for any fixed

\mathfrak{c}\in{\mathbb{C}};
and

\Gamma(z)

has a simple poles in the points

Re\,z=-m,

m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}.

In particular, these relations with straightforward calculations enable to conclude that the functions:
Y_{0}(z)=\Gamma(z+\mathfrak{c}),

Y_{1}(z)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(1+\mathfrak{c}-z)}
,
Y_{2}(z)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\mathfrak{c}+z)}
,
Y_{3}(z)=\Gamma(1+\mathfrak{c}-z)
,

Y_{4}(z)=\mathfrak{c}^{z}
,
Y_{5}(z)=\exp\{\mathfrak{c}z^{3}+\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{1}-\mathfrak{c}}{2}z^{2}+\frac{\mathfrak{c}-3\mathfrak{c}_{1}}{6}z\}
,
solve the simplest homogenous difference equations:

\displaystyle Y_{0}(z+1)

\displaystyle-(z+\mathfrak{c})Y_{0}(z)=0,\quad Y_{1}(z+1)-(\mathfrak{c}-z)Y_{1}(z)=0,\quad Y_{2}(z+1)-(z+\mathfrak{c})^{-1}Y_{2}(z)=0,

\displaystyle Y_{3}(z+1)

\displaystyle-(\mathfrak{c}-z)^{-1}Y_{3}(z)=0,\quad Y_{4}(z+1)-\mathfrak{c}Y_{4}(z)=0,\quad Y_{5}(z+1)-\exp\{\mathfrak{c}z^{2}+\mathfrak{c}_{1}z\}Y_{5}(z)=0.

Thus, the straightforward calculations tell us that (
2.4
) is a general solution of (
3.1
). Besides, under assumptions (
2.2
) and (
2.3
), this solution does not have any poles.

At this point, we verify the convergence of the infinite product
\mathbb{L}_{1}(z)
. It is apparent that its convergence is equivalent to convergence of the series

\displaystyle\ln\mathbb{L}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big[(z-1/2)\ln\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta_{i,n}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta_{i,n}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma_{i,n}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\ln\Gamma(\gamma_{i,n}+z)+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\ln\Gamma(1+\zeta_{i,n}-z)

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\ln\Gamma(1+\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\ln\Gamma(\eta_{i,n}+z)+\mathcal{R}(n)\Big]\equiv\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}R_{n}(z).

(3.2)

Here, for any fixed
z
satisfying (
2.3
), we show that the reminder of (
3.1
) is bounded. To this end, we chose
n=\mathfrak{M}
such that the inequality holds

|z|\leq\underset{n\geq\mathfrak{M}}{\min}\{\gamma_{1,n},...,\gamma_{M_{6},n},\zeta_{1,n},...,\zeta_{M_{7},n},\eta_{1,n},...,\eta_{M_{8},n},\mathfrak{h}_{1,n},...,\mathfrak{h}_{M_{5},n}\}.

After that, denoting

\psi^{(0)}(z):=\psi(z)=\frac{d}{dz}\ln\Gamma(z),\quad\psi^{(j-1)}=\frac{d^{j}}{dz^{j}}\psi(z),j\in\mathbb{N},

and using Taylor expansion for the function
\ln\Gamma(z+\mathfrak{c})
near the point
\mathfrak{c},
(
\mathfrak{c}>0
):

\ln\Gamma(z+\mathfrak{c})=\ln\Gamma(\mathfrak{c})+\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\psi^{(j-1)}(\mathfrak{c})\frac{z^{j}}{j!},

we rewrite the reminder of series (
3.1
) in more convenient form

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{M}}^{+\infty}R_{n}(z)=\sum_{n=\mathfrak{M}}^{+\infty}Q_{1}(n)+z\sum_{n=\mathfrak{M}}^{+\infty}Q_{2}(n)+\frac{z^{2}}{2}\sum_{n=\mathfrak{M}}^{+\infty}Q_{3}(n)+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{M}}^{+\infty}Q_{4}(z,n).

(3.3)

Here we set

\displaystyle Q_{1}(n)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\ln\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta_{i,n}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta_{i,n}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma_{i,n}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\ln\Gamma(\gamma_{i,n})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\ln\Gamma(1+\zeta_{i,n})-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\ln\Gamma(1+\mathfrak{h}_{i,n})

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\ln\Gamma(\eta_{i,n})+\mathcal{R}(n),

Q_{2}(n)=\ln\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta_{i,n}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta_{i,n}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma_{i,n}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\psi(\gamma_{i,n})-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\psi(1+\zeta_{i,n})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\psi(1+\mathfrak{h}_{i,n})-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\psi(\eta_{i,n}),

Q_{3}(n)=\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\psi^{(1)}(\gamma_{i,n})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\psi^{(1)}(1+\zeta_{i,n})-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\psi^{(1)}(1+\mathfrak{h}_{i,n})-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\psi^{(1)}(\eta_{i,n}),

\displaystyle Q_{4}(z,n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=3}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\psi^{(j-1)}(\gamma_{i,n})\frac{z^{j}}{j!}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\psi^{(j-1)}(1+\zeta_{i,n})\frac{(-z)^{j}}{j!}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\psi^{(j-1)}(1+\mathfrak{h}_{i,n})\frac{(-z)^{j}}{j!}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\psi^{(j-1)}(\eta_{i,n})\frac{z^{j}}{j!}\Big].

At this point, being within assumptions
H2
and (
2.3
), we check (separately) that each series in the right-hand side of (
3.3
) is absolutely convergent. To this end, we essentially use asymptotic representations of
\psi(z)
and
\ln\Gamma(z)
as
|z|\to+\infty
and
|\arg z|<\pi
and the recurrence relations to
\psi(z)
(see e.g.
[
1
, Section 6]
):

\displaystyle\psi(z+1)

\displaystyle=\psi(z)+z^{-1},\,\psi^{(1)}(z+1)=\psi^{(1)}(z)-z^{-2};\,\psi(z)=\ln z-\frac{1}{2z}+O(z^{-2}),\,\psi^{(1)}(z)=\frac{1}{z}+O(z^{-2}),

\displaystyle\ln\Gamma(z)

\displaystyle=(z-1/2)\ln z-z+\frac{\ln(2\pi)}{2}+\frac{1}{12z}+O(z^{-3}).

(3.4)

\bullet
Concerning the term
Q_{1}(n)
, the last asymptotic in (
3.1
)
arrives at the relation

\displaystyle Q_{1}(n)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}(n)+\frac{\ln(2\pi)}{2}[M_{6}+M_{7}-M_{5}-M_{8}]+\frac{1}{12}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i,n}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{1}{\zeta_{i,n}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{1}{\eta_{i,n}}\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma_{i,n}[\ln\gamma_{i,n}-1]+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta_{i,n}[\ln\zeta_{i,n}-1]-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}[\ln\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-1]-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta_{i,n}[\ln\eta_{i,n}-1]

\displaystyle+\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}O(\gamma_{i,n}^{-3})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}O(\zeta_{i,n}^{-3})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}O(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}^{-3})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}O(\eta_{i,n}^{-3})\bigg].

Then, collecting this equality with definition of
\mathcal{R}(n)
and taking into account assumption
H2
, we end up with the desired convergence of the series

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{M}}^{+\infty}\,|Q_{1}(n)|
.

\bullet
Asymptotic of
\psi(z)
in (
3.1
) allows us to conclude

\displaystyle Q_{2}(n)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{1}{\zeta_{i,n}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{1}{\eta_{i,n}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i,n}}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}O(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}^{-2})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}O(\gamma_{i,n}^{-2})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}O(\zeta_{i,n}^{-2})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}O(\eta_{i,n}^{-2})\bigg].

After that, we appeal to assumptions (
2.1
) and deduce that the series
\sum\limits_{n=\mathfrak{M}}^{+\infty}|Q_{2}(n)|
is convergent.

\bullet
Coming to the third term in (
3.3
), the asymptotic representation to the derivative
\psi^{(1)}(z)
in (
3.1
) entails

\displaystyle Q_{3}(n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i,n}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{1}{\zeta_{i,n}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{1}{\eta_{i,n}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}O(\mathfrak{h}^{-2}_{i,n})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}O(\gamma^{-2}_{i,n})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}O(\zeta^{-2}_{i,n})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}O(\eta^{-2}_{i,n}).

Then, assumption
H2
provides the convergence of
\sum\limits_{n=\mathfrak{M}}^{+\infty}|Q_{3}(n)|
.

\bullet
Finally, operating arguments from
[
3
, p.445]
and appealing to condition
H2
, we end up with the bound

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{M}}^{+\infty}|Q_{4}(n,z)|<C.

In summary, we conclude the absolutely convergence of series in the right-hand side of (
3.3
).
Thus, we arrive at the convergence of
\mathbb{L}_{1}(z)
for each fixed
z
which in turn finishes the proof of the first part of Theorem
2.1
in the case of
\beta=1
. ∎

3.2.
Asymptotic (
2.5
)

The key point in our arguments here is the following technical assertion which will be proved in Appendix.

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
z\in{\mathbb{C}},

z=z_{1}+iz_{2}
, and let
\{\mathfrak{b}(n)\}_{n=1}^{+\infty}
be a real strictly monotonic increasing sequence obeying the properties:

\bullet

\mathfrak{b}(n)>0
for all

n\in\mathbb{N},
and

\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{b}^{\,2}(n)}<+\infty,

\bullet
for each fixed
z_{1}
and each real number
C^{\star}
, there are integer numbers
\mathfrak{N}_{0},\mathfrak{N}_{1}\geq 1
, such that inequalities hold

\mathfrak{b}(n)\pm z_{1}>0\,\text{ for }n\geq\mathfrak{N}_{0}+1,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}\pm z_{1}>0\,\text{ for }n\geq\mathfrak{N}_{1}+1.

Besides, there exists
\mathfrak{N}\geq\max\{\mathfrak{N}_{0},\mathfrak{N}_{1}\}
such that bounds are fulfilled:

\mathfrak{b}(n)>4|z_{1}|,\quad\mathfrak{b}(n)+2C^{\star}>4|z_{1}|\quad\text{for each }n\geq\mathfrak{N}.

Then, for
|z_{2}|\to+\infty
the relations hold:

i:

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}}^{+\infty}\Big|\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)}\Big|+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}}^{+\infty}\Big|\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)(\mathfrak{b}(n)-z)}\Big|\leq C[1+\ln|z|]+O(|z|^{-1}),

ii:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}}^{+\infty}\Big|\frac{z}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z+C^{\star})(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)}\Big|+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}}^{+\infty}\Big|\frac{z}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)-z+C^{\star})(\mathfrak{b}(n)-z)}\Big|+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}}^{+\infty}\Big|\frac{z}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)-z+C^{\star})(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C+O(|z|^{-1}),

iii:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}}^{+\infty}\Big|\mathfrak{b}(n)\bigg(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\bigg)^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j+3}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{j}\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}}^{+\infty}\Big|\mathfrak{b}(n)\bigg(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)-z}\bigg)^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(-1)^{j-2}}{j+3}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)-z}\Big)^{j}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C[1+|z^{2}|+|z^{2}|\ln|z|].

iv:

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{b}(n)\bigg(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\bigg)^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j+3}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{j}

\displaystyle-\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}}^{+\infty}[\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}]\bigg(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\bigg)^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j+3}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z+C^{\star}}\Big)^{j}\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{b}(n)\bigg(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)-z}\bigg)^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(-1)^{j-2}}{j+3}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)-z}\Big)^{j}

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}}^{+\infty}[\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}]\bigg(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}-z}\bigg)^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(-1)^{j-2}}{j+3}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)-z+C^{\star}}\Big)^{j}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C|z|+O(1).

Coming to asymptotic (
2.5
) and being within assumptions (
2.2
) and (
2.3
) to
Re\,z
, we first remark that the desired asymptotic
is a simple consequence of the following relations

\displaystyle\ln\frac{\mathbb{L}(z)}{\mathbb{L}_{1}(z)}-(z-1/2)\ln\Omega_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=C_{1}^{0}\ln z+C_{2}^{0}z+O(1),

\displaystyle\ln\mathbb{L}_{1}(z)-(z-1/2)\ln\Omega_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=C_{1}^{1}\ln z+C_{2}^{1}z+\Phi^{\star}(z),\,\text{ as }|Im\,z|\to+\infty,

(3.5)

where
\Phi^{\star}(z)
obeys the properties of
\Phi(z)
,
C_{1}^{0},

C_{2}^{0}
,
C_{1}^{1}
and
C_{2}^{1}
are some constants, and

\Omega_{0}(z)=\frac{\Omega(z)}{\mathfrak{d}_{0}\Omega_{1}(z)}\exp\{-Az^{2}-Bz\}.

Thus, we are left to prove (
3.2
).
To this end, we first fixate
Re\,z
and being within assumptions
H2
and (
2.3
), we choose a positive integer number
N
(which may depend on
Re\,z
) such that the estimate holds

4|Re\,z|<\max\{\gamma_{1,N},...,\gamma_{M_{6},N},\eta_{1,N},...,\eta_{M_{8},N},\zeta_{1,N},...,\zeta_{M_{7},N},\mathfrak{h}_{1,N},...,\mathfrak{h}_{M_{5},N}\}.

(3.6)

Then putting

\displaystyle S_{1}^{0}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{M_{2}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2}\ln(\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2}+z)+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{3}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}\ln(\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}-z)-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{4}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4}\ln(\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4}+z)-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{1}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{1}\ln(d_{i}^{1}-z),

\displaystyle S_{2}^{0}(z)

\displaystyle=z[M_{3}+M_{4}-M_{2}-M_{1}],

\displaystyle S_{1,N}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}O((\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}+z)^{-3})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}O((z+\gamma_{i,n})^{-3})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}O((\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{-3})

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}O((z+\eta_{i,n})^{-3})\Big],

\displaystyle S_{1,\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}O((\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)^{-3})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}O((z+\gamma_{i,n})^{-3})+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}O((\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{-3})

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}O((z+\eta_{i,n})^{-3})\Big],

\displaystyle S_{2,N}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}(\gamma_{i,n}+z)^{-1}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)^{-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}(\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{-1}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}(\eta_{i,n}+z)^{-1}\Big],

\displaystyle S_{2,\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}(\gamma_{i,n}+z)^{-1}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)^{-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}(\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{-1}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}(\eta_{i,n}+z)^{-1}\Big],

\displaystyle S_{3,N}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}(\gamma_{i,n}\ln\frac{\gamma_{i,n}+z}{\gamma_{i,n}}-z)+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}(\zeta_{i,n}\ln\frac{\zeta_{i,n}-z}{\zeta_{i,n}}+z)+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}(z-\eta_{i,n}\ln\frac{\eta_{i,n}+z}{\eta_{i,n}})

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}\ln\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}+z)\Big]

\displaystyle S_{3,\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}(\gamma_{i,n}\ln\frac{\gamma_{i,n}+z}{\gamma_{i,n}}-z)+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}(\zeta_{i,n}\ln\frac{\zeta_{i,n}-z}{\zeta_{i,n}}+z)+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}(z-\eta_{i,n}\ln\frac{\eta_{i,n}+z}{\eta_{i,n}})

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}\ln\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}+z)\Big],

and
appealing to asymptotic (
3.1
) and assumptions (
2.2
) and (
2.3
), we rewrite the left-hand sides in (
3.2
) in the form

\displaystyle\ln\frac{\mathbb{L}(z)}{\mathbb{L}_{1}(z)}-(z-1/2)\ln\Omega_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=S_{1}^{0}(z)+S_{2}^{0}(z)+O(1),

\displaystyle\ln\mathbb{L}_{1}(z)-(z-1/2)\ln\Omega_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{3}[S_{j,N}(z)+S_{j,\infty}(z)],\quad|Imz|\to+\infty.

(3.7)

Performing technical calculations, we deduce the equality

\displaystyle S_{1}^{0}(z)

\displaystyle=\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{2}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{3}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{4}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{1}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{1}\Big]\ln z

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{z}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{2}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2})^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{3}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3})^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{4}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4})^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{1}}(\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{1})^{2}\Big]+O(1),

which in turn together with the representation of
S_{2}^{0}(z)
arrive at the first relation in (
3.2
) with

C_{1}^{0}=M_{3}+M_{4}-M_{2}-M_{1},\quad C_{2}^{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{M_{2}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{3}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{4}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{1}}\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{1}.

In order to obtain the second relation in (
3.2
), we are left to evaluate, separately, each term
S_{j,\infty}(z)
and
S_{j,N}(z)
at the right-hand side of the second relation in (
3.2
).

\bullet
Recasting the arguments leading to representation of
S_{1}^{0}(z)
and taking into account that
|Im\,z|\to+\infty
, we immediately conclude

\displaystyle S_{1,N}(z)+S_{2,N}(z)+S_{3,N}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma_{i,n}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta_{i,n}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta_{i,n}\Big]\ln z

\displaystyle+zN[M_{7}+M_{8}-M_{6}-M_{5}]+O(1).

(3.8)

\bullet
The first relation in assumption (
2.1
) and condition (
3.6
) lead to the bound

|S_{1,\infty}(z)|\leq\frac{C}{|Im\,z|}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma_{i,n}^{-2}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta_{i,n}^{-2}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta_{i,n}^{-2}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}^{-2}\Big],

which provides the desired equality

S_{1,\infty}(z)=O(1)\quad\text{as}\quad|Im\,z|\to+\infty.

\bullet
Introducing the functions:

\displaystyle S_{2,\infty}^{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\gamma_{i,n}^{-1}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}^{-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\zeta_{i,n}^{-1}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\eta_{i,n}^{-1}\Big],

\displaystyle S_{2,\infty}^{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{z}{12}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{1}{\zeta_{i,n}(\zeta_{i,n}-z)}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{1}{\eta_{i,n}(\eta_{i,n}+z)}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i,n}(\gamma_{i,n}+z)}\Bigg],

we rewrite the term

S_{2,\infty}(z)
in more suitable form

S_{2,\infty}(z)=S_{2,\infty}^{1}(z)+S_{2,\infty}^{2}(z).

It is apparent that the second inequality in (
2.1
) ensures the absolutely convergence of the series
S_{2,\infty}^{1}(z)
, which in turn means

S_{2,\infty}^{1}(z)=O(1)\quad\text{as}\quad|Im\,z|\to+\infty.

Concerning the term
S_{2,\infty}^{2}(z),
we can take advantage of the straightforward relations as
|Im\,z|\to+\infty
(which are simple consequences of
H2
and (
3.6
)):

\displaystyle\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{1}{\zeta_{i,n}|\zeta_{i,n}-z|}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{C}{\zeta_{i,n}^{2}}=O(1),\quad\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i,n}|\gamma_{i,n}+z|}\leq\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{C}{\gamma_{i,n}^{2}}=O(1),

\displaystyle\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}|\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z|}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{C}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}^{2}}=O(1),\quad\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{1}{\eta_{i,n}|\eta_{i,n}+z|}\leq\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{C}{\eta_{i,n}^{2}}=O(1).

Accordingly, we end up with the equality

S_{2,\infty}^{2}(z)=Cz+O(1)\quad\text{as}\quad|Im\,z|\to+\infty.

Collecting this equality with representation of
{S_{2,\infty}^{1}(z)}
, we deduce

S_{2,\infty}(z)=C_{7}z+O(1)\quad\text{as }|Im\,z|\to+\infty.

(3.9)

At this point, assuming additionally either (i) or (ii) conditions in Theorem
2.1
, we show that
the constant
C_{7}
in (
3.9
) equals to zero.
In light of arguments leading to (
3.9
), we are left to verify that
S_{2,\infty}^{2}(z)=O(1)
as
|Im\,z|\to+\infty
and either (i) or (ii) holds. Indeed, the technical calculations arrive at equalities:

\displaystyle S_{2,\infty}^{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{z}{12}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Bigg[C_{3}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\Big\{\frac{1}{(\zeta_{i,n}-z)(\zeta_{i,n}+C_{3}-z)(C_{3}+\zeta_{i,n})}+\frac{1}{\zeta_{i,n}(C_{3}+\zeta_{i,n})(\zeta_{i,n}-z)}\Big\}

\displaystyle-C_{4}\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\Big\{\frac{1}{(\gamma_{i,n}+z)(\gamma_{i,n}+C_{4}+z)(C_{4}+\gamma_{i,n})}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{i,n}(C_{4}+\gamma_{i,n})(\gamma_{i,n}+z)}\Big\}\Bigg]

if (i) holds while in the case of (ii)

\displaystyle S_{2,\infty}^{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{z}{12}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Bigg[-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{\gamma_{i,n}^{2}+(C_{5}+\gamma_{i,n})^{2}-C_{5}z}{\gamma_{i,n}(C_{5}+\gamma_{i,n})(C_{5}+\gamma_{i,n}-z)(\gamma_{i,n}+z)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{(C_{6}+\zeta_{i,n})^{2}+\zeta_{i,n}^{2}+C_{6}z}{\zeta_{i,n}(C_{6}+\zeta_{i,n})(\zeta_{i,n}-z)(C_{6}+\zeta_{i,n}+z)}\Bigg].

Then, appealing to assumptions (
3.6
),
H2
and Proposition
3.1
to control the right-hand sides in the relations above, we conclude

S_{2,\infty}^{2}(z)=O(1)\text{ and }S_{2,\infty}(z)=O(1)\quad\text{as }|Im\,z|\to+\infty,

and if either (i) or (ii) is fulfilled.

\bullet
Concerning the term
S_{3,\infty}(z)
, we are left to examine the following two relations:

|S_{3,\infty}(z)|\leq C|z^{2}|\ln|z|\quad\text{as }|Im\,z|\to+\infty,

(3.10)

and, under the additional assumption (i) or (ii) of Theorem
2.1
,

S_{3,\infty}(z)=C_{8}z+O(1).

(3.11)

Coming to inequality (
3.10
), we first remark that

\Big|\frac{z}{\gamma_{i,n}+z}\Big|<1,\quad\Big|\frac{z}{\zeta_{i,n}-z}\Big|<1,\quad\Big|\frac{z}{\eta_{i,n}+z}\Big|<1,\quad\Big|\frac{z}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z}\Big|<1

if
|Im\,z|\to+\infty
,
n\geq N
and (
3.6
) holds. Then, taking into account these relations and employing the
Taylor expansion, we rewrite
S_{3,\infty}(z)
in the form

S_{3,\infty}(z)=\sum_{j=1}^{4}S_{3,\infty}^{j},

(3.12)

where we set

\displaystyle S^{1}_{3,\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{2}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i,n}+z}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{1}{\eta_{i,n}+z}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{1}{\zeta_{i,n}-z}\Big];

\displaystyle S^{2}_{3,\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{z^{2}}{2}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{\gamma_{i,n}}{(\gamma_{i,n}+z)^{2}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}{(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{\zeta_{i,n}}{(\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{2}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{\eta_{i,n}}{(\eta_{i,n}+z)^{2}}\Big];

\displaystyle S^{3}_{3,\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{z^{3}}{3}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{\gamma_{i,n}}{(\gamma_{i,n}+z)^{3}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}{(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)^{3}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{\zeta_{i,n}}{(\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{3}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{\eta_{i,n}}{(\eta_{i,n}+z)^{3}}\Big];

\displaystyle S^{4}_{3,\infty}(z)

\displaystyle=z^{3}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{\gamma_{i,n}}{(\gamma_{i,n}+z)^{3}}\sum_{j=4}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j}\Big(\frac{z}{\gamma_{i,n}+z}\Big)^{j-3}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}{(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)^{3}}\sum_{j=4}^{+\infty}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{j}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z}\Big)^{j-3}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{\zeta_{i,n}}{(\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{3}}\sum_{j=4}^{+\infty}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{j}\Big(\frac{z}{\zeta_{i,n}-z}\Big)^{j-3}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{\eta_{i,n}}{(\eta_{i,n}+z)^{3}}\sum_{j=4}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j}\Big(\frac{z}{\eta_{i,n}+z}\Big)^{j-3}\Big].

After that, we examine each term
S_{3,\infty}^{j}(z)
. To this end, exploiting Proposition
3.1
and assumptions
H2
, (
3.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle|S^{1}_{3,\infty}(z)|=\Big|z^{3}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i,n}(\gamma_{i,n}+z)}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{1}{\eta_{i,n}(\eta_{i,n}+z)}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{1}{\zeta_{i,n}(\zeta_{i,n}-z)}\Big]

\displaystyle+z^{2}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i,n}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{1}{\eta_{i,n}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{1}{\zeta_{i,n}}\Big]\Big|\leq C|z^{2}|[1+\ln|z|]+O(|z|),

\displaystyle|S^{2}_{3,\infty}(z)|=\Big|\frac{z^{2}}{2}\Big|\Big|\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{z(2\eta_{i,n}+z)}{\eta_{i,n}(\eta_{i,n}+z)^{2}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{z(2\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)^{2}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{z(2\gamma_{i,n}+z)}{\gamma_{i,n}(\gamma_{i,n}+z)^{2}}+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{z(2\zeta_{i,n}-z)}{\zeta_{i,n}(\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{2}}\Big]+\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i,n}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{1}{\eta_{i,n}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{1}{\zeta_{i,n}}\Big]\Big|\leq C|z^{2}|[1+\ln|z|]+O(|z|),

\displaystyle|S^{3}_{3,\infty}(z)|=\Big|\frac{z^{2}}{3}\Big|\Big|\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{z}{(\gamma_{i,n}+z)^{2}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{z}{(\eta_{i,n}+z)^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{z}{(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)^{2}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{z}{(\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle-\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{z^{2}}{(\gamma_{i,n}+z)^{3}}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{z^{2}}{(\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}-z)^{3}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}\frac{z^{2}}{(\eta_{i,n}+z)^{3}}-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}\frac{z^{2}}{(\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{3}}\Big]\Big|\leq C(|z^{2}|+O(|z|),

|S_{3,\infty}^{4}(z)|\leq C[1+|z^{2}|+|z^{2}|\ln|z|]\quad\text{as}\quad|Im\,z|\to+\infty.

Hence, collecting these estimates with (
3.12
), we end up with inequality (
3.10
).

Finally, we are left to achieve asymptotic (
3.11
) in the case of additional assumption (i) or (ii) in Theorem
2.1
.
Here, we will carry out the detailed proof of (
3.11
) if (i) holds. The proof of (
3.11
) in the case of (ii) is almost identical and left to the interested reader.
In virtue of assumption (i), the terms
S_{3,\infty}^{j}(z),

j\in\{1,2,3,4\},
can be rewritten in the form

S_{3,\infty}^{1}(z)=-z\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\frac{C_{4}z}{(\gamma_{i,n}+z)(\gamma_{i,n}+C_{4}+z)}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\frac{C_{3}z}{(\zeta_{i,n}+C_{3}-z)(\zeta_{i,n}-z)}\Big];

\displaystyle S_{3,\infty}^{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{z}{2}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\Big[\frac{C_{4}z\gamma_{i,n}(2\gamma_{i,n}+C_{4}+2z)}{(\gamma_{i,n}+z)^{2}(\gamma_{i,n}+C_{4}+z)^{2}}-\frac{C_{4}z}{(\gamma_{i,n}+C_{4}+z)^{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\Big[\frac{C_{3}z\zeta_{i,n}(2\zeta_{i,n}+C_{3}-2z)}{(\zeta_{i,n}+C_{3}-z)^{2}(\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{2}}-\frac{C_{3}z}{(\zeta_{i,n}+C_{3}-z)^{2}}\Big]\Big);

\displaystyle S_{3,\infty}^{3}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{z}{3}\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\Big[\frac{C_{4}z^{2}\gamma_{i,n}}{(\gamma_{i,n}+z)^{3}(\gamma_{i,n}+C_{4}+z)}-\frac{C_{4}z^{2}}{(\gamma_{i,n}+C_{4}+z)^{3}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{4}z^{2}\gamma_{i,n}}{(\gamma_{i,n}+z)^{2}(\gamma_{i,n}+C_{4}+z)^{2}}+\frac{C_{4}z^{2}\gamma_{i,n}}{(\gamma_{i,n}+z)(\gamma_{i,n}+C_{4}+z)^{3}}\Big]

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\Big[\frac{C_{3}z^{2}\zeta_{i,n}}{(\zeta_{i,n}+C_{3}-z)^{3}(\zeta_{i,n}-z)}+\frac{C_{3}z^{2}}{(\zeta_{i,n}+C_{3}-z)^{3}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{3}z^{2}\zeta_{i,n}}{(\zeta_{i,n}+C_{3}-z)^{2}(\zeta_{i,n}-z)^{2}}+\frac{C_{3}z^{2}\zeta_{i,n}}{(\zeta_{i,n}+C_{3}-z)^{3}(\zeta_{i,n}-z)}\Big]\Big);

\displaystyle S_{3,\infty}^{4}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=N+1}^{+\infty}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}\Big[\gamma_{i,n}\Big(\frac{z}{\gamma_{i,n}+z}\Big)^{3}\sum_{j=4}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j}\Big(\frac{z}{\gamma_{i,n}+z}\Big)^{j-3}

\displaystyle-(C_{4}+\gamma_{i,n})\Big(\frac{z}{\gamma_{i,n}+C_{4}+z}\Big)^{3}\sum_{j=4}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j}\Big(\frac{z}{\gamma_{i,n}+z+C_{4}}\Big)^{j-3}\Big]

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}\Big[\zeta_{i,n}\Big(\frac{z}{\zeta_{i,n}-z}\Big)^{3}\sum_{j=4}^{+\infty}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{j}\Big(\frac{z}{\zeta_{i,n}-z}\Big)^{j-3}

\displaystyle-(C_{3}+\zeta_{i,n})\Big(\frac{z}{\zeta_{i,n}+C_{3}-z}\Big)^{3}\sum_{j=4}^{+\infty}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{j}\Big(\frac{z}{\zeta_{i,n}-z+C_{3}}\Big)^{j-3}\Big]\Big).

Then, taking advantage of Proposition
3.1
to evaluate the right-hand sides of these relations, we immediately end up with equality (
3.11
).

In summary, collecting relations for
S_{j,N}(z)
and
S_{j,\infty}(z)
, we complete the proof of asymptotic (
2.5
) in the case of
\beta=1
. Besides, in light of Subsection
3.2
, we finish the proof of Theorem
2.1
if
\beta=1
. ∎

Remark 3.1
.

Recasting arguments of Section
3.2
(see the proof of (
3.2
)) in the case of
\Omega_{1}=1
, we easily obtain the asymptotic (
2.5
) with
\Phi(z)=O(1)
as
|Imz|\to+\infty
and
Rez
satisfying assumptions of Theorem
2.1
.

3.3.
Conclusion of the Proof of Theorem
2.1

In order to complete the proof of Theorem
2.1
, we are left to reduce equation (
1.1
) with
\beta\neq 1
to equation (
3.1
). To this end, we introduce new variable
y=z/\beta
and new unknown function
V(y,\sigma)=\mathcal{Y}(\beta y,\sigma)
and rewrite equation (
1.1
) as

(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})V(y+1,\sigma)-\Omega(\beta y)V(y,\sigma)=\mathbb{F}(\beta y,\sigma).

After that, setting

\bar{\Omega}(y)=\Omega(\beta y),\quad\bar{\mathbb{F}}(y,\sigma)=\mathbb{F}(\beta y,\sigma),

we deduce

(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})V(y+1,\sigma)-\bar{\Omega}(y)V(y,\sigma)=\bar{\mathbb{F}}(y,\sigma).

(3.13)

Then, putting

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}^{1}=\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{1}}{\beta},\quad\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}^{2}=\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2}}{\beta},\quad\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}^{3}=\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}}{\beta},\,\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}^{4}=\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4}}{\beta},\quad\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{0}=\mathfrak{d}_{0}\beta^{M_{1}+M_{2}-M_{3}-M_{4}},\,\bar{A}=A\beta^{2},\,\bar{B}=B\beta,

\displaystyle\bar{\gamma}_{i,n}=\frac{\gamma_{i,n}}{|\beta|},\,\bar{\zeta}_{i,n}=\frac{\zeta_{i,n}}{|\beta|},\,\bar{\eta}_{i,n}=\frac{\eta_{i,n}}{|\beta|},\,\bar{\mathfrak{h}}_{i,n}=\frac{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}}{|\beta|},

we conclude that
\bar{\Omega}(y)
possesses the properties of
\Omega(z)
in the case of
\beta=1
.

Finally, setting
\bar{\mathbb{F}}(y,\sigma)\equiv 0
in (
3.13
) and recasting the arguments from Sections
3.1
-
3.2
, we complete the proof of Theorem
2.1
in the general case of
\beta
. ∎

4.
Proof of Theorem
2.2

In light of Theorem
2.1
and arguments in Section
3.3
, in order to prove Theorem
2.2
, it is enough to verify that (
2.10
) is a particular solution of (
1.1
) in the case of
\beta=1
. First, we analyze the case of
d_{0}=1
, i.e. if
\ell_{d_{0}}=\ell_{1}
.

Being within assumptions of Theorem
2.2
, we fixate a suitable periodic function
{\mathbb{P}}_{1}:={\mathbb{P}}(z)
in (
2.4
) and set

\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma):=\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})
in (
2.10
). Then, we substitute (
2.10
) in the left-hand side of (
1.1
).
Taking into account that
\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z,\sigma)

solves homogenous equation (
1.1
), we end up with relations

\displaystyle(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\mathcal{Y}_{ih}(z+1,\sigma)-\Omega(z)\mathcal{Y}_{ih}(z,\sigma)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+1,\sigma)}{\mathcal{K}_{1}(0)2i}\Big[\int_{\ell_{1}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+1+\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+2+\xi,\sigma)}d\xi-\int_{\ell_{1}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+1+\xi,\sigma)}d\xi\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+1,\sigma)}{\mathcal{K}_{1}(0)2i}\Big[\int_{\ell_{0}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+1+\xi,\sigma)}d\xi-\int_{\ell_{1}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+1+\xi,\sigma)}d\xi\Big].

In order to reach the last equality, we exploit assumptions
H4, H5
.
At last,
Cauchy’s residue theorem arrives at the desired equality

(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\mathcal{Y}_{ih}(z+1,\sigma)-\Omega(z)\mathcal{Y}_{ih}(z,\sigma)=\frac{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+1,\sigma)}{\mathcal{K}_{1}(0)}\pi\text{res}_{\xi=0}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+1+\xi,\sigma)}=\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma).

It is worth noting that, the last relations are simple consequence of
H4-H5
and (
2.6
), (
2.7
) and the fact of the function
\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\xi,\sigma)}{\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+1+\xi,\sigma)}
has no poles if
Re\,\xi\in[-1,0]
and
Re\,z
satisfies (
2.6
) and (
2.7
). Summarizing, we complete the proof of Theorem
2.2
if
\ell_{d_{0}}=\ell_{1}
in (
2.10
). We remark that the case
d_{0}=0
, i.e.
\ell_{d_{0}}=\ell_{0}
is studied with the same arguments.

Finally, collecting (
2.6
), (
2.7
) with Cauchy theorem, we arrive at the identity

\int_{\ell_{1}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+1+\xi,\sigma)}d\xi=\int_{\ell_{d_{0}}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})\mathcal{Y}_{h}(z+1+\xi,\sigma)}d\xi,\quad d_{0}\in(0,1),

which means that (
2.10
) with
d_{0}\in(0,1)
solves inhomogeneous equation (
1.1
). That completes the proof of Theorem
2.2
. ∎

Observing the proof of Theorems
2.1
and
2.2
, we conclude that arguments of Sections
2
-
4
can be extended to equation (
1.1
) with the coefficient
\Omega(z)
containing complex sequences. Indeed, in this case, instead of
H2
and
H3
, we state the following assumptions on the sequences.

H6:
For each
i
infinite sequences
\{\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
,
\{\gamma_{i,n}\}_{i=1}^{\infty},

\{\zeta_{i,n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
and
\{\eta_{i,n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
are complex-valued and satisfy the second estimate in (
2.1
). Moreover, the following relations hold

\displaystyle 0<Re\,\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}<Re\,\mathfrak{h}_{i,n+1},\,0<Re\,\gamma_{i,n}<Re\,\gamma_{i,n+1},\,0<Re\,\zeta_{i,n}<Re\,\zeta_{i,n+1},\,0<Re\,\eta_{i,n}<Re\,\eta_{i,n+1};

\displaystyle 0\leq Im\,\mathfrak{h}_{i,n}\leq Im\,\mathfrak{h}_{i,n+1},\,0\leq Im\,\gamma_{i,n}\leq Im\,\gamma_{i,n+1},\,0\leq Im\,\zeta_{i,n}\leq Im\,\zeta_{i,n+1},\,0\leq Im\,\eta_{i,n}\leq Im\,\eta_{i,n+1};

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{M_{5}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{i,n})^{-2}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{6}}(\widehat{\zeta}_{i,n})^{-2}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{7}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{i,n})^{-2}+\sum_{i=1}^{M_{8}}(\widehat{\eta}_{i,n})^{-2}\Big]<C,

where for each
i
we set

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{i,n}=\begin{cases}Re\,\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{i,n},\quad\text{if}\,Im\,\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{i,n}\equiv const.\,\text{for all }n,\\
\min\{Re\,\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{i,n},Im\,\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}_{i,n}\},\quad\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}\quad\widehat{\gamma}_{i,n}=\begin{cases}Re\,\widehat{\gamma}_{i,n},\quad\text{if}\quad Im\,\widehat{\gamma}_{i,n}\equiv const.\,\text{for all }n,\\
\min\{Re\,\widehat{\gamma}_{i,n},Im\,\widehat{\gamma}_{i,n}\},\quad\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\widehat{\zeta}_{i,n}=\begin{cases}Re\,\widehat{\zeta}_{i,n},\quad\text{if}\quad Im\,\widehat{\zeta}_{i,n}\equiv const.\,\text{for all }n,\\
\min\{Re\,\widehat{\zeta}_{i,n},Im\,\widehat{\zeta}_{i,n}\},\quad\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}\quad\widehat{\eta}_{i,n}=\begin{cases}Re\,\widehat{\eta}_{i,n},\quad\text{if}\quad Im\,\widehat{\eta}_{i,n}\equiv const.\,\text{for all }n,\\
\min\{Re\,\widehat{\eta}_{i,n},Im\,\widehat{\eta}_{i,n}\},\quad\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

H7:
Finite sequences:
\{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{1}\}_{i=1}^{M_{1}},

\{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{2}\}_{i=1}^{M_{2}},

\{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}\}_{i=1}^{M_{3}},

\{\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{4}\}_{i=1}^{M_{4}},
are complex-valued and
\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{1}\neq 0

for all
i\in\{1,2,...,M_{1}\}
,
\mathfrak{d}_{i}^{3}\neq 0

for all
i\in\{1,2,...,M_{3}\}
.

After that, recasting the arguments of Sections
3
and
4
with nonessential modifications, we deduce the following result.

Theorem 4.1
.

Under assumptions
H1,H6,H7
and (
2.2
) and (
2.3
), a general solution of homogenous equation (
1.1
) is given with (
2.5
) and, besides, statements
(s.1)
and
(s.2)
of Theorem
2.1
hold.

If, in addition, assumptions
H4-H5
and (
2.6
) and (2.7) hold, then a general solution of inhomogeneous equation (
1.1
) is given with (
2.10
) and (
2.9
).

5.
Solvability of other functional equations

In this section, we will exploit the technique developed in Sections
2
-
4
to find solutions of another class of functional difference equations. To this end, we focus on the equation:

(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})Y(z+\beta,\sigma)-\mathcal{S}(z)Y(z,\sigma)=\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma),

(5.1)

where
Y=Y(z,\sigma)
is unknown function, quantities
a_{1},

a_{2}
,
\sigma
,
\nu
,
\beta
meet requirement
H1
, and
\mathbb{F}
is a given function specified below. Coming to the variable coefficient
\mathcal{S}:=\mathcal{S}(z),
we will analyze here different kinds of this function.
Namely, introducing entire functions
\mathcal{S}_{1}:=\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}:=\mathcal{S}_{2}(z)
,
\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)\neq 0,
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}(z)\neq 0,
we will say that

\bullet

\mathcal{S}(z)
is a function of the first kind (
FFK
), if
\mathcal{S}(z)=\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)
;

\bullet

\mathcal{S}(z)
is called a function of the second kind (
FSK
), if
\mathcal{S}(z)=\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{2}(z)}
;

\bullet
and, finally,
\mathcal{S}(z)=\frac{\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)}{\mathcal{S}_{2}(z)}
is a function of the third kind (
FTK
).

Here, we analyze (
5.1
) in the case of
\mathcal{S}(z)
is either an entire function or is presented as a quotient of two entire functions.
It is apparent that,
\mathcal{S}(z)
of the second kind is a particular case of the
FTK
case (i.e. with
\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)=1
).

In our analysis, we will exploit the following strategy. In the first step, appealing to properties of entire functions, we describe sufficient conditions on the function
\mathcal{S}(z)
, which allow us to present
\mathcal{S}(z)
as a finite or an infinite product. Thus, we reduce equation (
5.1
) to (
1.1
) with the corresponding coefficient
\Omega(z)
constructed by
\mathcal{S}(z)
. Besides, we give several examples of the function
\mathcal{S}(z)
together with its factorization. Then, we apply Theorems
2.1
-
2.2
or
4.1
in
order to solve equation (
5.1
). Finally, we provide several examples of (
5.1
) (with explicit view of
\mathcal{S}(z)
and
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
)
illustrating the obtained results.

5.1.
Factorization of
\mathcal{S}(z)

At this point, we start by specifying the functions
\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)
,
\mathcal{S}_{2}(z)
and, accordingly, the function
\mathcal{S}(z)
. Let
\mathfrak{z}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{y}_{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N},
be zeros of the functions
\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}(z)
, correspondingly, lying in the plane
Re\,z>0
; and let
\mathfrak{z}_{-n}
and
\mathfrak{y}_{-n}
be zeros of these functions being located in the plane
Re\,z<0
. The number of these zeros will be indicated below.
Denoting

\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{\pm n}=\begin{cases}|Re\,\mathfrak{z}_{\pm n}|,\quad\text{if}\,Im\,\mathfrak{z}_{\pm n}\equiv const.\text{ for all n},\\
\min\{|Re\,\mathfrak{z}_{\pm n}|,|Im\,\mathfrak{z}_{\pm n}|\},\qquad\text{otherwise},\end{cases}\quad\widehat{\mathfrak{y}}_{\pm n}=\begin{cases}|Re\,\mathfrak{y}_{\pm n}|,\quad\text{if}\,Im\,\mathfrak{y}_{\pm n}\equiv const.\text{ for all n},\\
\min\{|Re\,\mathfrak{y}_{\pm n}|,|Im\,\mathfrak{y}_{\pm n}|\},\qquad\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

we state our assumptions.

H8 (Assumption on
\mathcal{S}_{1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}
):
We assume that entire functions
\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}(z)
have order
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
, respectively,
0\leq\kappa_{1}\leq 2,

0\leq\kappa_{2}\leq 2.

Besides,
\mathcal{S}_{1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}
have either a finite or an infinite number of zeros.

H9 (Assumption on the zeros of
\mathcal{S}_{1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}
):
We require that all zeros of
\mathcal{S}_{1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}
repeated according to multiplicity are arranged as:

0<|\mathfrak{z}_{n}|\leq|\mathfrak{z}_{n+1}|,\quad n\in\{1,2,...,N_{1}\},\quad 0<|\mathfrak{z}_{-n}|\leq|\mathfrak{z}_{-n-1}|,\quad n\in\{1,2,...,N_{2}\},

0<|\mathfrak{y}_{n}|\leq|\mathfrak{y}_{n+1}|,\quad n\in\{1,2,...,N_{3}\},\quad 0<|\mathfrak{y}_{-n}|\leq|\mathfrak{y}_{-n-1}|,\quad n\in\{1,2,...,N_{4}\}.

We assume also that
\mathfrak{z}_{0}=0
is the
\mu_{1}-
multiple root of
\mathcal{S}_{1}
, while
\mathfrak{y}_{0}=0
is the
\mu_{2}-
multiple root of
\mathcal{S}_{2}
.
The case
\mu_{1}=0
or/and
\mu_{2}=0
being taken to mean
\mathcal{S}_{1}(0)\neq 0
or/and
\mathcal{S}_{2}(0)\neq 0
.

H10 (Assumption on the sequences of zeros):
In the case of
N_{j}=+\infty
,
j\in\{1,2,3,4\},
we assume
that the sequences
\{|Re\,\mathfrak{z}_{\pm n}|\}_{n=1}^{+\infty}
,
\{|Re\,\mathfrak{y}_{\pm n}|\}_{n=1}^{+\infty}
are
strictly monotonically increasing, and

\underset{n\to+\infty}{\lim}|\mathfrak{z}_{-n}|=+\infty,\quad\underset{n\to+\infty}{\lim}|\mathfrak{z}_{n}|=+\infty,\quad\underset{n\to+\infty}{\lim}|\mathfrak{y}_{-n}|=+\infty,\quad\underset{n\to+\infty}{\lim}|\mathfrak{y}_{n}|=+\infty,

Concerning to
\{|Im\,\mathfrak{z}_{\pm n}|\}_{n=1}^{+\infty}
,
\{|Im\,\mathfrak{y}_{\pm n}|\}_{n=1}^{+\infty}
, we assume either all terms of the corresponding sequences are constant, or these sequence do not decrease.
In addition, we require the convergence of the series:
