\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\widehat{\mathfrak{z}\,}^{-2}_{n},\,\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\widehat{\mathfrak{z}\,}^{-2}_{-n}
,
\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\widehat{\mathfrak{y}\,}^{-2}_{n},\,\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\widehat{\mathfrak{y}\,}^{-2}_{-n}
and, besides

\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big|\frac{1}{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{n}}+\frac{1}{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{-n}}\Big|<+\infty\,\text{ in the \bf{FFK} case,}\,\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big|\frac{1}{\widehat{\mathfrak{y}}_{n}}+\frac{1}{\widehat{\mathfrak{y}}_{-n}}\Big|<+\infty\,\text{ in the \bf{FSK} case,}

\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big|\frac{1}{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{n}}+\frac{1}{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}_{-n}}+\frac{1}{\widehat{\mathfrak{y}}_{n}}+\frac{1}{\widehat{\mathfrak{y}}_{-n}}\Big|<+\infty\quad\text{in the \bf{FTK} case.}

Denote by

P_{1}(z)=\begin{cases}0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if}\quad\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{z}_{n}|}+\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{z}_{-n}|}\Big)<+\infty,\\
z\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{z}_{n}|}+\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{z}_{-n}|}\Big),\quad\text{if}\quad\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{z}_{n}|}+\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{z}_{-n}|}\Big)=+\infty,\end{cases}

P_{2}(z)=\begin{cases}0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if}\quad\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{y}_{n}|}+\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{y}_{-n}|}\Big)<+\infty,\\
z\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{y}_{n}|}+\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{y}_{-n}|}\Big),\quad\text{if}\quad\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{y}_{n}|}+\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{y}_{-n}|}\Big)=+\infty.\end{cases}

The following result is a direct consequence of the Weierstrass factorization theorem
[
24
, Chapter 7,
\lx@sectionsign
2.3]
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let assumptions
H8-H10
hold. If
\mathcal{S}_{1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}
have a finite number of zeroes, then there are representations:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=A_{0}^{I}z^{\mu_{1}}(-1)^{N_{1}}\prod_{n=1}^{N_{1}}(\mathfrak{z}_{n}-z)\prod_{n=1}^{N_{2}}(z-\mathfrak{z}_{-n})\exp\{g_{1}(z)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=A_{0}^{II}z^{\mu_{2}}(-1)^{N_{3}}\prod_{n=1}^{N_{3}}(\mathfrak{y}_{n}-z)\prod_{n=1}^{N_{4}}(z-\mathfrak{y}_{-n})\exp\{g_{2}(z)\},

with
g_{1}(z)
and
g_{2}(z)
being polynomials whose degree is less than integer part of
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
, respectively.

In the case of infinite sequences of zeros to
\mathcal{S}_{1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}
, the function
\mathcal{S}(z)
is presented as

\mathcal{S}(z)=\begin{cases}z^{\mu_{1}}A_{0}^{I}e^{g_{1}(z)+P_{1}(z)}\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(\mathfrak{z}_{n}-z)(z-\mathfrak{z}_{-n})}{\mathfrak{z}_{n}(-\mathfrak{z}_{-n})}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{in the \bf{FFK} case},\\
\,\\
z^{-\mu_{2}}A_{0}^{II}e^{-g_{2}(z)-P_{2}(z)}\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathfrak{y}_{n}(-\mathfrak{y}_{-n})}{(\mathfrak{y}_{n}-z)(z-\mathfrak{y}_{-n})}\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\text{in the \bf{FSK} case},\\
\,\\
z^{\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}}\frac{A_{0}^{I}}{A_{0}^{II}}e^{g_{1}(z)-g_{2}(z)+P_{1}(z)-P_{2}(z)}\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(\mathfrak{z}_{n}-z)(z-\mathfrak{z}_{-n})\mathfrak{y}_{n}(-\mathfrak{y}_{-n})}{(\mathfrak{y}_{n}-z)(z-\mathfrak{y}_{-n})\mathfrak{z}_{n}(-\mathfrak{z}_{-n})}\quad\text{in the \bf{FTK} case}.\end{cases}

(5.2)

As a consequence of the above results, we are left with the problem of finding a factorization to given below functions
\mathcal{S}(z)
. First, we start to analyze with
FFK
case. To this end, we denote

\theta_{+}:=\theta_{+}(\theta)=\begin{cases}\theta,\qquad\text{if }\theta\in[0,\pi),\\
\theta-\pi,\quad\text{if }\theta\in[\pi,2\pi),\end{cases}\quad\theta_{-}:=\theta_{-}(\theta)=\begin{cases}\theta,\qquad\text{if }\theta\in[-\pi/2,\pi/2),\\
\theta-\pi,\quad\text{if }\theta\in[\pi/2,3\pi/2),\\
\theta-2\pi,\quad\text{if }\theta\in[3\pi/2,2\pi).\end{cases}

(5.3)

Moreover, for simplicity consideration, we belive

\frac{\sin\theta_{+}}{\theta_{+}}=1
if
\theta_{+}=0
or
\pi
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\theta\in[0,2\pi)
,
\theta\neq\pm\pi/2,3\pi/2
, and
q_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
q_{0}\neq 0
. Then, there are the following relations:

(i)

\sin(q_{0}z\pm\theta)=(-1)^{\lfloor\theta/\pi\rfloor}q_{0}\frac{\sin\theta_{+}}{\theta_{+}}\big(z\pm\frac{\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}\big)\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\Big(\frac{\pi n\mp\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}-z\Big)\Big(\frac{\pi n\pm\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}+z\Big)}{\Big(\frac{\pi n+\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}\Big)\Big(\frac{\pi n-\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}\Big)},

where
\lfloor\theta\rfloor
is the floor function of
\theta
(i.e. the greatest integer less than or equal to
\theta
);

(ii)

\cos(q_{0}z\pm\theta)=(-1)^{\lfloor\frac{\theta}{\pi}+\frac{1}{2}\rfloor}\cos\theta_{-}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\Big(\frac{\pi(2n-1)\mp 2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}-z\Big)\Big(\frac{\pi(2n-1)\pm 2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}+z\Big)}{\Big(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}\Big)\Big(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}\Big)},

(iii)

\tan(q_{0}z\pm\theta)=(-1)^{\lfloor\frac{\theta}{\pi}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{2\theta+\pi}{2\pi}\rfloor}\frac{\sin\theta_{+}}{\theta_{+}\cos\theta_{-}}\bigg(z\pm\frac{\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}\bigg)\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\Big(\frac{\pi n\mp\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}-z\Big)}{\Big(\frac{\pi(2n-1)\mp 2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}-z\Big)}\frac{\Big(\frac{\pi n\pm\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}+z\Big)}{\Big(\frac{\pi(2n-1)\pm 2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}+z\Big)}

\times\frac{\Big(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}\Big)\Big(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}\Big)}{\Big(\frac{\pi n-\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}\Big)\Big(\frac{\pi n+\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}\Big)}.

Proof.

First of all, we remark that in the case of
\theta=0
and
q_{0}=1
, relations
(i)-(iii)
boil down to well-known decompositions of

\sin z=z\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big(1-\frac{z^{2}}{n^{2}\pi^{2}}\Big),

\cos z=\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\Big(1-\frac{4z^{2}}{(2n-1)^{2}\pi^{2}}\Big)
and their quotient. Actually, the proof of these statements is a simple consequence of Lemma
5.1
. Indeed, it is apparent that the functions
\sin(q_{0}z\pm\theta)
and
\cos(q_{0}z\pm\theta)
can be considered as the function
\mathcal{S}(z)
of the
FFK
while
\tan(q_{0}z\pm\theta)
is the
FTK
.

We begin with getting representation (i) and (ii). In light of
[
24
, Chapter 7, §1]
, we can conclude that
\sin(q_{0}z\pm\theta)
and
\cos(q_{0}z\pm\theta)
have the order
1
, and all their zeros are given by:

\bullet

\mathfrak{z}_{0}=\mp\frac{\theta}{q_{0}},

\mathfrak{z}_{n}=\frac{\pi n\mp\theta_{+}}{q_{0}},

\mathfrak{z}_{-n}=\frac{\pi n\pm\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}
,
n\in\mathbb{N},
in the case of
\sin(q_{0}z\pm\theta),

\bullet

\mathfrak{y}_{n}=\frac{\pi(2n-1)\mp\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}},

\mathfrak{y}_{-n}=-\frac{\pi(2n-1)\pm 2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}
,
n\in\mathbb{N},
in the case of
\cos(q_{0}z\pm\theta).

These values tell us that assumptions
H8-H10
are satisfied and therefore we can apply Lemma
5.1
. As a result, we have

\displaystyle\sin(q_{0}z\pm\theta)

\displaystyle=A_{0}^{\pm}e^{z\Big(A_{1}^{\pm}\pm\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{2q_{0}\theta_{+}}{n^{2}\pi^{2}-\theta_{+}^{2}}\Big)}\Big(z\pm\frac{\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}\Big)\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\bigg(\frac{\pi n\mp\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi n\pm\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}+z\bigg)}{\bigg(\frac{\pi n+\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi n-\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}\bigg)},

\displaystyle\cos(q_{0}z\pm\theta)

\displaystyle=B_{0}^{\pm}e^{z\Big(B_{1}^{\pm}\mp\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{4q_{0}\theta_{-}}{(2n-1)^{2}\pi^{2}-4\theta_{-}^{2}}\Big)}\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)\mp 2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)\pm 2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}+z\bigg)}{\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}}{2q_{0}}\bigg)}

(5.4)

where numbers
A_{0}^{\pm},

A_{1}^{\pm},

B_{0}^{\pm}
and
B_{1}^{\pm}
are unknown coefficients which will be identified below.

To this end, we will exploit the easy verified identities:

\sin(q_{0}z\pm\theta)=(-1)^{\lfloor\theta/\pi\rfloor}\sin(q_{0}z\pm\theta_{+}),

\cos(q_{0}z\pm\theta)=(-1)^{\lfloor\frac{2\theta+\pi}{2\pi}\rfloor}\cos(q_{0}z\pm\theta_{-}),

if
\theta\in[0,2\pi)
.
Then, substituting
z=0
to relations (
5.1
) and taking into account identities above, we arrive at the equalities

A_{0}^{\pm}=\frac{(-1)^{\lfloor\theta/\pi\rfloor}q_{0}\sin\theta_{+}}{\theta_{+}}\quad\text{and}\quad B_{0}^{\pm}=(-1)^{\lfloor\frac{2\theta+\pi}{2\pi}\rfloor}\cos\theta_{-}.

(5.5)

It is apparent, that the proof of statement (i) and (ii) of this proposition follows immediately from the relations:

A_{1}^{\pm}=\mp\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{2\theta_{+}q_{0}}{n^{2}\pi^{2}-\theta^{2}_{+}}\quad\text{and}\quad B_{1}^{\pm}=\pm\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{4\theta_{-}q_{0}}{(2n-1)^{2}\pi^{2}-4\theta^{2}_{-}},

which will be verified here below.

Here, we will carry out the detailed proof of the equality to
A_{1}^{+}
. The proof of the other identities is almost identical. Next, we take advantage of the straightforward relations:

\displaystyle\underset{z\to-\frac{\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}}{\lim}\frac{\sin(q_{0}z+\theta)}{q_{0}z+\theta_{+}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\lfloor\frac{\theta}{\pi}\rfloor},\quad\underset{z\to\frac{\pi-\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}}{\lim}\,\frac{\sin(q_{0}z+\theta)}{q_{0}z-\pi+\theta_{+}}=(-1)^{1+\lfloor\frac{\theta}{\pi}\rfloor},\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{2q_{0}\theta_{+}}{\pi^{2}n^{2}-\theta_{+}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{q_{0}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{x^{\theta_{+}/\pi}-x^{-\theta_{+}/\pi}}{1-x}dx=-\frac{q_{0}}{\theta_{+}}+q_{0}\cot\theta_{+}.

(5.6)

It is worth noting that, the last equality is consequence of formulas (0.244(1)) and (3.231(3)) in
[
15
]
. Then, substituting
z=\frac{-\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}
and
z=\frac{\pi-\theta_{+}}{\theta_{q_{0}}}
in the representation for
\sin(q_{0}z+\theta)
in (
5.1
) and taking into account the relations above, we deduce

\begin{cases}(-1)^{\lfloor\theta/\pi\rfloor}=\frac{\sin\theta_{+}}{\theta_{+}}(-1)^{\lfloor\theta/\pi\rfloor}\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(\pi n)^{2}}{\pi^{2}n^{2}-\theta_{+}^{2}}\exp\Big\{-\frac{\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}[A_{1}^{+}+q_{0}\cot\theta_{+}-q_{0}/\theta_{+}]\Big\},\\
(-1)^{1+\lfloor\theta/\pi\rfloor}=\frac{\sin\theta_{+}}{\theta_{+}}(-1)^{1+\lfloor\theta/\pi\rfloor}\frac{\pi\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}\pi(n+1)\prod_{n=2}^{+\infty}\pi(n-1)}{\prod_{n=1}^{+\infty}(\pi^{2}n^{2}-\theta_{+}^{2})}\exp\Big\{\frac{\pi-\theta_{+}}{q_{0}}[A_{1}^{+}+q_{0}\cot\theta_{+}-q_{0}/\theta_{+}]\Big\}.\end{cases}

Thus, we end up with the equality

\exp\Big\{\frac{\pi}{q_{0}}[A_{1}^{+}+q_{0}\cot\theta_{+}-q_{0}/\theta_{+}]\Big\}=1,

(5.7)

which provides the desired relations to
A_{1}^{+}
. This completes the proof of statements
(i)
and
(ii)
in this claim.

Finally, the representation in
(iii)
is simple consequence of Lemma
5.1
and relations in
(i)
and
(ii)
of this proposition. Hence, the proof of Proposition
5.1
is finished.
∎

Our next results are connected with a factorization of the functions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{+}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=\sin(z-\theta_{1})+q_{2}\sin(q_{1}z-\theta_{2}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{-}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=\sin(z-\theta_{1})-q_{2}\sin(q_{1}z-\theta_{2}),

and their quotient.
Here
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}\in[0,2\pi)
,
q_{2}
and
q_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,
q_{2}\neq 0,

q_{1}\neq 0
.
First, denoting the zeros of the functions
\mathcal{S}^{\pm}
in the segment
[0,4\pi q),
with fixed
q\in\mathbb{N}
by

z_{i}^{\pm}:=z_{i}^{\pm}(q_{1},q_{2},\theta_{1},\theta_{2}),\quad i\in\{0,1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\pm}\},

and introducing the sets for any integer
k
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}(k,\theta_{1},q_{2})

\displaystyle=[\theta_{1}-\arcsin q_{2}+2\pi k;\theta_{1}+\arcsin q_{2}+2\pi k]

\displaystyle\cup[\theta_{1}+\pi-\arcsin q_{2}+2\pi k;\theta_{1}+\pi+\arcsin q_{2}+2\pi k],

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}^{\star}(k,\theta_{2},q_{1})

\displaystyle=\Big[\frac{\pi k+\theta_{2}}{q_{1}}-\frac{\pi}{2q_{1}};\frac{\pi k+\theta_{2}}{q_{1}}+\frac{\pi}{2q_{1}}\Big],

we assert the following result.

Corollary 5.1
.

Let
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in[0,\pi]
,
q_{2},q_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,
p,q\in\mathbb{N}
and let
q/p
be not reducible fraction. We assume that

q_{2}>0,\quad q_{2}\neq 1,\quad\text{and}\quad p>2q>1,\quad q_{1}=p/2q.

Then there are the following statements:

i:

\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})
are entire periodic functions with the period
\mathbb{T}_{q}=4\pi q.
Besides, if
\theta_{i}=0
or
\pi,

i=1,2,
these functions are odd, while in the case of
\theta_{1}=\theta_{2}=\pi/2
,
\mathcal{S}^{\pm}
are even.

ii:

All zeros
\mathfrak{z}^{\pm}_{i}
of
\mathcal{S}^{\pm}
are real and their number in the segment
[0,4\pi q)
equals to
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\pm}:={\boldsymbol{K}}^{\pm}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},

q_{1},q_{2})
.

iii:

If
q_{2}>1
, then
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\pm}=2p
and all zeros are simple and satisfy relations:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{z}_{0}^{\pm}<\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{\pm}<...<\mathfrak{z}_{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\pm}-1}^{\pm}<4\pi q,\quad\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}\in\mathfrak{S}^{\star}(i,\theta_{2},q_{1}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}

\displaystyle\in\begin{cases}\mathfrak{S}^{\star}(i-1,\theta_{2},q_{1})\quad\text{if }\theta_{2}\in(\pi/2,\pi),\,\theta_{1}\in(0,\pi)\,\text{and }q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}<\sin\theta_{1};\\
\mathfrak{S}^{\star}(i+1,\theta_{2},q_{1})\quad\text{if }\theta_{2}\in(0,\min\{q_{1}\theta_{1},\pi/2\}),\,\theta_{1}\in(0,\pi)\,\text{and }q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}<\sin\theta_{1};\\
\mathfrak{S}^{\star}(i,\theta_{2},q_{1})\quad\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

for each
i\in\{0,1,...,2p-1\}.

iv:

If
q_{2}<1
and
q_{2}\neq 1/q_{1},
then all zeros of
\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})
are simple and strictly increasing, i.e.
\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\pm}<\mathfrak{z}_{i+1}^{\pm}
, and
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\pm}\in[4q,2p]
, while in the case of
q_{2}=1/q_{1}
the zeros are non-decreasing and some of them may have the third order,
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\pm}\in(4q,6p)
. Moreover,

\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\pm}\in\bigcup_{k=l_{1}}^{l_{2}}\mathfrak{S}(k,\theta_{1},q_{2}),

i\in\{0,1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\pm}-1,\}
where

l_{1}=\begin{cases}0,\qquad\text{if }0<\theta_{1}+\arcsin q_{2}\leq\pi,\\
-1,\quad\text{if }\pi<\theta_{1}+\arcsin q_{2}\leq 3\pi/2,\end{cases}\quad l_{2}=\begin{cases}2q-1,\quad\text{if }0<\theta_{1}-\arcsin q_{2}\leq\pi,\\
2q,\qquad\text{ if }0\leq\arcsin q_{2}-\theta_{1}\leq\pi.\end{cases}

v:

All zeros of the function
\mathcal{S}^{+}
are given with

\mathfrak{z}_{i,0}^{+}=\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+},\quad\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+}=\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}+n\mathbb{T}_{q},\quad\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{+}=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-n\mathbb{T}_{q},\,\text{if }\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(0,\pi),\\
-\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+},\quad\text{ if }\theta_{1},\theta_{2}=0\text{ or }\pi,\end{cases}

for each
i\in\{0,1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1\},
and
n\in\mathbb{N},
while the zeros of
\mathcal{S}^{-}
are presented with

\mathfrak{z}_{i,0}^{-}=\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-},\quad\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}=\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}+n\mathbb{T}_{q},\quad\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-}=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}-n\mathbb{T}_{q},\quad\text{if }\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(0,\pi),\\
-\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-},\quad\quad\text{ if }\theta_{1},\theta_{2}=0\text{ or }\pi,\end{cases}

for each
i\in\{0,1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1\},
and
n\in\mathbb{N},

vi:

\mathfrak{z}_{0}^{\pm}=0
solves the equation
\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=0
iff the equality holds

\sin\theta_{1}\pm q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}=0.

(5.8)

Besides,
\mathfrak{z}=\theta_{1}\pm n\mathbb{T}_{q},

n\in\mathbb{N}
are zeros of
\mathcal{S}^{\pm}
if

\frac{|q_{1}\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}|}{2\pi}-\bigg\lfloor\frac{|q_{1}\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}|}{2\pi}\bigg\rfloor=0.

vii:

If either
\theta_{1}
or
\theta_{2}\in(\pi,2\pi],
then the relations hold

\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=\begin{cases}-\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\hat{\theta}_{1},\hat{\theta}_{2},q_{1},q_{2}),\quad\text{if }\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(\pi,2\pi],\\
\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\theta_{1},\hat{\theta}_{2},q_{1},q_{2}),\qquad\text{if }\theta_{1}\in[0,\pi],\,\theta_{2}\in(\pi,2\pi],\\
-\mathcal{S}^{\mp}(z;\hat{\theta}_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2}),\quad\text{if }\theta_{1}\in(\pi,2\pi],\,\theta_{2}\in[0,\pi],\end{cases}

where
\hat{\theta}_{1}=\theta_{1}-\pi,

\hat{\theta}_{2}=\theta_{2}-\pi.

Proof.

This claim in the case of
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}=0
or
\pi
is proved in
[
7
, Proposition 3.1]
. The case of
\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},

q_{1},q_{2})
is analyzed in
[
5
, Section 3.1]
. Thus, recasting the same arguments in the case of
\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})
and performing standard technical calculations, we complete the proof of Corollary
5.1
.
∎

Setting

\mu_{1}^{+}=\begin{cases}1,\quad\text{if }z=0\text{ is a simple root of }\mathcal{S}^{+},\\
3,\quad\text{if }z=0\text{ is a 3-multiple root of }\mathcal{S}^{+},\end{cases}\quad\mu_{1}^{-}=\begin{cases}1,\quad\text{if }z=0\text{ is a simple root of }\mathcal{S}^{-},\\
3,\quad\text{if }z=0\text{ is a 3-multiple root of }\mathcal{S}^{-},\end{cases}

we assert the following result which is simple consequence of Lemma
5.1
and Corollary
5.1
.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let assumptions of Corollary
5.1
hold. Then, there are the following decompositions.

i:

If
\sin\theta_{1}-q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}\neq 0
and
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(0,\pi)
, then

\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=-[\sin\theta_{1}-q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}]\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}-z]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}-z][-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-}+z]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}(-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-})}.

In the case of
\sin\theta_{1}-q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}=0
and
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(0,\pi)
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\lfloor\mu_{1}^{-}/2\rfloor}z^{\mu_{1}^{-}}[\cos\theta_{1}-q_{1}^{\mu_{1}^{-}}q_{2}\cos\theta_{2}]\prod\limits_{i=\mu_{1}^{-}+1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}-z]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}

\displaystyle\times\prod\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}-z][-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-}+z]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}(-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-})}.

ii:

If
\sin\theta_{1}+q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}\neq 0
and
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(0,\pi)
, then

\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=-[\sin\theta_{1}+q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}]\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-z]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+}-z][-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{+}+z]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+}(-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{+})}.

In the case of
\sin\theta_{1}+q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}=0
and
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(0,\pi)
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\lfloor\mu_{1}^{+}/2\rfloor}z^{\mu_{1}^{+}}[\cos\theta_{1}+q_{1}^{\mu_{1}^{+}}q_{2}\cos\theta_{2}]\prod\limits_{i=\mu_{1}^{+}+1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-z]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}}

\displaystyle\times\prod\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+}-z][-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{+}+z]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+}(-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{+})}.

iii:

If
\theta_{1}=\theta_{2}=0
, then

\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;0,0,q_{1},q_{2})=(1+q_{1}q_{2})z\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\frac{(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-z)(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}+z)}{(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+})^{2}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+}-z][\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+}+z]}{(\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+})^{2}},

\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;0,0,q_{1},1/q_{1})=-(1-q_{1}^{3}q_{2})z^{3}\prod\limits_{i=4}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}-z)(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}+z)}{(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-})^{2}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}-z][\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}+z]}{(\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-})^{2}},

\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;0,0,q_{1},q_{2})=(1-q_{1}q_{2})z\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}-z)(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}+z)}{(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-})^{2}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}-z][\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}+z]}{(\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-})^{2}},\quad\text{if }\,q_{2}q_{1}\neq 1.

Besides, the equalities are fulfilled

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\pi,\pi,q_{1},q_{2})

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;0,0,q_{1},q_{2}),\quad\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;0,\pi,q_{1},q_{2})=\mathcal{S}^{\mp}(z;0,0,q_{1},q_{2}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\pi,0,q_{1},q_{2})

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{S}^{\mp}(z;0,0,q_{1},q_{2}).

iv:

If either
\theta_{1}\in(\pi,2\pi)
or
\theta_{2}\in(\pi,2\pi)
then

\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=\begin{cases}-\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1}-\pi,\theta_{2}-\pi,q_{1},q_{2}),\quad\text{if }\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(\pi,2\pi),\\
\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2}-\pi,q_{1},q_{2}),\quad\text{if }\theta_{1}\in[0,\pi],\,\theta_{2}\in(\pi,2\pi),\\
-\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1}-\pi,\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2}),\quad\text{if }\theta_{1}\in(\pi,2\pi),\,\theta_{2}\in[0,\pi],\end{cases}

\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=\begin{cases}-\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1}-\pi,\theta_{2}-\pi,q_{1},q_{2}),\quad\text{if }\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(\pi,2\pi),\\
\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2}-\pi,q_{1},q_{2}),\quad\text{if }\theta_{1}\in[0,\pi],\,\theta_{2}\in(\pi,2\pi),\\
-\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1}-\pi,\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2}),\quad\text{if }\theta_{1}\in(\pi,2\pi),\,\theta_{2}\in[0,\pi].\end{cases}

v:

For
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in[0,2\pi)
there are relations:

\displaystyle\sin(z+\theta_{1})-q_{2}\sin(q_{1}z+\theta_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;2\pi-\theta_{1},2\pi-\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2});

\displaystyle\sin(z+\theta_{1})+q_{2}\sin(q_{1}z+\theta_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;2\pi-\theta_{1},2\pi-\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2});

\displaystyle\cos(z-\theta_{1})\pm q_{2}\cos(q_{1}z-\theta_{2})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\frac{\pi}{2}+\theta_{1},\frac{\pi}{2}+\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2}),\text{ if }\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in[0,3\pi/2),\\
-\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\theta_{1}-\frac{3\pi}{2},\theta_{2}-\frac{3\pi}{2},q_{1},q_{2}),\text{ if }\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in[3\pi/2,2\pi),\\
-\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\frac{\pi}{2}+\theta_{1},\theta_{2}-\frac{3\pi}{2},q_{1},q_{2}),\text{ if }\theta_{1}\in[0,3\pi/2),\,\theta_{2}\in[3\pi/2,2\pi),\\
-\mathcal{S}^{\pm}(z;\theta_{1}-\frac{3\pi}{2},\theta_{2}+\frac{\pi}{2},q_{1},q_{2}),\text{ if }\theta_{2}\in[0,3\pi/2),\,\theta_{1}\in[3\pi/2,2\pi).\end{cases}

Proof.

It is worth noting that statement (i) in the case of
\sin\theta_{1}-q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}\neq 0
is proved in
[
7
, Section 3]
, while statement (iii) concerning the function
\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;0,0,q_{1},q_{2})
is verified in
[
5
, Proposition 3.1]
. It is apparent that the proof of statement (i) is a simple consequence of Lemma
5.1
and Corollary
5.1
. As for statements (ii) and (iii), they are examined with the arguments exploiting in the proof of claim (i). Coming statements (iv) and (v), they are follows from properties of the functions
\sin z,

\cos z
and standard calculations.

Thus, in order to prove Proposition
5.2
, it is enough to verify statements in the point (i). First, we analyze the case of
\sin\theta_{1}-q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}\neq 0
and

\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(0,\pi)
. By virtue of (i) in Corollary
5.1
, we easily conclude that
\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})
is the function
\mathcal{S}(z)
of
FFK
(see Lemma
5.1
). Hence, taking into account Corollary
5.1
and the decomposition of
\mathcal{S}(z)
in Lemma
5.1
, we obtain the equality

\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=A_{0}\exp\{A_{1}z+P_{1}(z)\}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}-z]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}-z][z-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-}]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}(\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-})}

(5.9)

with

P_{1}(z)=z\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\sum_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\Big(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}}+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-}}\Big),

and unknown coefficients
A_{0}
and
A_{1}
which will be identified below. In order to compute the series in
P_{1}(z),
we employ the arguments applying in calculation of
A_{1}^{\pm}
(see (
5.1
)) in the proof of Proposition
5.1
. Thus, we have

P_{1}(z)=z\sum_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{2\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}{\mathbb{T}_{q}^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathbb{T}_{q}^{2}}{n^{2}\mathbb{T}_{q}^{2}-(\mathfrak{z}^{-}_{i})^{2}}=-z\sum_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\Big[\frac{1}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}-\frac{\pi\cot(\pi\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}/\mathbb{T}_{q})}{\mathbb{T}_{q}}\Big].

As a result, we are left to check that the relations hold

A_{0}=-[\sin\theta_{1}-q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}],\quad A_{1}=\sum_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\Big[\frac{1}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}-\frac{\pi\cot(\pi\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}/\mathbb{T}_{q})}{\mathbb{T}_{q}}\Big].

(5.10)

To this end, we recast the arguments from the proof of Proposition
5.1
which provides the explicit values of
A_{0}^{\pm}
and
A_{1}^{\pm}

(see (
5.5
) and (
5.7
)).

\bullet
Indeed, substituting
z=0
to (
5.9
) and taking into account the inequality
\sin\theta_{1}-q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}\neq 0
, we end up with the first equality in (
5.10
).

\bullet
Coming to the equality for finding
A_{1}
, let us first write (by means of (i) in Proposition
5.1
)

\frac{[z-\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}-z][z-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-}]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}(-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-})}=\frac{\sin\frac{z-\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}{4q}}{\sin\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}{4q}},\quad i\in\{0,1,2,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1\}.

Then, simple tedious calculations entail

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{-}(2\mathbb{T}_{q};\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=-[\sin\theta_{1}-q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}]=\mathcal{S}^{-}(4\mathbb{T}_{q};\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2}),

\displaystyle\frac{[z-\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}-z][z-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-}]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}(-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-})}\Big|_{z=2\mathbb{T}_{q}}=-1=\frac{[z-\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}-z][z-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-}]}{\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{-}(-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{-})}\Big|_{z=4\mathbb{T}_{q}}.

Finally, substituting
z=2\mathbb{T}_{q}
and
z=4\mathbb{T}_{q}
to (
5.9
) and using relations above, we arrive at the system

\begin{cases}(-1)^{2{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}}\exp\Big\{2\mathbb{T}_{q}\Big(A_{1}-\sum_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\Big[\frac{1}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}-\frac{\pi\cot\frac{\pi\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}{\mathbb{T}_{q}}}{\mathbb{T}_{q}}\Big]\Big)\Big\}=1,\\
(-1)^{2{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}}\exp\Big\{4\mathbb{T}_{q}\Big(A_{1}-\sum_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\Big[\frac{1}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}-\frac{\pi\cot\frac{\pi\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}{\mathbb{T}_{q}}}{\mathbb{T}_{q}}\Big]\Big)\Big\}=1.\end{cases}

In conclusion, solving this system, we reach the second equality in (
5.10
).

Now, we treat the function
\mathcal{S}^{-}
if
\sin\theta_{1}-q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}=0
. In this case, Corollary
5.1
tells us that

\mathcal{S}^{-}(0;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=0.

Then, Lemma
5.1
provides the relation

\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})=B_{0}\exp\{B_{1}z+P_{1}(z)\}z^{\mu_{1}^{-}}\prod\limits_{i=\mu_{1}^{-}+1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}-z)}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{-}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\prod\limits_{j=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{j,n}^{-}-z][z-\mathfrak{z}_{j,-n}^{-}]}{\mathfrak{z}_{j,n}^{-}(-\mathfrak{z}_{j,-n}^{-})},

where
B_{0}
and
B_{1}
are unknown coefficients which will be specified below.
To this end, we recast the procedure leading to identification of
A_{0}
and
A_{1}
and obtain the desired equalities:
B_{1}=A_{1},
and
B_{0}=(-1)^{\lfloor\mu_{1}^{-}/2\rfloor}[\cos\theta_{1}-q_{2}q_{1}^{\mu_{1}^{-}}\cos\theta_{2}].

This completes the proof of Proposition
5.2
.
∎

Our next results are related to factorization of the function
\mathcal{S}(z)
in the
FTK
case. First, we need to introduce the following quantities depending on
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in[0,2\pi),
and real positive number
q_{3}
:

\displaystyle q_{2}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1\mp q_{3}}{1\pm q_{3}},\qquad\text{if }0<q_{3}<1,\\
-\frac{1\mp q_{3}}{1\pm q_{3}},\quad\text{ if }1<q_{3},\end{cases}\quad\theta_{2}^{\star}=\begin{cases}\theta_{2}+\theta_{1},\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{ if }0\leq\theta_{2}+\theta_{1}<2\pi,\\
\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}-2\pi\lfloor\frac{\theta_{2}+\theta_{1}}{2\pi}\rfloor,\quad\text{if }\theta_{2}+\theta_{1}\geq 2\pi,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\theta_{+}^{\star}(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\theta_{+}(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}),\qquad\quad\text{if }\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}\in[0,2\pi),\\
\theta_{+}(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}-2\pi),\quad\text{if }\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}\in[2\pi,4\pi)\end{cases}

(as for the definition of
\theta_{+}
see (
5.5
)).

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
0<\omega_{1}\leq\omega_{2}
, and
q_{3}>0
, and let

0\leq\theta_{1}\leq\theta_{2}<2\pi
.
Then the following factorizations hold for functions
\tan(\omega_{1}z-\theta_{1})\pm q_{3}\tan(\omega_{2}z-\theta_{2})
.

i:

If
q_{3}=1
then

\displaystyle\tan(\omega_{1}z-\theta_{1})-\tan(\omega_{2}z-\theta_{2})=-\frac{(-1)^{\lfloor\frac{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}{\pi}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{2\theta_{2}+\pi}{2\pi}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{2\theta_{1}+\pi}{2\pi}\rfloor}(\omega_{2}-\omega_{1})\sin\theta_{+}^{\star}(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})}{\theta_{+}(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})\cos\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})\cos\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}

\displaystyle\times\Big(z-\frac{\theta_{+}(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})}{\omega_{2}-\omega_{1}}\Big)\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\bigg(\frac{\pi n+\theta_{+}(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})}{\omega_{2}-\omega_{1}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi n-\theta_{+}(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})}{\omega_{2}-\omega_{1}}+z\bigg)}{\bigg(\frac{\pi n+\theta_{+}(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})}{\omega_{2}-\omega_{1}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi n-\theta_{+}(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})}{\omega_{2}-\omega_{1}}\bigg)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}\bigg)}{\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}+z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}+z\bigg)},

\displaystyle\tan(\omega_{1}z-\theta_{1})+\tan(\omega_{2}z-\theta_{2})=\frac{(-1)^{\lfloor\frac{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}{\pi}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{2\theta_{2}+\pi}{2\pi}\rfloor-\lfloor\frac{2\theta_{1}+\pi}{2\pi}\rfloor}(\omega_{2}+\omega_{1})\sin\theta^{\star}_{+}(\theta_{2}+\theta_{1})}{\theta_{+}^{\star}(\theta_{2}+\theta_{1})\cos\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})\cos\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}

\displaystyle\times\Big(z+\frac{\theta^{\star}_{+}(\theta_{2}+\theta_{1})}{\omega_{2}+\omega_{1}}\Big)\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\bigg(\frac{\pi n+\theta^{\star}_{+}(\theta_{2}+\theta_{1})}{\omega_{2}+\omega_{1}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi n-\theta^{\star}_{+}(\theta_{2}+\theta_{1})}{\omega_{2}+\omega_{1}}+z\bigg)}{\bigg(\frac{\pi n+\theta^{\star}_{+}(\theta_{2}+\theta_{1})}{\omega_{2}+\omega_{1}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi n-\theta^{\star}_{+}(\theta_{2}+\theta_{1})}{\omega_{2}+\omega_{1}}\bigg)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}\bigg)}{\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}+z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}+z\bigg)}.

ii:

If
q_{3}\neq 1
and, in addition,
\omega_{2}=\frac{1+q_{1}}{q_{1}-1}\omega_{1}
, where
q_{1}
meets the requirements of Corollary
5.1
, then in the case of
q_{3}\in(0,1)
the factorization holds

\displaystyle\tan(\omega_{1}z-\theta_{1})\pm q_{3}\tan(\omega_{2}z-\theta_{2})=-\frac{(1\pm q_{3})\mathcal{S}^{-}((\omega_{2}-\omega_{1})z;\theta_{2}-\theta_{1},\theta_{2}^{\star},\frac{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}}{\omega_{2}-\omega_{1}},q_{2}^{\pm})}{2(-1)^{\lfloor\frac{2\theta_{2}+\pi}{2\pi}\rfloor+\lfloor\frac{2\theta_{1}+\pi}{2\pi}\rfloor}\cos\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})\cos\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}

\displaystyle\times\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}\bigg)}{\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}+z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}+z\bigg)},

while in the case of
q_{3}>1
, there is the decomposition

\displaystyle\tan(\omega_{1}z-\theta_{1})\pm q_{3}\tan(\omega_{2}z-\theta_{2})=-\frac{(1\pm q_{3})\mathcal{S}^{+}((\omega_{2}-\omega_{1})z;\theta_{2}-\theta_{1},\theta_{2}^{\star},\frac{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}}{\omega_{2}-\omega_{1}},q_{2}^{\pm})}{2(-1)^{\lfloor\frac{2\theta_{2}+\pi}{2\pi}\rfloor+\lfloor\frac{2\theta_{1}+\pi}{2\pi}\rfloor}\cos\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})\cos\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}

\displaystyle\times\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}\bigg)}{\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{1})}{2\omega_{1}}+z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)+2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}-z\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\pi(2n-1)-2\theta_{-}(\theta_{2})}{2\omega_{2}}+z\bigg)}.

iii:

If, in addition, assumptions of Corollary
5.1
hold, and
\bar{\theta}_{1}
,
\bar{\theta}_{2}\in[0,2\pi)
,
q_{1}^{\star}=\frac{p^{\star}}{q^{\star}}
, where
p^{\star},

q^{\star}\in\mathbb{N}
,
p^{\star}>2q^{\star}>1,
and
q^{\star}/p^{\star}
being irreducible fraction and
q_{2}^{\star}>0,

q_{2}^{\star}\neq 1.
Then

\displaystyle\frac{\sin(z-\theta_{1})-q_{2}\sin(q_{1}z-\theta_{2})}{\sin(z-\theta^{\star}_{1})\mp q^{\star}_{2}\sin(q^{\star}_{1}z-\theta^{\star}_{2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})}{\mathcal{S}^{\mp}(z;\theta^{\star}_{1},\theta^{\star}_{2},q^{\star}_{1},q^{\star}_{2})},

\displaystyle\frac{\sin(z-\theta_{1})+q_{2}\sin(q_{1}z-\theta_{2})}{\sin(z-\theta^{\star}_{1})\mp q^{\star}_{2}\sin(q^{\star}_{1}z-\theta^{\star}_{2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})}{\mathcal{S}^{\mp}(z;\theta^{\star}_{1},\theta^{\star}_{2},q^{\star}_{1},q^{\star}_{2})},

where factorizations of
\mathcal{S}^{\mp}(z;\theta^{\star}_{1},\theta^{\star}_{2},q^{\star}_{1},q^{\star}_{2})
and
\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})
are given in Proposition
5.2
.

Proof.

First of all, we remark that statement (i) in the case of
\omega_{1}=0
and
\theta_{1}\in[0,\pi/2]
is proved in
[
27
, §5]
and
[
6
, §3]
. It is apparent that the statement (iii) is a simple consequence of Proposition
5.2
and the definition of functions
\mathcal{S}^{\pm}
. Concerning the proof of statements in (i) and (ii), it follows immediately from Propositions
5.1
and
5.2
and the easily verified identities below

\displaystyle\tan(\omega_{1}z-\theta_{1})\mp\tan(\omega_{2}z-\theta_{2})=\mp\frac{\sin[(\omega_{2}\mp\omega_{1})z\mp(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})]}{\cos(\omega_{1}z-\theta_{1})\cos(\omega_{2}z-\theta_{2})},

\displaystyle\tan(\omega_{1}z-\theta_{1})\pm q_{3}\tan(\omega_{2}z-\theta_{2})=-\frac{(1\pm q_{3})}{2\cos(\omega_{1}z-\theta_{1})\cos(\omega_{2}z-\theta_{2})}

\displaystyle\times\begin{cases}\Big[\sin(y-\theta_{2}+\theta_{1})-q_{2}^{+}\sin\Big(\frac{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}}{\omega_{2}-\omega_{1}}y-\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\Big)\Big]\Big|_{y=(\omega_{2}-\omega_{1})z},\quad\text{if }q_{3}\in(0,1),\\
\Big[\sin(y-\theta_{2}+\theta_{1})+q_{2}^{-}\sin\Big(\frac{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}}{\omega_{2}-\omega_{1}}y-\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\Big)\Big]\Big|_{y=(\omega_{2}-\omega_{1})z},\quad\text{if }q_{3}>1.\end{cases}

That completes the proof of this claim.
∎

To test the results proposed in Corollary
5.1
and Proposition
5.2
, the example of
\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})
is examined here below, where, in particular, the zeros of
\mathcal{S}^{+}
are found in the analytical form.

Example 5.1
.

Consider the function
\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},2\theta_{1},2,q_{2})=\sin(z-\theta_{1})+q_{2}\sin 2(z-\theta_{1})
with
\theta_{2}=2\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{1}\in[\pi/4,\pi/3]
,
q=1
,
p=4
(i.e.
q_{1}=2
).

In this example, it is easy to verify that

\mathbb{T}_{q}=4\pi
and
\sin\theta_{1}+q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}\neq 0,

for
\theta_{1}\in[\pi/4,\pi/3]
and
\theta_{2}\in[\pi/2,2\pi/3]
.
Performing the simple calculations, we arrive at the explicit form of the zeros in the segment
[0,4\pi)
which are listed in
Table
1
. Besides, all zeros of
\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},2\theta_{1},2,q_{2})
are given by

\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+}=\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}+4\pi n,\quad\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{+}=\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-4\pi n,\quad n\in\mathbb{N},\,i=0,1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1.

, then

,

\displaystyle=[\theta_{1}-\arcsin q_{2},\theta_{1}+\arcsin q_{2}]

\displaystyle\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}\in\mathfrak{S}(1,\theta_{1},q_{2})

\displaystyle=[2\pi+\theta_{1}-\arcsin q_{2},2\pi+\theta_{1}+\arcsin q_{2}]

\displaystyle\cup[3\pi+\theta_{1}-\arcsin q_{2},3\pi+\theta_{1}+\arcsin q_{2}],\quad i\in\{4,5,6,7\};

\bullet
if

, then there are 3-multiple roots of
\mathcal{S}^{+}
and

\mathfrak{z}_{0}^{+},\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}=\mathfrak{z}_{2}^{+}=\mathfrak{z}_{3}^{+}\in\mathfrak{S}(0,\theta_{1},1/2)
and
\mathfrak{z}_{4}^{+},\mathfrak{z}_{5}^{+}=\mathfrak{z}_{6}^{+}=\mathfrak{z}_{7}^{+}\in\mathfrak{S}(1,\theta_{1},1/2);

\bullet
if

, then all zeroes are simple and

\mathfrak{z}_{0}^{+},\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}\in\mathfrak{S}(0,\theta_{1},q_{2})
and
\mathfrak{z}_{2}^{+},\mathfrak{z}_{3}^{+}\in\mathfrak{S}(1,\theta_{1},q_{2}).

Summarizing, we conclude that the outcomes in Example
5.1
are fitting to Corollary
5.1
. Finally, statement (ii) in Proposition
5.2
provides the following factorization of the function
\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},2\theta_{1},2,q_{2})
:

\displaystyle\sin(z-\theta_{1})+q_{2}\sin 2(z-\theta_{1})

\displaystyle=-[\sin\theta_{1}+q_{2}\sin 2\theta_{1}]

\displaystyle\times\begin{cases}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{7}\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-z}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{7}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}+4\pi n-z][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}+z]}{[\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}+4\pi n][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}]},\,\text{ if }q_{2}>1/2,\\
\prod\limits_{i=0}^{3}\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-z}{\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{3}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}+4\pi n-z][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}+z]}{[\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}+4\pi n][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}]},\,\text{ if }0<q_{2}<1/2,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\sin(z-\theta_{1})+\frac{1}{2}\sin 2(z-\theta_{1})=-\Big[\sin\theta_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\sin 2\theta_{1}\Big]\frac{(\mathfrak{z}_{0}^{+}-z)}{\mathfrak{z}_{0}^{+}}\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}-z}{\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}}\Big)^{3}\frac{(\mathfrak{z}_{4}^{+}-z)}{\mathfrak{z}_{4}^{+}}\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{5}^{+}-z}{\mathfrak{z}_{5}^{+}}\Big)^{3}

\displaystyle\times\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{0}^{+}+4\pi n-z][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{0}^{+}+z]}{[\mathfrak{z}_{0}^{+}+4\pi n][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{0}^{+}]}\Big(\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}+4\pi n-z][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}+z]}{[\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}+4\pi n][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}]}\Big)^{3}

\displaystyle\times\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{4}^{+}+4\pi n-z][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{4}^{+}+z]}{[\mathfrak{z}_{4}^{+}+4\pi n][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{4}^{+}]}\Big(\frac{[\mathfrak{z}_{5}^{+}+4\pi n-z][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{5}^{+}+z]}{[\mathfrak{z}_{5}^{+}+4\pi n][4\pi n-\mathfrak{z}_{5}^{+}]}\Big)^{3}.

5.2.
Solvability of (
5.1
)

In this section, assuming
H8-H10
and appealing to Theorems
2.1
-
2.2
,
4.1
, we will construct a solution of equation (
5.1
). Indeed, assumptions
H8-H10
provide the validity of Lemma
5.1
, which in turn means that the function
\mathcal{S}(z)
has the form similar to
\Omega(z)
. To conclude this fact, it is enough to compare representation (
5.2
) with (
1.2
). Thus, we can apply Theorem
2.1
or
4.1
in the case of
\mathbb{F}\equiv 0
and Theorem
2.2
or
4.1
for
\mathbb{F}\neq 0
to equation (
5.1
).

First, we make additional assumptions.

H11:
If
\mathcal{S}(z)=\mathcal{S}_{1}(z),
then we require

Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{z}_{-n}}{\beta}\Big)\neq-m,\quad\text{if}\quad\beta>0,\quad\text{and}\quad Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{z}_{n}}{|\beta|}\Big)\neq-m,\quad\text{if}\quad\beta<0,

(5.11)

for
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\},
and
n\in\{1,2,...,N_{1}\}
if
\mathcal{S}_{1}
has the finite number of zeros, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
otherwise.
In addition, if
\mu_{1}\neq 0
, we assume that the inequality holds:

Re(z/\beta)\neq-m.

In the case of
\mathcal{S}(z)=1/\mathcal{S}_{2}(z)
, we require

Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{y}_{n}}{\beta}\Big)\neq 1+m,\quad\text{if}\quad\beta>0,\quad\text{and}\quad Re\,\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{y}_{-n}}{|\beta|}\Big)\neq-1-m,\quad\text{if}\quad\beta<0,

(5.12)

for
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\},
and
n\in\{1,2,...,N_{3}\}
if
\mathcal{S}_{2}
has the finite number of zeros, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
otherwise.

If
\mathcal{S}(z)=\mathcal{S}_{1}(z)/\mathcal{S}_{2}(z)
, then inequalities (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) hold simultaneously.

At this point, for simplicity consideration, we put

A_{0}^{I}\exp\{g_{1}(z)+P_{1}(z)\}:=A_{0}^{I}\exp\{B_{1}^{I}(z)\},\quad A_{0}^{II}\exp\{-g_{2}(z)-P_{2}(z)\}:=A_{0}^{II}\exp\{B_{1}^{II}(z)\},

and then introduce the functions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{I}(n)

\displaystyle=sgn\,(\beta)\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{-n}}{|\beta|}\Big[\ln\Big(-\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{-n}}{|\beta|}-1\Big)\Big]+\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{n}}{|\beta|}\Big[\ln\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{n}}{|\beta|}-1\Big)\Big]\Big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{II}(n)

\displaystyle=-sgn\,(\beta)\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{y}_{-n}}{|\beta|}\Big[\ln\Big(-\frac{\mathfrak{y}_{-n}}{|\beta|}-1\Big)\Big]+\frac{\mathfrak{y}_{n}}{|\beta|}\Big[\ln\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{y}_{n}}{|\beta|}-1\Big)\Big]\Big),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{I}(z)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{\prod\limits_{n=1}^{N_{2}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{z}_{-n}}{\beta}\Big)\Big(\Gamma(z/\beta)\Big)^{\mu_{1}}}{\prod\limits_{n=1}^{N_{1}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{n}-z}{\beta}+1\Big)}\exp\{i\pi z(N_{1}+1)\},\quad\text{if }\mathcal{S}_{1}\text{ has a finite number of zeros},\\
\Big(\Gamma(z/\beta)\Big)^{\mu_{1}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\Gamma\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{z}_{-n}}{\beta}\Big)}{\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{n}-z}{\beta}+1\Big)}\Big[\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{n}(-\mathfrak{z}_{-n})}{\beta^{2}}\Big]^{\frac{\beta-2z}{2\beta}}\exp\{\mathcal{R}^{I}\},\quad\text{if }\mathcal{S}_{1}\text{ has an infinite number }\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{of zeros and }\beta>0,\\
\Big(\Gamma(z/\beta)\Big)^{\mu_{1}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{n}-z}{|\beta|}\Big)}{\Gamma\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{z}_{-n}}{|\beta|}+1\Big)}\Big[\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{n}(-\mathfrak{z}_{-n})}{\beta^{2}}\Big]^{\frac{\beta-2z}{2\beta}}\exp\{\mathcal{R}^{I}\},\quad\text{if }\mathcal{S}_{1}\text{ has an infinite number}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\text{ of zeros and }\beta<0,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{L}^{II}(z)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\Big(\Gamma\big(\frac{z}{\beta}\big)\Big)^{-\mu_{2}}\frac{\prod\limits_{n=1}^{N_{3}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{y}_{n}-z}{\beta}+1\Big)}{\prod\limits_{n=1}^{N_{4}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{y}_{-n}}{\beta}\Big)}\exp\{i\pi z(N_{3}+1)\},\quad\text{if }\mathcal{S}_{2}\text{ has a finite number of zeros},\\
\Big(\Gamma(z/\beta)\Big)^{-\mu_{2}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{y}_{n}-z}{\beta}+1\Big)}{\Gamma\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{y}_{-n}}{\beta}\Big)}\Big[\frac{\mathfrak{y}_{n}(-\mathfrak{y}_{-n})}{\beta^{2}}\Big]^{\frac{2z-\beta}{2\beta}}\exp\{\mathcal{R}^{II}\},\quad\text{if }\mathcal{S}_{2}\text{ has an infinite number }\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\text{ of zeros and }\beta>0,\\
\Big(\Gamma(z/\beta)\Big)^{-\mu_{2}}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\Gamma\Big(1+\frac{z-\mathfrak{y}_{-n}}{|\beta|}\Big)}{\Gamma\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{y}_{n}-z}{|\beta|}\Big)}\Big[\frac{\mathfrak{y}_{n}(-\mathfrak{y}_{-n})}{\beta^{2}}\Big]^{\frac{2z-\beta}{2\beta}}\exp\{\mathcal{R}^{II}\},\quad\text{if }\mathcal{S}_{1}\text{ has an infinite number }\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{ of zeros and }\beta<0.\end{cases}

Finally, denoting

\mathfrak{d}_{0}^{\star}=\begin{cases}A_{0}^{I}\beta^{-\mu_{1}}\quad\text{in the \bf{FFK} case},\\
\,\\
\frac{\beta^{\mu_{2}}}{A_{0}^{II}}\quad\text{in the \bf{FSK} case},\\
\,\\
\frac{\beta^{\mu_{2}-\mu_{1}}A_{0}^{I}}{A_{0}^{II}}\quad\text{in the \bf{FTK} case},\end{cases}\quad B^{\star}=\begin{cases}B_{1}^{I}\quad\quad\text{in the \bf{FFK} case},\\
\,\\
B_{1}^{II}\quad\quad\text{in the \bf{FSK} case},\\
\,\\
B_{1}^{I}-B_{2}^{II}\quad\text{in the \bf{FTK} case},\end{cases}

\mathbb{L}^{\star}(z)=\begin{cases}\mathbb{L}^{I}\quad\quad\text{in the \bf{FFK} case},\\
\,\\
\mathbb{L}^{II}\quad\quad\text{in the \bf{FSK} case},\\
\,\\
\frac{\mathbb{L}^{I}}{\mathbb{L}^{II}}\quad\text{in the \bf{FTK} case},\end{cases}

and appealing to Theorem
2.1
, we end up with the assertion.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let assumptions
H1
and
H8-H10
hold,
\mathbb{F}\equiv 0
. Then a general solution

of homogenous equation (

) is given by

Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})=\exp\Big\{\frac{B^{\star}}{2\beta}z^{2}-\frac{B^{\star}}{2}z\Big\}(\mathfrak{d}_{0}^{\star})^{\frac{2z-\beta}{2\beta}}{\mathbb{P}}(z/\beta)(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma)^{\frac{\beta-2z}{2\beta}}\mathbb{L}^{\star}(z),

(5.13)

where
{\mathbb{P}}(z)
is a function describing in Theorem
2.1
.

Moreover, statements
(s.1), (s.2)
of Theorem
2.1
with
\mathbb{L}(z):=\mathbb{L}^{\star}(z)
and

\mathbb{L}_{1}(z):=\mathbb{L}^{\star}(z)
hold.

Coming to inhomogeneous equation (
5.1
), we state additional hypotheses.

H12:
For
d_{0}\in[0,1]
and
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\},
we assume

Re\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{z}_{n}}{\beta}\Big)\neq d_{0}+m,\quad n\in\{1,2,...,N_{1}\},

in the case of a finite number of zeros at
\mathcal{S}_{1}
. Otherwise, we require that these inequalities hold with

if

, while in the case of

In the case of a finite number of zeros at
\mathcal{S}_{2}
, we require that

Re(z/\beta)\neq-1-m+d_{0}\quad\text{if}\quad\mu_{2}\neq 0;\quad\text{and }Re\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{y}_{-n}}{\beta}\Big)\neq-m-1+d_{0},\quad n\in\{1,2,...,N_{4}\}.

Otherwise, we require that these relations hold with
n\in\mathbb{N}
if
\beta>0
, while in the case of
\beta<0
the inequalities are fulfilled

Re(z/\beta)\neq-1-m+d_{0}\quad\text{if}\quad\mu_{2}\neq 0;\quad\text{and }Re\Big(\frac{z-\mathfrak{y}_{n}}{|\beta|}\Big)\neq m+1-d_{0},\quad n\in\mathbb{N}.

In the case of the function
\mathcal{S}_{1}/\mathcal{S}_{2}
, we assume that all inequalities hold simultaneously.

To make the following assumption, we set
{\mathbb{P}}_{1}:={\mathbb{P}}(z/\beta)
for each chosen periodic function and

Y_{h}(z,\sigma)=Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1}).

H13
We assume that
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
is an analytic function for
z\in{\mathbb{C}}
and each
\sigma
satisfying
H1
. Moreover, for some functions
{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(z/\beta)
and
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)
, the equality holds

\underset{|Im\xi|\to+\infty}{\lim}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{K}(\xi)\mathbb{F}(z+\beta\xi,\sigma)}{Y_{h}(z+\beta\xi+\beta,\sigma)}\Big|=0.

(5.14)

for all
z
satisfying
H.11
and

and each
Re\xi\in[-1,0]
.

Now, taking into account Theorems

,
4.1
and
5.1
we conclude.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let assumptions of Theorem
5.1
and hypotheses

hold. Then a general solution of (

) has a form

Y_{g}(z,\sigma)=Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})+Y_{ih}(z,\sigma),

where

is defined in Theorem
5.1
and

being a particular solution is given by

Y_{ih}(z,\sigma)=\frac{Y_{h}(z,\sigma)}{\mathcal{K}_{1}(0)}\int\limits_{\ell_{d_{0}}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\beta\xi,\sigma)\mathcal{K}(\xi)}{(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})Y_{h}(z+\beta\xi+\beta,\sigma)}d\xi.

(5.15)

Finally, to demonstrate results of Theorems
5.1
-
5.2
, we conclude this section with examples of explicit solutions to (

) with the coefficient
\mathcal{S}
presented in Propositions
5.1
-
5.3
. In light of formulas (

B^{\star}
,

and the kernel

. As for

We begin with consideration of the homogenous case of (

\mathbb{L}^{\star}(z)
and
\mathfrak{d}_{0}^{\star}
for the rest coefficients
\mathcal{S}(z)
described in Propositions
5.1
-
5.3
.

Summarizing, in order to obtain an explicit solution of homogenous equation (
5.1
) in the case of the coefficient
\mathcal{S}
listed in Table
3
, we are left to substitute the corresponding values of
\mathbb{L}^{\star}(z)
and
\mathfrak{d}_{0}^{\star}
from Tables

3
-
2
to expression (
5.13
) with
B^{\star}=0
.

Remark 5.1
.

Appealing to the well-known relations:

\sinh(q_{0}z)=-i\sin(iq_{0}z),

\cosh(q_{0}z)=\cos(iq_{0}z),

and performing the inessential modifications in the arguments above, we obtain the very same results for homogenous equation (
5.1
) with
\mathcal{S}(z)=\sinh(q_{0}z),

\cosh(q_{0}z)
,
\tanh(q_{0}z)
,
\coth(q_{0}z)
and their linear expressions.

At this point, we analyze inhomogeneous equation (
5.1
) with

\mathcal{S}(z)=\frac{\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})}{\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;0,0,q_{1},q_{2}^{\star})}
and demonstrate particular solution (
5.15
) with different kinds of
{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(z)
.

Example 5.2
.

We consider (
5.1
) with
\mathcal{S}(z)=\frac{\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{1},q_{2})}{\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;0,0,q_{1},q_{2}^{\star})}
where
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in(0,\pi)
and
\sin\theta_{1}-q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}\neq 0.
In this example, we analyze three kinds of
{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(z)
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{I}

\displaystyle:={\mathbb{P}}_{1}(z)=1,\quad{\mathbb{P}}_{II}:={\mathbb{P}}_{1}(z)=\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1}\sin\pi(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}-z+1)\exp\{i\pi(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}-z+1)\},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{III}

\displaystyle:={\mathbb{P}}_{1}(z)=\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1}\sin\pi(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}-z+1)\exp\{i\pi(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}-z+1)\}}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\sin\pi(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-z+1)\exp\{i\pi(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-z+1)+i\pi z\}\sin\pi z}

and two types of
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
. Namely, the main difference of these kinds is related with the behavior of
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
as
|Imz|
tends to the infinite for each fixed
Rez
and
\sigma\in{\mathbb{C}}
,
Re\sigma>0.

Indeed, we assume that
\mathbb{F}
does not have any poles for
z\in{\mathbb{C}}
and
\sigma\in{\mathbb{C}}
with
Re\sigma>0
and

i:
either

\underset{|Imz|\to+\infty}{\lim}\,|F(z,\sigma)|=C,

(5.16)

ii:
or

\underset{|Imz|\to+\infty}{\lim}\,e^{c^{\star}\pi|Imz|}|F(z,\sigma)|=C,

(5.17)

with
c^{\star}=\max\{4,2{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}\}
.

It is apparent that the functions
\mathbb{F}
considered in this example meet the requirements of Theorem
5.2
. It is worth noting that, assumptions
H12-H13
tell us that if for each
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
the relations hold

Rez\neq m+\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{\star,+}+1,\,i\in\{0,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1\},\,n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\};\,Rez\neq\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{+}-m,\,i\in\{0,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1\},\,n\in\mathbb{N},

(5.18)

then the function
\frac{Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})}{{\mathbb{P}}(z,\sigma)}
has no poles, while
\frac{{\mathbb{P}}(z+1+\xi,\sigma)}{Y_{h}(z+1+\xi,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})}
has no poles if for
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
the system is fulfilled

\begin{cases}Rez\neq m+d_{0}+\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+},\quad i\in\{0,1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1\},\,n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\},\\
Rez\neq-m-1+d_{0},\\
Rez\neq-m+d_{0}-1-\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{\star,+},\quad i\in\{0,1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1\},\,n\in\mathbb{N},\\
Rez\neq-m+d_{0}-\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+},\quad i\in\{1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1\}.\\
\end{cases}

(5.19)

Nevertheless, the function
{\mathbb{P}}(z)
of the second and the third kinds permit to relax conditions (
5.18
) and (
5.19
). Indeed, in the case of
{\mathbb{P}}:={\mathbb{P}}_{II}
, this function is chosen so as to eliminate the poles of the functions
\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}-z+1),

i\in\{1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1\}
, which means the first inequality in (
5.18
) should be satisfied only for
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus, we can extend the domain of analyticity to the homogenous solution
Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{II})
. However, the such choice of
{\mathbb{P}}(z)
provides additional simple nulls to
Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{II})
or in turn, additional poles of
\frac{1}{Y_{h}(z+1+\xi,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{II})}
. To avoid this problem, we are left to require that
Rez\neq\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}+d_{0}-m,\quad m\in\mathbb{N}.

Coming to the function
{\mathbb{P}}_{III}(z)
, clearly, the function
\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{III}(z)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1}\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}-z+1)}{\Gamma(z)\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-z+1)}

does not have any poles if

Rez\neq\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-m+1,\quad i\in\{0,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1\},\quad\text{and}\quad Rez\neq m,

while the function

\frac{\Gamma(z+\xi+1)\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-z-\xi)}{{\mathbb{P}}_{III}(z+1+\xi)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1}\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}-z-\xi)}

does not have any poles if

Rez\neq-m+\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}+d_{0},\quad i\in\{1,2,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1\}.

Thus, this choice of
{\mathbb{P}}(z)
allows us to eliminate the poles of
\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}-z+1)
,
i\in\{1,2,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1\}
in the case of the function
Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{III})
and to remove the poles of
\Gamma(z+\xi+1)
and
\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-z-\xi),

i\in\{0,1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1\}
in the case of the function
\frac{1}{Y_{h}(z+1+\xi,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{III})}
.

In summary, to construct the solution of inhomogeneous equation (
5.1
), we are left to select the suitable kernel
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)
such that equality (
5.14
) holds. Appealing to Theorem
5.1
and asymptotic (
2.5
) and performing technical calculations, we end up with inequalities:

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{e^{-(\varepsilon+\theta_{2})|Im\,z|}}{Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{I})}\Big|\leq C\begin{cases}e^{\frac{3\pi}{2}Im\,z},\,Im\,z\to+\infty,\\
e^{\frac{\pi}{2}Im\,z},\,Im\,z\to-\infty,\end{cases}\,\Big|\frac{e^{-(\varepsilon+\theta_{2})|Im\,z|}}{Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{II})}\Big|\leq C\begin{cases}e^{\frac{3\pi}{2}Im\,z},\qquad\quad\quad\,Im\,z\to+\infty,\\
e^{-2\pi[{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-\frac{5}{4}]Im\,z},\,Im\,z\to-\infty,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{e^{-(\varepsilon+\theta_{2})|Im\,z|}}{Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{III})}\Big|\leq C\begin{cases}e^{(\frac{11\pi}{2}-2\pi{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+})Im\,z},\,Im\,z\to+\infty,\\
e^{-\frac{3\pi}{2}Im\,z},\qquad\qquad Im\,z\to-\infty,\end{cases}

where
\varepsilon>0
is enough small value, e.g.
\varepsilon<<\frac{\pi}{100}
. Thus, taking into account these relations and behavior of the function
|\mathbb{F}|
as
|Imz|\to+\infty
(see (
5.16
) and (
5.17
)), we arrive at
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi):=\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi;{\mathbb{P}}_{j})
,

j=I,II,III
listed in Tables
5
-
4
.
It is apparent that, decay of
|\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)|
as
|Imz|\to+\infty
permits to relax assumption on
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi,{\mathbb{P}}_{j}),

j=I,II,III
.

Table 4.
The value of
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)
in the case of
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)
satisfying (
5.17
)

in the case of

satisfying (
5.16
)

if
\pi(\frac{5}{2}-2{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+})-\theta_{2}-\varepsilon>0

if
\pi(\frac{5}{2}-2{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+})-\theta_{2}-\varepsilon<0
