Finally, collecting the results above with Theorems
5.1
-
5.2
, we derive the general solution to (
5.1
) in the form

Y(z,\sigma)=Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})+Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{j})\int\limits_{\ell_{d_{0}}}\frac{\mathbb{F}(z+\xi,\sigma)[\cot\pi\xi+i]\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi,{\mathbb{P}}_{j})}{[a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu}]Y_{h}(z+\xi+1,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{j})}d\xi,\,j=I,II,III,

(5.20)

where
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi;{\mathbb{P}}_{j})
are given in Tables
4
-
5
, and

\displaystyle Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{(\sin\theta_{1}+q_{2}\sin\theta_{2})\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1}(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+})^{2}}{(1+q_{2}^{\star}q_{1})\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}}\bigg]^{z-1/2}\frac{e^{i\pi z}(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})^{\frac{1-2z}{2}}}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\Gamma(z)\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{+}-z+1)}

\displaystyle\times\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1}\frac{\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}-z+1)}{\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i}^{\star,+}+z)}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\frac{\Gamma(z-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{+})}{\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+}-z+1)}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1}\frac{\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{\star,+}-z+1)}{\Gamma(\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{\star,+}+z)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{\star,+}-1}(\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{\star,+})^{2z-1}}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}[\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+}(-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{+})]^{\frac{2z-1}{2}}}\exp\Big\{\sum\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}(\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{+}[\ln(-\mathfrak{z}_{i,-n}^{+})-1]+\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+}[\ln(\mathfrak{z}_{i,n}^{+})-1])\Big\}.

It is worth noting that, if we search a bounded solution of (
5.1
) if
|Imz|\to+\infty
, then we should put
{\mathbb{P}}\equiv 0
in

Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})
, which means
Y_{h}(z,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})=0
and (
5.20
) is rewritten as
Y(z,\sigma)=Y_{ih}(z,\sigma).

Remark 5.2
.

Clearly,
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)=1
meets requirements of Theorem
5.2
and satisfies equality (
5.16
), while
\mathbb{F}(z,\sigma)=\exp\{-c^{\star}(z\bar{z})\}
for each fixed
Rez
satisfies equality (
5.17
).

Remark 5.3
.

Observing arguments of Sections
3
-
5
, one can easily conclude that all results of Section
5
(possibly with minor modifications) can be extended to the functions
\mathcal{S}
presented as either
\mathcal{S}=\prod_{j=1}^{N_{I}}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{j}
or
\mathcal{S}=\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{N_{I}}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{j}}{\prod_{j=1}^{N_{II}}\mathcal{S}_{2}^{j}}
with fixed integer
N_{I}
and
N_{II}
and the functions
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{j}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}^{j}
having the properties of the functions
\mathcal{S}_{1}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}
, respectively.

Besides, in light of Remark
2.5
, the results of Theorems
5.1
and
5.2
hold for multidimensional equation similar to (
2.11
) with
\mathbf{S}(z)=\prod_{j=1}^{k}\mathcal{S}^{j}(z)
, where
\mathcal{S}^{j}(z)
obeys properties of the function
\mathcal{S}(z)
.

It is worth noting that, results obtained in Section
5
mean that
FDEs
studied in
[
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
11
,
22
,
23
,
27
]
(see also references therein) fall in our analysis.

6.
Explicit solutions of transmission boundary value problems with

a fractional dynamic boundary condition in plane corners

In this Section, we discuss a construction of a solution to a transmission problem (
1.5
)-(
1.9
) via results of Sections
2
and
5
. Other words, we demonstrate the application of solutions (
1.1
) to look for an explicit solution to the non-classical transmission boundary value problem.

We assume that
\omega_{0}=q\pi/p\in[0,\pi/2)
with
p
and
q
satisfying assumptions of Corollary
5.1
.
Coming to the coefficients involved,
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
meet requirements
H1
, and
a_{3}
,
a_{4},

\mathfrak{K}\neq 1
are positive quantities, and
\mathfrak{s}_{0}
is a given real number.

As for the right-hand sides in (
1.5
)-(
1.9
), we state the following hypothesis.

H14:
We require that for some fixed
\alpha\in(0,1),

f_{1},f\in\mathcal{C}([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{1+\alpha}(g)),\,\,f_{2}\in\mathcal{C}([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\partial G_{1}\backslash g)),\,\,f_{3}\in\mathcal{C}([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\partial G_{2}\backslash g)),

f_{0,1}\in\mathcal{C}([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\bar{G}_{1})),\,f_{0,2}\in\mathcal{C}([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(\bar{G}_{2})),

and

f,f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{0,1},f_{0,2}\equiv 0\quad\text{if either}\quad r\in[0,\varepsilon_{1}]\cup[R_{1},+\infty)\quad\text{or}\quad t\leq 0,

for some fixed small positive
\varepsilon_{1}
and enough large
R_{1}
.

It is apparent that assumptions on the smoothness of the right-hand sides may not be enough to prove existence of a classical smooth solution, but
H14
is enough to construct explicit solutions to (
1.5
)-(
1.9
). Here we focus on the solution of the problem which vanishes as
r\to+\infty
.
First of all, we remark that the explicit solutions of classical Dirichlet-transmission problem to Poisson equations in domain
G_{1}\cup g\cup G_{2}
were searched in
Section 9.4
[
32
]
. In light of this fact, we are left to construct solutions in the case of homogenous equations (
1.5
) and homogenous Dirichlet and transmission conditions (
1.8
) and (
1.9
), i.e. we put

f_{0,1},f_{0,2},f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}\equiv 0.

(6.1)

In order to build the solution of (
1.5
)-(
1.9
), we will exploit the following strategy. In the first stage, employing special change of variables and then Fourier and Laplace transforms, we reduce problem (
1.5
)-(
1.9
) to a functional difference equation. The second stage is related with applying Theorems
2.1
and
2.2
to this equation, and in turn with obtaining solution via formula like (
2.4
), (
2.9
) and (
2.10
). Finally, performing the inverse Laplace and Fourier transformation, we conclude with the explicit integral form of the solution to (
1.5
)-(
1.9
).

\bullet

The First Stage.
We start with standard change of variable (see e.g. (2.6) in
[
4
]
or (3.9) in
[
5
]
) in the case of a boundary value problem stated in the domain with corner points:

x_{1}=\ln r,\quad x_{2}=\varphi.

(6.2)

This map transforms the corners
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
to the strips
\mathfrak{G}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{G}_{2}
, respectively,

\mathfrak{G}_{1}=\{(x_{1},x_{2}):\,x_{1}\in\mathbb{R},\,-\pi/2<x_{2}<\omega_{0}\},\quad\mathfrak{G}_{2}=\{(x_{1},x_{2}):\,x_{1}\in\mathbb{R},\,\omega_{0}<x_{2}<\pi/2\},

while the image of the boundary
g
is
\mathfrak{g}=\{(x_{1},x_{2}):\,x_{1}\in\mathbb{R},\,x_{2}=\omega_{0}\}.

Performing the change of variables (
6.2
) in relations (
1.5
)-(
1.9
) and using the same notations for the functions
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
and
f
in the new variables, we end up with the problem

\begin{cases}\Delta u_{1}=0\qquad\text{in}\qquad\mathfrak{G}_{1,T},\qquad\quad u_{1}(x_{1},x_{2},0)=0\qquad\text{in}\qquad\bar{\mathfrak{G}}_{1},\\
\Delta u_{2}=0\qquad\text{in}\qquad\mathfrak{G}_{2,T},\qquad\quad u_{2}(x_{1},x_{2},0)=0\qquad\text{in}\qquad\bar{\mathfrak{G}}_{2},\\
\exp\{(\mathfrak{s}_{0}+1)x_{1}\}\Big[a_{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(u_{1}-u_{2})+a_{2}\mathbb{D}_{t}^{\nu}(u_{1}-u_{2})\Big]\\
-\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{2}}(u_{1}-u_{2})+a_{3}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}(u_{1}-u_{2})=e^{x_{1}}f(x_{1},t)\qquad\quad\text{on}\qquad\mathfrak{g}_{T},\\
\frac{\partial u_{1}}{\partial x_{2}}-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\partial u_{2}}{\partial x_{2}}+\mathfrak{K}a_{4}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}(u_{1}-u_{2})=0\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{on}\quad\mathfrak{g}_{T},\\
u_{1}(x_{1},-\pi/2,t)=0\quad\text{and}\quad u_{2}(x_{1},\pi/2,t)=0\qquad\qquad\text{for}\quad x_{1}\in\mathbb{R},\,t\in[0,T].\end{cases}

(6.3)

After that, as usual to the analysis of boundary value problems in domains with singular boundaries (see e.g.
[
16
, Section 4]
), we will search a solution to (
6.3
) in the form:

u_{1}(x_{1},x_{2},t)=e^{\mathfrak{s}x_{1}}U_{1}(x,_{1},x_{2},t),\quad u_{2}(x_{1},x_{2},t)=e^{\mathfrak{s}x_{1}}U_{2}(x,_{1},x_{2},t),

where the value
\mathfrak{s}\neq 0
will be specified below, and new unknown functions solve the problem

\begin{cases}\Delta U_{1}+2\mathfrak{s}\frac{\partial U_{1}}{\partial x_{1}}+\mathfrak{s}^{2}U_{1}=0\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathfrak{G}_{1,T},\qquad\quad U_{1}(x_{1},x_{2},0)=0\qquad\text{in}\quad\bar{\mathfrak{G}}_{1},\\
\Delta U_{2}+2\mathfrak{s}\frac{\partial U_{2}}{\partial x_{1}}+\mathfrak{s}^{2}U_{2}=0\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathfrak{G}_{2,T},\quad\qquad U_{2}(x_{1},x_{2},0)=0\qquad\text{in}\quad\bar{\mathfrak{G}}_{2},\\
\exp\{(\mathfrak{s}_{0}+1)x_{1}\}\Big[a_{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(U_{1}-U_{2})+a_{2}\mathbb{D}_{t}^{\nu}(U_{1}-U_{2})\Big]\\
-\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{2}}(U_{1}-U_{2})+a_{3}\Big[\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}(U_{1}-U_{2})+\mathfrak{s}(U_{1}-U_{2})\Big]=e^{(1-\mathfrak{s})x_{1}}f(x_{1},t)\quad\text{on}\quad\mathfrak{g}_{T},\\
\frac{\partial U_{1}}{\partial x_{2}}-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\partial U_{2}}{\partial x_{2}}+\mathfrak{K}a_{4}\Big[\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}(U_{1}-U_{2})+\mathfrak{s}(U_{1}-U_{2})\Big]=0\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{on}\quad\mathfrak{g}_{T},\\
U_{1}(x_{1},-\pi/2,t)=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad U_{2}(x_{1},\pi/2,t)=0\quad\qquad\qquad\text{for}\quad x_{1}\in\mathbb{R},\,t\in[0,T].\end{cases}

It is worth noting that the introducing new functions
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
and rewriting problem (
6.3
) in the terms of these functions permit to study the corresponding problem in either Hölder or Sobolev spaces instead of using weighted spaces (see for detail e.g.
[
4
, Section2]
or
[
7
, Section 3.1]
).

Then, we denote by
\tilde{U}(\lambda,x_{2},t)
the Fourier transform of
U(x_{1},x_{2},t)
, and by
\hat{U}(x_{1},x_{2},\sigma)
the Laplace transform of
U(x_{1},x_{2},t)
, and use the notation
*
instead of
\hat{\tilde{\,}}
. Thus, performing these transformations in the relations above, and setting

\mathfrak{x}=i\lambda+\mathfrak{s},

\mathfrak{s}^{\star}=\mathfrak{s}_{0}+1,

we have

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial^{2}U^{*}_{1}}{\partial x_{2}^{2}}+\mathfrak{x}^{2}U^{*}_{1}=0,\quad\tilde{U_{1}}(\lambda,x_{2},0)=0,\quad x_{2}\in(-\pi/2,\omega_{0}),\\
\frac{\partial^{2}U^{*}_{2}}{\partial x_{2}^{2}}+\mathfrak{x}^{2}U^{*}_{2}=0,\quad\tilde{U_{2}}(\lambda,x_{2},0)=0,\quad x_{2}\in(\omega_{0},\pi/2),\\
(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})[U_{1}^{*}(\lambda+i\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\omega_{0},\sigma)-U_{2}^{*}(\lambda+i\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\omega_{0},\sigma)]-\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{2}}[U_{1}^{*}(\lambda,\omega_{0},\sigma)-U_{2}^{*}(\lambda,\omega_{0},\sigma)]\\
+a_{3}\mathfrak{x}[U_{1}^{*}(\lambda,\omega_{0},\sigma)-U_{2}^{*}(\lambda,\omega_{0},\sigma)]=f^{*}(\lambda+i(1-\mathfrak{s}),\sigma),\\
\frac{\partial U_{1}^{*}}{\partial x_{2}}(\lambda,\omega_{0},\sigma)-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\partial U_{2}^{*}}{\partial x_{2}}(\lambda,\omega_{0},\sigma)+\mathfrak{K}a_{4}\mathfrak{x}[U_{1}^{*}(\lambda,\omega_{0},\sigma)-U_{2}^{*}(\lambda,\omega_{0},\sigma)]=0,\\
U_{1}^{*}(\lambda,-\pi/2,\sigma)=0,\quad U_{2}^{*}(\lambda,\pi/2,\sigma)=0.\end{cases}

(6.4)

To satisfy equations and boundary conditions on
x_{2}=\pm\pi/2
, we set

U_{1}^{\star}(\lambda,x_{2},\sigma)=\mathcal{M}_{1}(\lambda,\sigma)\sin\mathfrak{x}(x_{2}+\pi/2),\quad U_{2}^{\star}(\lambda,x_{2},\sigma)=\mathcal{M}_{2}(\lambda,\sigma)\sin\mathfrak{x}(x_{2}-\pi/2),

(6.5)

where unknown functions
\mathcal{M}_{1}(\lambda,\sigma)
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}(\lambda,\sigma)
will be found via transmission conditions in (
6.4
). To this end, substituting
U_{1}^{*}
and
U^{*}_{2}
to the transmission conditions and putting

\mathcal{N}(\lambda)=\frac{\cos\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)+a_{4}\sin\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)}{\cos\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}+\pi/2)+\mathfrak{K}a_{4}\sin\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}+\pi/2)},

we arrive at the system with unknown functions
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}
:

\begin{cases}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\lambda,\sigma)=\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}(\lambda)\mathcal{M}_{2}(\lambda,\sigma),\\
[a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu}]\mathcal{M}_{2}(\lambda+i\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\sigma)[\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}(\lambda+i\mathfrak{s}^{\star})\sin\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}+\pi/2)-\sin\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)]\\
-\mathfrak{x}\mathcal{M}_{2}(\lambda,\sigma)\{a_{3}\sin\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)-\cos\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)\\
+\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}(\lambda)[\cos\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}+\pi/2)-a_{3}\sin\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)]\}=f^{*}(\lambda+i(1-\mathfrak{s}),\sigma).\end{cases}

(6.6)

In virtue of this system, we are left to look for the function
\mathcal{M}_{2}
. Denoting

\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)=\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}\sin\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}+\pi/2)-\sin\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)

and substituting the expression of
\mathcal{M}_{1}
to the second equation in (
6.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{x}\mathcal{M}_{2}(\lambda,\sigma)\{\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}(\lambda)\cos\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}+\pi/2)-a_{3}\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)-\cos\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)\}

\displaystyle-[a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu}]\mathcal{M}_{2}(\lambda+i\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\sigma)\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda+i\mathfrak{s}^{\star})=-f^{*}(\lambda+i(1-\mathfrak{s}),\sigma)

which in turn is reduced to a functional equation in unknown function
\mathcal{U}:=\mathcal{M}_{2}(\lambda,\sigma)\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda),

[a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu}]\mathcal{U}(\lambda+i\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\sigma)-\mathfrak{x}\mathcal{G}(\lambda)\mathcal{U}(\lambda,\sigma)=f^{*}(\lambda+i(1-\mathfrak{s}),\sigma).

Here we put

\mathcal{G}(\lambda)=\frac{\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}(\lambda)\cos\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}+\pi/2)-\cos\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)}{\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)}-a_{3}.

Remark 6.1
.

It is apparent that if
\mathfrak{s}_{0}=-1,
i.e.
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}=0
, then the function

\mathcal{U}=\frac{f^{*}(\lambda+i(1-\mathfrak{s}),\sigma)}{a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu}-\mathfrak{x}\,\mathcal{G}(\lambda)}

solves the equation above, which in turn entails

U^{*}_{1}=\mathfrak{K}\sin\mathfrak{x}(x_{2}+\pi/2)\mathcal{U}(\lambda,\sigma)\mathcal{N}(\lambda)\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda),\qquad U^{*}_{2}=\sin\mathfrak{x}(x_{2}-\pi/2)\mathcal{U}(\lambda,\sigma)\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda).

Taking into account this remark, we will assume further
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\neq 0
and introduce the new variable

\lambda=i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho,

(6.7)

and new unknown function
V(\rho,\sigma)=\mathcal{U}(i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho,\sigma),

we rewrite the equation above in the form

[a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu}]V(\rho+1,\sigma)-[\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho]\mathcal{G}(i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)V(\rho,\sigma)=\mathfrak{F}(\rho,\sigma),

(6.8)

where
\mathfrak{F}(\rho,\sigma):=f^{*}(i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho+i(1-\mathfrak{s}),\sigma)
.

Thus, we have reduced original problem (
1.5
)-(
1.9
) to functional equation (
6.8
).

The Second Stage.
In order to apply the results of Sections
2
and
5
to (
6.8
), we are left to factorize the function
\mathcal{G}(i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)
. To this end, coming to the function
\mathcal{G}(\lambda)
, we substitute
\mathcal{N}(\lambda)
and
\mathcal{N}_{1}(\lambda)
to the expression of
\mathcal{G}(\lambda)
and performing tedious technical calculations, we derive

\mathcal{G}(\lambda)=\frac{\cos\pi\mathfrak{x}-\frac{a_{3}(1+\mathfrak{K}\,)+2\mathfrak{K}\,a_{4}}{\mathfrak{K}-1}\sin\pi\mathfrak{x}+\cos 2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{x}-a_{3}\sin 2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{x}}{\sin 2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{x}+\frac{\mathfrak{K}+1}{\mathfrak{K}-1}\sin\pi\mathfrak{x}}.

Finally, denoting

\displaystyle q_{2}^{*}=\frac{\mathfrak{K}+1}{|\mathfrak{K}-1|},\,q_{2}=\Big(\frac{[a_{3}(\mathfrak{K}+1)+2\mathfrak{K}\,a_{4}]^{2}+(\mathfrak{K}-1)^{2}}{(\mathfrak{K}-1)^{2}(1+a_{3}^{2})}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},\,\,\sin\theta_{1}=(1+a_{3}^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}},\,\cos\theta_{1}=a_{3}(1+a_{3}^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\sin\theta_{2}=\Big(1+\frac{[2\mathfrak{K}\,a_{4}+a_{3}(1+\mathfrak{K})]^{2}}{[\mathfrak{K}-1]^{2}}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{2}},\,\cos\theta_{2}=\frac{[a_{3}(\mathfrak{K}+1)+2\mathfrak{K}\,a_{4}]\text{sgn}(\mathfrak{K}-1)}{\sqrt{(\mathfrak{K}-1)^{2}+[2\mathfrak{K}\,a_{4}+a_{3}(1+\mathfrak{K})]^{2}}},

we rewrite
\mathcal{G}(\lambda)
in the form

\mathcal{G}(\lambda)=-\frac{\sqrt{1+a_{3}^{2}}[\sin(2\mathfrak{x}\omega_{0}-\theta_{1})+q_{2}\sin(\pi\mathfrak{x}-\theta_{2})]}{\sin 2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{x}+\text{sgn}(\mathfrak{K}-1)q_{2}^{*}\sin\pi\mathfrak{x}}.

Keeping in mind of assumptions on the coefficients, we easily conclude

\theta_{1}\in(0,\pi/2)\quad\text{and}\quad\theta_{2}\in(0,\pi),\quad q_{2}^{*}>1,\quad q_{2}>1,\quad\sin\theta_{1}+q_{2}\sin\theta_{2}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{1+a_{3}^{2}}}.

(6.9)

Then, performing the change of variable (
6.7
) and taking into account the relations

\frac{\pi}{2\omega_{0}}=\frac{p}{2q}>1,

we deduce

\mathcal{G}(i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)=-\sqrt{1+a_{3}^{2}}\begin{cases}\frac{\mathcal{S}^{+}(2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho);\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\frac{p}{2q},q_{2})}{\mathcal{S}^{+}(2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho);0,0,\frac{p}{2q},q^{*}_{2})},\quad\mathfrak{K}>1,\\
\frac{\mathcal{S}^{+}(2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho);\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\frac{p}{2q},q_{2})}{\mathcal{S}^{-}(2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho);0,0,\frac{p}{2q},q^{*}_{2})},\quad\mathfrak{K}<1,\end{cases}

This representation means that the function
\mathcal{G}(i\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\rho)
is the function
\mathcal{S}
in the
FTK
case (see Section
5
). Appealing to Corollary
5.1
, we denote by

\displaystyle\bar{z}_{i,n}^{+}

\displaystyle:=\bar{z}_{i,n}^{+}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{2}),\quad\bar{z}_{i,-n}^{+}:=\bar{z}_{i,n}^{+}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{2}),\quad\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}:=\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},q_{2}),

\displaystyle z_{i,n}^{+}

\displaystyle:=z_{i,n}^{+}(0,0,q^{*}_{2}),\quad{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}:={\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}(0,0,q^{*}_{2}),\quad z_{i,n}^{-}:=z_{i,n}^{-}(0,0,q^{*}_{2}),\quad{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}:={\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}(0,0,q^{*}_{2}),

the zeros and their number in
[0,4\pi q)
of the functions
\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\frac{p}{2q},q_{2}),

\mathcal{S}^{+}(z;0,0,\frac{p}{2q},q^{*}_{2})
and
\mathcal{S}^{-}(z;0,

0,\frac{p}{2q},q^{*}_{2}),
respectively. In virtue of (
6.9
), we can employ Proposition
5.1
to conclude that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)=\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}[\bar{z}_{i}^{+}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho-\mathfrak{s})]\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}(z_{i}^{+})^{2}}{\omega_{0}(1+q_{2}^{*}q_{1})(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{*}\rho)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}[z_{i}^{+}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho-\mathfrak{s})][z_{i}^{+}-2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho-\mathfrak{s})]\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\bar{z}_{i}^{+}}

\displaystyle\times\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\Big[\bar{z}_{i,n}^{+}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho-\mathfrak{s})\Big]\Big[-\bar{z}_{i,-n}^{+}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)\Big]}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}\Big[z_{i,n}^{+}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho-\mathfrak{s})\Big]\Big[z_{i,n}^{+}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)\Big]}\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1}(z_{i,n}^{+})^{2}}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\bar{z}_{i,n}^{+}(-\bar{z}_{i,n}^{+})},\,\text{if}\,\mathfrak{K}>1,

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)=\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}[\bar{z}_{i}^{+}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho-\mathfrak{s})]\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}(z_{i}^{-})^{2}}{\omega_{0}(1-q_{2}^{*}q_{1})(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{*}\rho)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}[z_{i}^{-}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho-\mathfrak{s})][z_{i}^{-}-2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho-\mathfrak{s})]\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\bar{z}_{i}^{+}}

\displaystyle\times\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\Big[\bar{z}_{i,n}^{+}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho-\mathfrak{s})\Big]\Big[-\bar{z}_{i,-n}^{+}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)\Big]}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}\Big[z_{i,n}^{-}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho-\mathfrak{s})\Big]\Big[z_{i,n}^{-}+2\omega_{0}(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)\Big]}\times\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1}(z_{i,n}^{-})^{2}}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\bar{z}_{i,n}^{+}(-\bar{z}_{i,n}^{+})},\,\text{if}\,\mathfrak{K}<1.

At this point, we state the first assumption on
\mathfrak{s}
.

H15:
We require that
\mathfrak{s}
satisfies inequalities:
\,\sin 2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}+\text{sgn}(\mathfrak{K}-1)q^{*}_{2}\sin\pi\mathfrak{s}\neq 0,
and

|\mathfrak{s}|<4\pi q,\quad 2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}\neq-\bar{z}^{+}_{i,n},\,2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}\neq\bar{z}^{+}_{i,-n},\quad i\in\{0,1,...,\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1\},\,

and

2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}\neq\pm z^{-}_{i,n},\,\quad i\in\{0,1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-}-1\},\,n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\},\quad\text{if}\,\mathfrak{K}<1,

while

2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}\neq\pm z^{+}_{i,n},\,i\in\{0,1,...,{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+}-1\},\,n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\},\quad\text{if}\,\mathfrak{K}>1.

Then, setting for
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}

\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}:=\begin{cases}{\boldsymbol{K}}^{+},\quad\text{if}\quad\mathfrak{K}>1,\\
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{-},\quad\text{if}\quad\mathfrak{K}<1,\end{cases}

\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,n}:=\frac{\bar{z}_{i,n}^{+}-2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}}{|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|2\omega_{0}},\quad\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,-n}:=\frac{-\bar{z}_{i,-n}^{+}+2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}}{|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|2\omega_{0}},\quad i\in\{0,1,...,\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1\},

Z_{i,n}=\begin{cases}\frac{z_{i,n}^{+}-2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}}{|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|2\omega_{0}},\quad\text{if}\quad\mathfrak{K}>1,\\
\frac{z_{i,n}^{-}-2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}}{|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|2\omega_{0}},\quad\text{if}\quad\mathfrak{K}<1,\end{cases}\quad Z_{i,-n}=\begin{cases}\frac{z_{i,n}^{+}+2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}}{|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|2\omega_{0}},\quad\text{if}\quad\mathfrak{K}>1,\\
\frac{z_{i,n}^{-}+2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}}{|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|2\omega_{0}},\quad\text{if}\quad\mathfrak{K}<1,\end{cases}\,i\in\{0,1,...,\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}\},

and taking into account
H15
and the easy verified equality

\mathcal{G}(0)=-\sqrt{1+a_{3}^{2}}\frac{\sin(2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}-\theta_{1})+q_{2}\sin(\pi\mathfrak{s}-\theta_{2})}{\sin 2\omega_{0}\mathfrak{s}+\text{sgn}(\mathfrak{K}-1)q_{2}^{*}\sin\pi\mathfrak{s}},

we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{G}(0)\mathfrak{s}}{(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)}\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}[\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,0}+\text{sgn}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star})\rho]}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1}[Z_{i,0}+\text{sgn}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star})\rho][Z_{i,-0}-\text{sgn}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star})\rho]}\frac{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1}Z_{i,0}Z_{i,-0}}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\bar{Z}_{i,0}^{+}}

\displaystyle\times\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}[\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,n}+\text{sgn}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star})\rho][\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,-n}-\text{sgn}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star})\rho]}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1}[Z_{i,n}+\text{sgn}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star})\rho][Z_{i,-n}-\text{sgn}(\mathfrak{s}^{\star})\rho]}\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1}Z_{i,n}Z_{i,-n}}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\bar{Z}_{i,n}^{+}\bar{Z}_{i,-n}^{+}}.

(6.10)

In virtue of assumptions
H15
and Corollary
5.1
, we conclude that the sequences:
\{Z_{i,n}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
,
\{Z_{i,-n}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
,
\{\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,n}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
,
\{\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,-n}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
, meet the requirements of
H2
and
H3
for each
i
, besides the terms of these sequences are positive for
n\geq 1
and don’t vanish if
n=0
.

Now, coming to equation (
6.8
) and keeping in mind factorization (
6
), we employ Theorems
2.1
and
2.2
to build the explicit solutions homogeneous and inhomogeneous equation (
6.8
).

At this point, we first find a solution of homogeneous (6.11) (
\mathcal{F}\equiv 0
). To this end, putting

\mathfrak{d}_{0}(\mathfrak{K}):=\frac{\mathcal{G}(0)\mathfrak{s}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1}Z_{i,0}Z_{i,-0}}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\bar{Z}_{i,0}^{+}},\,\,\mathbb{L}_{0}(\rho,\mathfrak{K},\mathfrak{s}^{\star})=\begin{cases}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\Gamma(\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,0}+\rho)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\bar{K}-1}\frac{\Gamma(1+Z_{i,-0}-\rho)}{\Gamma(\rho+Z_{i,0})},\quad\text{if}\quad\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0,\\
\frac{1}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\Gamma(1+\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,0}-\rho)}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\bar{K}-1}\frac{\Gamma(1+Z_{i,0}-\rho)}{\Gamma(\rho+Z_{i,-0})},\quad\,\text{if}\quad\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0,\end{cases}

\mathcal{R}(n,\mathfrak{K},\mathfrak{s}^{\star})=\begin{cases}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}[\bar{Z}_{i,-n}^{+}(\ln\bar{Z}_{i,-n}^{+}-1)-\bar{Z}_{i,n}^{+}(\ln\bar{Z}_{i,n}^{+}-1)]\\
-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1}[Z_{i,-n}(\ln Z_{i,-n}-1)-Z_{i,n}(\ln Z_{i,n}-1)],\quad\text{ if }\quad\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0,\\
\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}[\bar{Z}_{i,-n}^{+}(\ln\bar{Z}_{i,-n}^{+}-1)-\bar{Z}_{i,n}^{+}(\ln\bar{Z}_{i,n}^{+}-1)]\\
+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1}[Z_{i,-n}(\ln Z_{i,-n}-1)-Z_{i,n}(\ln Z_{i,n}-1)],\quad\text{ if }\quad\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0,\end{cases}

and

\mathbb{L}_{1}(\rho,\mathfrak{K},\mathfrak{s}^{\star})=\begin{cases}\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\frac{\Gamma(\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,n}+\rho)}{\Gamma(\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,-n}+1-\rho)}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1}\frac{\Gamma(Z_{i,-n}-\rho+1)}{\Gamma(Z_{i,n}+\rho)}\bigg(\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1}Z_{i,n}Z_{i,-n}}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,n}\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,-n}}\bigg)^{\rho-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\mathcal{R}(\rho,\mathfrak{K},\,\mathfrak{s}^{\star})}\quad\text{if}\quad\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0,\\
\prod\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\frac{\Gamma(\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,-n}+\rho)}{\Gamma(\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,n}+1-\rho)}\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1}\frac{\Gamma(Z_{i,n}-\rho+1)}{\Gamma(Z_{i,-n}+\rho)}\bigg(\frac{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1}Z_{i,n}Z_{i,-n}}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1}\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,n}\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,-n}}\bigg)^{\rho-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\mathcal{R}(\rho,\mathfrak{K},\,\mathfrak{s}^{\star})}\quad\text{if}\quad\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0,\end{cases}

and exploiting Theorem
2.1
, we end up with the explicit form of the solution to homogeneous equation (
6.8
):

V_{h}(\rho,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})=(\mathfrak{d}_{0}(\mathfrak{K}))^{\rho-1/2}{\mathbb{P}}(\rho)(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})^{\frac{1}{2}-\rho}\mathbb{L}_{0}(\rho,\mathfrak{K},\mathfrak{s}^{\star})\mathbb{L}_{0}(\rho,\mathfrak{K},\mathfrak{s}^{\star})

(6.11)

where, for
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
n\in\mathbb{N},

Re\,\rho
satisfies the following inequalities (similar to (
2.2
) and (
2.3
)) :

Re\rho\neq-m-\bar{Z}_{i,n}^{+},\,i\in\{0,...,\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1\},\,Re\rho\neq m+1+Z_{j,1-n},\,j\in\{0,...,\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1\},\,Re\rho\neq 1+m+Z_{0,-n}

(6.12)

if
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0
, while

Re\rho\neq-m-\bar{Z}_{i,-n}^{+},\,i\in\{0,...,\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}^{+}-1\},\,Re\rho\neq m+1+Z_{j,n-1},\,j\in\{0,...,\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}-1\},\,Re\rho\neq 1+m+Z_{0,n}

(6.13)

if
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0
.

Besides, the following statement is simple consequence of Theorem
2.1
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
Re\,\rho
meet requirement (
6.12
) in the case of
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0
and (
6.13
) if
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0
. Then the function
\frac{V_{h}(\rho,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}})}{{\mathbb{P}}(\rho)}
has no poles and the infinite product
\mathbb{L}_{1}(\rho,\mathfrak{K},\mathfrak{s}^{\star})
converges for each complex
\rho
. Besides, the relations hold:

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{d}_{0}(\mathfrak{K}))^{\rho-1/2}\mathbb{L}_{0}(\rho,\mathfrak{K},\mathfrak{s}^{\star})\mathbb{L}_{0}(\rho,\mathfrak{K},\mathfrak{s}^{\star})=[(\mathfrak{s}-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)\mathcal{G}(i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho)]^{\rho-1/2}\exp\{C_{1}\ln\rho+C_{2}\rho+O(1)\},

\displaystyle|V_{h}(\rho,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}){\mathbb{P}}^{-1}(\rho)|\leq C|\rho|^{C_{1}+Re\rho-1/2}\exp\{\arg(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})Im\rho+[-\frac{\pi}{2}\text{sgn}\,(Re\rho)+\theta_{2}(\text{sgn}\,\mathfrak{s}^{\star})]|Im\rho|\},

as
|Im\rho|\to+\infty
and
Re\rho
remains bounded.

Since we look for a bounded solution at the infinity, Remark
2.1
and Theorem
2.2
tell us that we are left to construct the particular solution in form (
2.10
). To this end, we first put the function
{\mathbb{P}}(\rho):={\mathbb{P}}_{1}(\rho)
where

{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(\rho)=\begin{cases}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{5}\sin\pi(1+Z_{i,0}-\rho)\prod\limits_{i=0}^{2}\sin^{-1}\pi(1+\bar{Z}_{i,0}^{+}-\rho),\quad\text{if}\,\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0,\\
\prod\limits_{i=0}^{4}\sin\pi(\bar{Z}_{i,0}^{+}+\rho)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{3}\sin^{-1}\pi(Z_{i,0}+\rho),\qquad\qquad\,\text{ if }\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0.\end{cases}

(6.14)

It is apparent that the introduced function
{\mathbb{P}}_{1}
obeys the properties:

{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(\rho)\approx Ce^{2\pi|Im\rho|},\quad\text{as}\quad|Im\rho|\to+\infty,

(6.15)

besides the functions:

\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(\rho)\prod\limits_{i=1}^{5}\Gamma(1+Z_{i,0}-\rho)}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{2}\Gamma(1+\bar{Z}_{i,0}^{+}-\rho)}\,
if
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0\,
and
\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{1}(\rho)\prod\limits_{i=0}^{4}\Gamma(\bar{Z}_{i,0}^{+}+\rho)}{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{3}\Gamma(Z_{i,0}+\rho)}\,
if
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0

do not have any poles if for
l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, the inequalities hold

Re\rho\neq l_{2}-Z_{i,0},\,i\in\{1,2,3\},\text{ if }\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0,\,\text{and }Re\rho\neq 1+\bar{Z}^{+}_{j,0}-l_{1},\,j\in\{0,1,2\},\text{ if }\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0,

(6.16)

and do not have any nulls if

Re\rho\neq l_{2}-\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,0},\,i\in\{0,1,2,3,4,\}\text{ if }\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0,\,\text{and }Re\rho\neq 1+Z_{j,0}-l_{1},\,j\in\{1,2,3,4,5\}\text{ if }\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0.

(6.17)

Collecting Proposition
6.1
with (
6.12
)-(
6.17
) and setting

Z^{*}=\begin{cases}1+Z_{6,-0},\quad\text{if}\quad\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}>6,\\
4\pi q,\quad\qquad\text{ if}\quad\bar{{\boldsymbol{K}}}\leq 6,\end{cases}

we conclude.

Proposition 6.2
.

The function
V_{h}(\rho,\sigma):=V_{h}(\rho,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})
given with (
6.11
) and (
6.14
) has the following properties

i:

V_{h}(\rho,\sigma)
does not have any poles if in the case of
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0

the inequalities hold

-\bar{Z}_{0,-1}^{+}<Re\rho<Z^{*}\,\text{and }Re\rho\neq 1-\bar{m}(i)+\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,0},\quad i\in\{0,1,2\},

(6.18)

where the integer
\bar{m}(i)
satisfies inequalities

\max\{1;\bar{Z}_{i,0}^{+}-Z^{*}+\}<\bar{m}(i)<1+\bar{Z}_{i,0}^{+}+\bar{Z}_{0,-1}^{+},

while in the case of
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0
there hold

-\bar{Z}_{5,0}^{+}<Re\rho<1+Z_{1,-0},\,Re\rho\neq\bar{m}(i)-Z_{i,0},\quad i\in\{1,2,3\},

(6.19)

where the integer
\bar{m}(i)
satisfies relations

\max\{1;Z_{i,0}-\bar{Z}^{+}_{5,0}\}<\bar{m}(i)<1+Z_{i,0}+Z_{1,-0};

ii:

V_{h}(\rho,\sigma)
does not vanish if in the case of
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0
the inequalities hold:

-{Z}_{1,-0}<Re\rho<1+\bar{Z}^{+}_{3,0},\text{ and }\,Re\rho\neq 1-\underline{m}(i)+Z_{i,0},\quad i\in\{1,2,3,4,5\},

(6.20)

with the integer
\underline{m}(i)
satisfying bounds:

\max\{1;Z_{i,0}-\bar{Z}^{+}_{3,0}\}<\underline{m}(i)<1+Z_{i,0}+Z_{1,-0},

while in the case of
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0
there are inequalities

-Z_{4,0}<Re\rho<1+\bar{Z}^{+}_{0,-1},\text{ and }\,Re\rho\neq\underline{m}(i)-\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,0},\quad i\in\{0,1,2,3,4\},

(6.21)

with the integer
\underline{m}(i)
satisfying bounds:
\max\{1;\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,0}-Z_{4,0}\}<\underline{m}(i)<1+\bar{Z}^{+}_{i,0}+\bar{Z}^{+}_{0,-1}.

iii:

If
Re\rho
meets requirements (
6.20
) and (
6.21
), then the estimate holds

|V_{h}(\rho,\sigma)|^{-1}\leq C\exp\{-\Big[\frac{\pi}{2}-\varepsilon+\theta_{2}(sgn\mathfrak{s}^{\star})\Big]|Im\rho|\},\quad\text{as}\quad|Im\rho|\to+\infty,

where
\varepsilon
is a sufficiently small positive number,
0<\varepsilon<\theta/10
.

Taking into account Propositions
6.1
and
6.2
and Theorem
2.2
, and choosing
\mathcal{K}_{1}(\xi)=\frac{\sin^{2}\pi d}{\sin^{2}\pi(\xi+d)},

0<d-d_{0}<1
(see Table
4
in Example
5.2
), we construct the particular solution of equation (
6.8
).

Proposition 6.3
.

Let assumptions
H14
and
H15
hold. Moreover, we assume that

-Z_{1,-0}<Re\rho<\bar{Z}_{3,0}^{+},\text{ and }\,Re\rho\neq d_{0}+Z_{i,-0}-\underline{m}(i),\quad i\in\{1,2,3,4,5\},

if
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0
, and in the case of
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0
, there are

-Z_{4,0}<Re\rho<\bar{Z}_{0,-1}^{+},\text{ and }\,Re\rho\neq d_{0}-Z_{i,0}-1+\underline{m}(i),\quad i\in\{0,1,2,3,4\},

with
\underline{m}(i)
meets the requirements of
(ii)
in Proposition
6.2
.
Then the function

V(\rho,\sigma)=\frac{1}{2i}\int\limits_{\ell_{d_{0}}}\frac{V_{h}(\rho,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})f^{*}(i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\rho+i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\xi+i(1-\mathfrak{s}),\sigma)}{V_{h}(\rho+1+\xi,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})}[\cot\pi\xi+i]\frac{\sin^{2}\pi d}{\sin^{2}\pi(\xi+d)}d\xi

solves equation (
6.8
).
Besides, the function
\frac{V_{h}(\rho,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}{V_{h}(\rho+1+\xi,\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}
is analytic in
\rho
if
\xi=-d_{0}+iy,

y\in\mathbb{R}
,
d_{0}\in[0,1],
and
Re\rho
meets the requirements

-Z_{1,-0}<Re\rho<\bar{Z}^{+}_{3,0},\,\,Re\rho\neq d_{0}+Z_{i,-0}-\underline{m}(i),\,\,i\in\{1,2,3,4,5\},\,\,Re\rho\neq 1-\bar{m}(i)+\bar{Z}_{j,0}^{+},\,\,dj\in\{0,1,2\},

in the case of
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0,
and

-Z_{4,0}<Re\rho<1+Z_{1,-0},\,\,Re\rho\neq d_{0}-Z_{i,0}-1+\underline{m}(i),\,\,i\in\{0,1,2,3,4\},\,\,Re\rho\neq\bar{m}(i)-Z_{j,0},\,\,j\in\{1,2,3\},

if
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0.

Integer
\underline{m}(i)
and
\bar{m}(i)
meet requirement of Proposition
6.2
.

Third Stage.
Denoting

\mathcal{N}_{2}(\mathfrak{x})=[\cot\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)+a_{4}][\cot\mathfrak{x}(\omega_{0}+\pi/2)+\mathfrak{K}a_{4}]^{-1},

and collecting Proposition
6.3
with relations (
6.5
) and (
6.6
), (
6.7
), we end up with the solution

\displaystyle U_{1}^{*}(\lambda,x_{2},\sigma)

\displaystyle=\frac{V_{h}(-i\lambda/\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}{2i}\frac{\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}_{2}(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})\sin(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})(x_{2}+\pi/2)}{[\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}_{2}(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})+1]\sin(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})(\omega_{0}+\pi/2)}

\displaystyle\times\int\limits_{\ell_{d_{0}}}\frac{f^{*}(\lambda+i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\xi+i(1-\mathfrak{s}),\sigma)[\cot\pi\xi+i]}{(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})V_{h}(1+\xi-i\lambda/\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}\frac{\sin^{2}\pi d}{\sin^{2}\pi(\xi+d)}d\xi,

\displaystyle U_{2}^{*}(\lambda,x_{2},\sigma)

\displaystyle=\frac{V_{h}(-i\lambda/\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}{2i}\frac{\sin(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})(x_{2}-\pi/2)}{[\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}_{2}(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})+1]\sin(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)}

(6.22)

\displaystyle\times\int\limits_{\ell_{d_{0}}}\frac{f^{*}(\lambda+i\mathfrak{s}^{\star}\xi+i(1-\mathfrak{s}),\sigma)[\cot\pi\xi+i]}{(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})V_{h}(1+\xi-i\lambda/\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})}\frac{\sin^{2}\pi d}{\sin^{2}\pi(\xi+d)}d\xi.

Finally, we are left to compute inverse Laplace and Fourier transformations to obtain the integral representation of the solution (
1.5
)-(
1.9
). To this end, we appeal to (5.1.33) in
[
14
]
and deduce

\mathcal{L}(t,y):=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int\limits_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}\frac{e^{\sigma t}d\sigma}{(a_{1}\sigma+a_{2}\sigma^{\nu})^{d_{0}-iy}}=\begin{cases}\frac{t^{-1+\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\nu}{a_{1}+a_{2}}[d_{0}-iy]}}{\Gamma\big(\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\nu}{a_{1}+a_{2}}[d_{0}-iy]\big)(a_{2}+a_{1})^{d_{0}-iy}},\quad\quad\text{if}\,\text{ either }a_{1}=0\text{ or }a_{2}=0,\\
\,\\
a_{1}^{-d_{0}+iy}t^{-1+d_{0}-iy}E_{1-\nu,d_{0}-iy}^{d_{0}-iy}(-\frac{a_{2}}{a_{1}}t^{1-\nu}),\quad\text{if}\quad a_{1},a_{2}>0,\end{cases}

where
E_{\alpha,\beta}^{\gamma}(\cdot)
is the three-parametric Mittag-Leffler function (see, e.g. (5.1.4) in
[
14
]
).

Then, collecting these relations with (
6.22
), we arrive at the explicit solution to (
1.5
)-(
1.9
).

Proposition 6.4
.

Let assumptions of Propositions
6.1
-
6.3
and restriction (
6.1
) hold, then the following functions solves problem (
1.5
)-(
1.9
):

\displaystyle U_{1}(x_{1},x_{2},t)

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{t}d\tau\int\limits_{-\infty}^{+\infty}d\varrho f(x_{1}-\varrho,t-\tau)e^{[-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}d_{0}+1-\mathfrak{s}](x_{1}-\varrho)}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{+\infty}dy\frac{\mathcal{L}(t,y)e^{iy\mathfrak{s}^{\star}(x_{1}-\varrho)}\sin^{2}\pi d}{\sin\pi(d_{0}-iy)\sin^{2}\pi(d-d_{0}+iy)}

\displaystyle\times\int\limits_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\frac{e^{i\lambda\varrho}V_{h}(-i\lambda/\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}_{2}(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})\sin(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})(x_{2}+\pi/2)}{V_{h}(1-d_{0}+iy-i\lambda/\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})[1+\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}_{2}(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})]\sin(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})(\omega_{0}+\pi/2)}d\lambda;

\displaystyle U_{2}(x_{1},x_{2},t)

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{t}d\tau\int\limits_{-\infty}^{+\infty}d\varrho f(x_{1}-\varrho,t-\tau)e^{[-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}d_{0}+1-\mathfrak{s}](x_{1}-\varrho)}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{+\infty}dy\frac{\mathcal{L}(t,y)e^{iy\mathfrak{s}^{\star}(x_{1}-\varrho)}\sin^{2}\pi d}{\sin\pi(d_{0}-iy)\sin^{2}\pi(d-d_{0}+iy)}

\displaystyle\times\int\limits_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\frac{e^{i\lambda\varrho}V_{h}(-i\lambda/\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})\sin(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})(x_{2}-\pi/2)}{V_{h}(1-d_{0}+iy-i\lambda/\mathfrak{s}^{\star},\sigma;{\mathbb{P}}_{1})[1+\mathfrak{K}\mathcal{N}_{2}(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})]\sin(i\lambda+\mathfrak{s})(\omega_{0}-\pi/2)}d\lambda,

where
Im\lambda
meets requirements:

-Z_{1,-0}<\frac{Im\lambda}{-\mathfrak{s}^{\star}}<\bar{Z}^{+}_{3,0},\,\,\frac{Im\lambda}{\mathfrak{s}^{\star}}\neq d_{0}+Z_{i,-0}-\underline{m}(i),\,i\in\{1,2,3,4,5\},\,\,\frac{Im\lambda}{\mathfrak{s}^{\star}}\neq 1-\bar{m}(j)+\bar{Z}_{j,0}^{+},\,j\in\{0,1,2\},

in the case of
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0
, while if
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0
then the inequalities hold

-Z_{4,0}<\frac{Im\lambda}{\mathfrak{s}^{\star}}<1+Z_{1,-0},\,\,\frac{Im\lambda}{\mathfrak{s}^{\star}}\neq d_{0}-1-Z_{i,0}+\underline{m}(i),\,i\in\{0,1,2,3,4\},\,\,\frac{Im\lambda}{\mathfrak{s}^{\star}}\neq\bar{m}(j)-Z_{j,0},\,j\in\{1,2,3\}

with integer
\underline{m}(i)
and
\bar{m}(i)
satisfying relations in
(i),(ii)
of Proposition
6.2
.

Remark 6.2
.

It is worth noting that relations on
Im\lambda
in Proposition
6.4
provide additional assumptions on the weight
\mathfrak{s}
. Indeed, putting
Im\lambda=0
in the relations above, we arrive at the conditions:

\bullet
if
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}<0
, then

\mathfrak{s}\neq\frac{\bar{z}_{i}^{+}}{2\omega_{0}}-|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|[\bar{m}(i)-1],\,i\in\{0,1,2\},
and for
j\in\{0,1,...,5\}

-\frac{z_{1}^{+}}{2\omega_{0}}<\mathfrak{s}<\frac{\bar{z}_{3}^{+}}{2\omega_{0}},\quad\mathfrak{s}\neq|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|[\underline{m}(j)-d_{0}]-\frac{z_{j}^{+}}{2\omega_{0}},\,\,\text{ if }\,\mathfrak{K}>1,

-\frac{z_{1}^{-}}{2\omega_{0}}<\mathfrak{s}<\frac{\bar{z}_{3}^{+}}{2\omega_{0}},\quad\mathfrak{s}\neq|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|[\underline{m}(j)-d_{0}]-\frac{z_{j}^{-}}{2\omega_{0}},\,\,\text{ if }\,\mathfrak{K}<1;

\bullet
if
\mathfrak{s}^{\star}>0
, then

-\frac{z_{1}^{+}}{2\omega_{0}}-1<\mathfrak{s}<\frac{z_{4}^{+}}{2\omega_{0}},\mathfrak{s}\neq\frac{z_{i}^{+}}{2\omega_{0}}+|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|[1-\underline{m}(i)-d_{0}],\,i\in\{0,...,4\},\mathfrak{s}\neq-|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|\bar{m}(j)+\frac{z_{j}^{+}}{2\omega_{0}},j\in\{1,2,3\},\text{if }\mathfrak{K}>1,

while if
\mathfrak{K}<1
then

-\frac{z_{1}^{-}}{2\omega_{0}}-1<\mathfrak{s}<\frac{z_{4}^{-}}{2\omega_{0}},\mathfrak{s}\neq\frac{z_{i}^{-}}{2\omega_{0}}+|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|[1-\underline{m}(i)-d_{0}],\,i\in\{0,...,4\},\mathfrak{s}\neq-|\mathfrak{s}^{\star}|\bar{m}(j)+\frac{z_{j}^{-}}{2\omega_{0}},j\in\{1,2,3\}.

It is apparent that the selected weight
\mathfrak{s}
in accordance with these assumptions will satisfy
H15
.

We conclude this section with some comments related to other boundary problems with dynamic boundary conditions in plane corners.

Remark 6.3
.

Actually, with nonessential modifications in the arguments in Section
6
, the very same results hold for transmission problems (
1.5
)-(
1.8
) supplemented with Neumann boundary conditions on
\partial G_{i}\backslash g,

i=1,2.
Besides, the proposed approach in Section
6
can be incorporated to find explicit solutions of the non-classical Dirichlet or Neumann boundary value problem with fractional dynamic boundary conditions in plane corners.

Appendix: Proof of Proposition
3.1

We start with validation of the first estimate in Proposition
3.1
. To this end, it is enough to evaluate the first term in the left-hand side of the inequality (i). The second series will be examined with the same arguments.
The simple technical calculations and properties of the sequence of
\mathfrak{b}(n)
provide the relations

\displaystyle\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)}=\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{z_{1}(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})}{\mathfrak{b}(n)[(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}]}+i\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{z_{2}}{[(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}]}

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{z_{2}^{2}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)[(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}]}=C+O(1/|z_{2}|)+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}+1}^{+\infty}\frac{z_{2}^{2}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)[(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}]}.

(A.1)

To handle the last term in the right-hand side of this equality, we take advantage of the easy verified inequalities

\Big|\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{z_{2}^{2}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)[(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}]}\Big|\leq\int_{\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{Cz_{2}^{2}dx}{\mathfrak{b}(x)[z_{2}^{2}+\mathfrak{b}^{2}(x)]}\leq\frac{z_{2}^{2}}{\delta_{0}}\int_{\mathfrak{b}(\mathfrak{N}\,+1)}^{+\infty}\frac{Cdy}{y[z_{2}^{2}+y^{2}]}

with
\delta_{0}>0
. Here we used that the sequence
\{\mathfrak{b}(n)\}_{n=1}^{+\infty}
is the strictly increasing, i.e.

\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}(x)>\delta_{0}>0,\quad\forall x\geq\mathfrak{N}+1.

(A.2)

Performing the change of variable
(z^{2}_{2}y^{-2})=\mathfrak{p}
, we obtain

\Big|\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{z_{2}^{2}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)[(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}]}\Big|\leq\frac{C}{\delta_{0}}\int^{z_{2}^{2}\mathfrak{b}^{-2}(\mathfrak{N}\,+1)}_{0}\frac{d\mathfrak{p}}{1+\mathfrak{p}^{2}}=C\ln[1+z_{2}^{2}\mathfrak{b}^{-2}(\mathfrak{N}\,+1)].

Collecting this inequality with (
Appendix: Proof of Proposition
), we arrive at the first estimate in Proposition
3.1
.

\bullet
Concerning the second estimate in Proposition
3.1
, we can conclude that second and third terms in the left-hand side of this inequality are evaluated exactly like the first one. Hence, we focus here only on the proof of the equality

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\Big|\frac{z}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)}\Big|=C+O(1/|z|),

if
|z_{2}|\to+\infty
and
z_{1}
meets the requirements of Proposition
3.1
.

Performing the simple calculations, we reach to the representation

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{z}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)}=z\sum_{j=1}^{4}\mathfrak{B}_{j}(z),

(A.3)

where we put

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}\frac{C^{\star}(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})}{[(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}][(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}]};

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}\frac{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}}{[(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}][(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}]};

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{3}(z)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}\frac{z_{2}^{2}}{[(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}][(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}]};

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{4}(z)

\displaystyle=-i\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}\frac{z_{2}(2\mathfrak{b}(n)+2z_{1}+C^{\star})}{[(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}][(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}]}.

Next, we exploit the inequality (
A.2
) to achieve the relations

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{b}^{2}(n)+z_{2}^{2}}\leq\int_{\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{dx}{\mathfrak{b}^{2}(x)+z_{2}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\delta_{0}}\int_{\mathfrak{b}(\mathfrak{N}\,+1)}^{+\infty}\frac{dy}{y^{2}+z_{2}^{2}}=\frac{C}{|z_{2}|},

which in turn arrive at the bound

|z_{2}||\mathfrak{B}_{1}(z)|+|\mathfrak{B}_{2}(z)|+|\mathfrak{B}_{3}(z)|+|\mathfrak{B}_{4}(z)|\leq\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{C}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{z_{2}^{2}}.

Collecting these estimates with representation (
A.3
), we end up with the desired bound.

\bullet
As for the third estimate in Proposition
3.1
, to verify this statement is enough to obtain the inequality

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}+1}\Big|\mathfrak{b}(n)\frac{z^{3}}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{3}}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{(j+3)}\frac{z^{j}}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{j}}\Big|\leq C|z^{2}|[\ln|z|+1]+O(1),\quad\text{as }|z_{2}|\to+\infty

(A.4)

and
z_{1}
satisfying the requirements of Proposition
3.1
.

It is apparent that assumptions on the sequence
\mathfrak{b}(n)
and
z_{1}
provide the bound

\Big|\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big|<1\quad\text{for each }n\geq\mathfrak{N}+1\quad\text{and }|z_{2}|\to+\infty.

In light of the last inequality, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{b}(n)+z|}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{(j+3)}\Big|\frac{z}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)}\Big|^{j}\leq\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{b}(n)+z|}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\Big|\frac{z}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)}\Big|^{j}=\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{b}(n)+z|-|z|}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1})^{2}+z_{2}^{2}}+\sqrt{z_{1}^{2}+z_{2}^{2}}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+2z_{1})}\leq C\sum_{k=1}^{3}\mathfrak{A}_{k}(z),

where we set

\mathfrak{A}_{1}(z)=\frac{|z_{1}|}{\mathfrak{b}(n)|\mathfrak{b}(n)+2z_{1}|},\,\mathfrak{A}_{2}(z)=\frac{|z_{2}|}{\mathfrak{b}(n)|\mathfrak{b}(n)+2z_{1}|},\,\mathfrak{A}_{3}(z)=\frac{|\mathfrak{b}(n)+z_{1}|}{\mathfrak{b}(n)|\mathfrak{b}(n)+2z_{1}|}.

Summarizing, we are left to produce the suitable bound of

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}+1}^{+\infty}|z|\mathfrak{b}(n)\Big|\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big|^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\mathfrak{A}_{k}(z).

To this end, we appeal to estimates in points (i) and (ii) in Proposition
3.1
and arrive at the relations

\displaystyle\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{b}(n)\Big|\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big|^{2}[|z|\mathfrak{A}_{1}(z)+\mathfrak{A}_{2}(z)]\leq C|z|\ln|z|+O(1),

\displaystyle\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}|z|\mathfrak{b}(n)\Big|\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big|^{2}\mathfrak{A}_{3}(z)\leq C|z|[|z|+1].

in the case of
z_{2}\to\pm\infty
.
Collecting these inequalities, we deduce bound (
A.4
) and, accordingly, the desired estimate in (iii).

\bullet
At this point, we will examine the first equality in (iv). Note that the second one is verified with the same arguments.
First, we use the easily verified relations:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{(j+3)}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{-3}{j(j+3)}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{j},

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{(j+3)}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{-3}{j(j+3)}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{j}.

In particular, taking into account of the assumptions on
\mathfrak{b}(n)
and
z_{1}
, we deduce the equalities:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{(j+3)}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{-3}{j(j+3)}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{j}-\ln\Big(1-\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{(j+3)}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j(j+3)}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{j}-\ln\Big(1-\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z+C^{\star}}\Big).

Therefore, we end up with the equality

\displaystyle\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{\infty}\Big[\mathfrak{b}(n)\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j+3}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{j}

\displaystyle-(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star})\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{j+3}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{j}\Big]\equiv\sum_{k=1}\mathfrak{D}_{k}(z),

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{1}(z)=C^{\star}\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{3}\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{3}{j(j+3)}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{j}+\ln\Big(1-\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)\Big],

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{2}(z)=\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{\infty}\Big[\frac{\mathfrak{b}(n)z^{3}}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{3}}-\frac{\mathfrak{b}(n)z^{3}}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{3}}\Big]\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{3}{j(j+3)}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{j}+\ln\Big(1-\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)\Big],

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{3}(z)=\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{b}(n)\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{3z^{j}}{j(j+3)}\Big(\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{j}}-\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{j}}\Big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{4}(z)=\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}\mathfrak{b}(n)\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{3}\ln\frac{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star})(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)}{\mathfrak{b}(n)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)}.

At this point, we treat each term
\mathfrak{D}_{k}(z),
separately.

\bullet
Collecting statement (ii) of Proposition
3.1
with easily verified relations:

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big|<1,\quad\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{3}{j(j+3)}\Big|\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big|^{j}<\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{C}{j^{2}}=C,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\ln\Big(1-\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z+C^{\star}}\Big)\Big|<C,

(A.5)

with the positive constant
C
being independent of
n
and
z
, we end up with

|\mathfrak{D}_{1}(z)|\leq C|z|+O(1).

\bullet
Performing simple technical calculations, we conclude

\mathfrak{D}_{2}(z)=-\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}^{+\infty}\frac{C^{\star}\mathfrak{b}(n)z^{3}[(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{2}+(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)+(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{2}]}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{3}(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{3}}

\times\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\frac{3}{j(j+3)}\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{j}+\ln\Big(1-\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)\Big].

After that, inequalities (
Appendix: Proof of Proposition
) and statement (ii) in Proposition
3.1
arrive at the inequality

|\mathfrak{D}_{2}(z)|\leq C|z|+O(1).

\bullet
Since
\Big|\frac{C^{\star}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big|<<1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{j}}-\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{j}}\Big[1-\Big(1+\frac{C^{\star}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big)^{j}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{j}}\Big[\frac{C^{\star}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}+(j-1)O\Big(\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{2}}\Big)\Big]

and, accordingly,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{z^{j}}{j(j+3)}\Big[\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{j}}-\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{j}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{z^{j}}{j(j+3)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{j}}\Big[\frac{C^{\star}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}+(j-1)O\Big(\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{2}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{\star}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{z^{j}}{j(j+3)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{j}}\Big[1-O\Big(\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{z^{j}}{(j+3)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{j}}O\Big(\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{\star}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{z^{j}}{j(j+3)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{j}}\Big[1-O\Big(\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{2}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle-O\Big(\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{2}}\Big)\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\Big[\frac{3z^{j}}{j(j+3)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z)^{j}}-\ln\Big(1-\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)\Big].

Finally, coming to
\mathfrak{D}_{3}(z)
and taking into account (
Appendix: Proof of Proposition
), assumptions in Proposition
3.1
and aforementioned relations, we deduce

|\mathfrak{D}_{3}(z)|\leq C|z|+O(1).

\bullet
In order to manage
\mathfrak{D}_{4}(z)
, we take advantage of the identities

\ln\frac{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)(\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star})}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z+C^{\star})\mathfrak{b}(n)}=\ln\frac{\bigg(1+\frac{C^{\star}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)}\bigg)}{\bigg(1+\frac{C^{\star}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\bigg)}=\frac{C^{\star}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)}+O\Big(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{b}^{2}(n)}\Big)-\frac{C^{\star}}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}+O\Big(\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{2}}\Big)

which in turn provide

\mathfrak{D}_{4}(z)=\sum_{n=\mathfrak{N}\,+1}C^{\star}\Big[\mathfrak{b}(n)\{O((\mathfrak{b}(n)+z)^{-2})+O(\mathfrak{b}^{-2}(n))\}-\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+z}\Big]\Big(\frac{z}{\mathfrak{b}(n)+C^{\star}+z}\Big)^{3}.

At last, applying statements
(i)
and
(ii)
of Proposition
3.1
to the right-hand side of the last equality, we obtain the desired bound

|\mathfrak{D}_{4}(z)|\leq C|z|+O(1).

Collecting representations of all
\mathfrak{D}_{k}
,
k=1,2,3,4,
we arrive at the inequality in (iv) of this proposition. Thus, the proof is completed. ∎
